## intake-1977#record — LittleBit: Ultra Low-Bit Quantization via Latent Factorization

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13771
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: quantization, hardware_optimization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: monitor

### Claims (as verified)

0. Each linear layer is replaced by two parallel low-rank sign-factor paths (primary + residual): W~diag(h)U_sign diag(l)V_sign^T diag(g), U,V in {-1,+1}, with FP16 row/column/latent scales; rank r is chosen to hit a target BPW (0.1 to 1.0), and BPW counts only transformer-block linears (embeddings and lm_head stay FP16).
   - *narrowed*: two parallel paths; BPW excludes embeddings/lm_head
   - anchor `§3.1 Eq.1-2` @ arXiv:2506.13771v5 (html), sha256 8c4b6147ecbd38227d963f8e7b84a84991612fe0e92a3a59e76ce01bec642c82; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "The resulting factors 𝐔 and 𝐕 are then binarized:"
1. Multi-scale compensation: learned FP16 scales across row (h), column (g) and latent (l) dimensions.
   - *unaffected*: confirmed
   - anchor `§3.1 Eq.3-4` @ arXiv:2506.13771v5 (html), sha256 8c4b6147ecbd38227d963f8e7b84a84991612fe0e92a3a59e76ce01bec642c82; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "LittleBit incorporates learnable FP16 scales."
2. Training is QAT with knowledge distillation from the FP16 teacher (Dual-SVID SVD-based init, residual path, SmoothSign gradient; WikiText-2+C4, 2048 seq, 5 epochs) - not post-training; GPU budget not stated in the paper.
   - *narrowed*: explicitly QAT+KD, not just 'initialisation for QAT'; no GPU-hour disclosure
   - anchor `§4.1 Training Details` @ arXiv:2506.13771v5 (html), sha256 8c4b6147ecbd38227d963f8e7b84a84991612fe0e92a3a59e76ce01bec642c82; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "We optimized the LittleBit model parameters, initialized via the Dual-SVID method (Section 3.2), using QAT with knowledge distillation (KD)"
3. Llama2-7B WikiText-2 PPL at 0.1 BPW (15.92) is below STBLLM at 0.7 BPW (19.17) - the only sub-1-bit baseline - but remains ~2.9x FP16 (5.47); at 1.0 BPW it trails QAT 1-bit baselines. Memory: Llama2-13B 0.84 GB (31x) incl. FP16 embeddings/lm_head.
   - *narrowed*: one baseline (STBLLM PTQ); quality still ~2.9x FP16 ppl; 'leading methods' plural not supported
   - anchor `§4.2 Superior Perplexity` @ arXiv:2506.13771v5 (html), sha256 8c4b6147ecbd38227d963f8e7b84a84991612fe0e92a3a59e76ce01bec642c82; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "For instance, on Llama2-7B, LittleBit at 0.55 BPW achieves a PPL of 10.47, which represents a substantial improvement over the 30.67 score reported for STBLLM."
4. The 11.6x speedup is one Llama2-70B MLP-layer GEMV on an A100 with a custom CUDA kernel vs FP16 (v1 claimed 5x); no end-to-end throughput in the main text and no kernel in the released code (CC BY-NC 4.0).
   - *narrowed*: single-layer kernel microbenchmark on A100; kernel unreleased; v1 said 5x
   - anchor `§5 Latency Considerations` @ arXiv:2506.13771v5 (html), sha256 8c4b6147ecbd38227d963f8e7b84a84991612fe0e92a3a59e76ce01bec642c82; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "a custom 1-bit GEMV CUDA kernel was developed to accelerate the primary computational path."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: read v5 HTML in full text (Eq.1-5, §4.1, Tables 1-3, §5, App. D) and cloned the official repo. Established QAT+KD dependency, two-path format and BPW accounting excluding embeddings, kernel-level A100 single-layer basis of 11.6x (v1 5x), absence of kernel in repo, and a LittleBit-2 (ICML 2026, arXiv 2603.00042) successor listed in the repo README.

### Verdict

LittleBit is a QAT+distillation recipe with a non-GGUF layer structure (two binary matmuls plus FP16 scale vectors, and in attention a latent-rank KV path), evaluated by perplexity/zero-shot on Llama/OPT/Phi-4/QwQ. It cannot be applied to our existing GGUF models without retraining each, is not expressible as a block-quant ggml type, ships no inference kernel. Carry: it is a reference point for how far sub-1-bit can go under QAT. Do NOT carry: 11.6x as a deployable speedup, 0.1 BPW as whole-model size, or 'post-training'. Not a path to IQ1/IQ2 CPU perf on DS41/Q38FN, which are PTQ lanes.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/tq3-quantization-evaluation.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Read full body: ppl/accuracy tables, speedup measurement hardware, QAT token budget; feed into handoffs/active/tq3-quantization-evaluation.md as a low-bit reference point.

## intake-1979#record — Magnitude (local inference engine with per-hardware kernel tuning)

- **URL**: https://github.com/magnitudedev/magnitude
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: inference_serving, local_inference, hardware_optimization, speculative_decoding, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Magnitude tunes per (model, device) at install time inside a hard ~1-minute deadline: enumerated schedule families (tile rows 8/16/32/64, operand-preparation schedule, output columns, thread/SIMD ownership, recurrent chunk) are timed against the canonical default in randomized matched rounds, a challenger is promoted only on an anytime-valid paired sign test (error rate alpha, 7 all-ahead rounds at 0.05), numerically admitted against the default at every timed point, and the winner is cached per exact identity (kernel, workload, numerics, validation, compiler, target, physical device).
   - *narrowed*: 'Auto-tunes kernels' = bounded schedule selection among hand-authored families, with rigorous stopping and cache; not kernel synthesis.
   - anchor `docs/faq.mdx:L12 @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "it spends up to a minute on your actual chip trying different ways to split up the math (how many GPU threads team up, how big a chunk each one takes, how far ahead to load data) and keeps the fastest combination that doesn't change the model's output"
   - anchor `inference-v3/src/ops/kernels/schedules.py:L79 (affine tile family; also matrix.py dense tile family) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "for rows in sorted({8, 16, 32, 64, bm})"
   - anchor `inference/seismic/runtime/src/native/sampling.rs:L23 (matched-round paired comparison, measured session-to-session drift) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "within one session a point's samples"
   - anchor `inference/seismic/runtime/src/native/tune.rs:L1 (numerical admission gates every served challenger) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "Native tuning serves no candidate its unit's numerical requirement has"
   - anchor `design/inference/seismic-compilation.md:L234 (per-entry cache) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "Every concluded choice, including fallback, is cache-only on later loads."
   - anchor `inference-v4/README.md:L27-L28 (no performance claim for the Seismic path) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "the estimate model behind selection is unqualified and no performance"
1. Per-model drafters are catalog pairings of existing upstream methods (DFlash, DFlash2, MTP, DSpark GGUFs, several mirrored from z-lab), with the catalog declaring method+file per model; no Magnitude-specific drafting algorithm was found in the source. The Qwen3.8-27B entry is the same DFlash2 drafter class as our champion.

2. The 2x-over-Ollama headline is vendor-run, averaged over 11 models at 4-bit/64k with speculative decoding on both sides but with different drafters and different Ollama backends per machine; the repo ships the benchmark harness and svg but not per-model results, and the Seismic engine's own README says no performance claim is made. Nothing here is a comparison against tuned llama.cpp.
   - *narrowed*: Unequal drafting and per-machine baselines confirmed from harness config; independent replication absent.
   - anchor `assets/benchmarks/ollama-light.svg:L27 @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "Average of 11 popular local models, 4-bit, 64k context, with speculative decoding."
   - anchor `inference/evaluation/bench/src/magnitude_bench/options.py:L98 @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "Ollama drafts with its own draft head or draft model, never the catalog drafter."
3. CPU coverage in the Seismic path is scalar (Cranelift) code only, and the shipped backends are Metal/CUDA/Vulkan: nothing is tuned for AVX-512 CPU kernels or HIP/gfx90a. The portable content is the search/measurement/validation protocol, not any kernel.
   - *narrowed*: (wave 2 pass 2) W1 corrected_key_claims[3] ('CPU coverage scalar (Cranelift) only; nothing tuned for AVX-512 CPU kernels') is OVERTURNED for the current inference/ tree: runtime-detected x86v3/x86v4/x86v4vnni tiers with VNNI dot and IQ2/IQ3 grid decode exist (see pass-2 claim 1). The Cranelift/scalar sentence survives only in the older inference-v4 README. 'No HIP backend' stays true but is incomplete: a Vulkan backend exists (pass-2 claims 0,2).
4. The Seismic (current, Rust) engine's Vulkan backend is NOT TileLang/SPIR-V-from-Python: kernels are hand-authored GLSL 4.60 compute (77 .comp files plus lib/*.glsl under inference/engine/kernels/kernels/vulkan, 76 `native ... for vulkan` entry declarations), formed with a statically linked glslang to sealed, SPIRV-Tools-validated SPIR-V and run through ash on one queue. Each entry is declared in a .seismic file as an ordered tunable candidate list (`params X in [default, ...]`, first = default) with a `where` feasibility predicate and `launch` geometry/shared_bytes formulas; the tuner (tune.rs/search.rs) times those candidates at install. The same declaration form exists for CPU (76), CUDA (80) and Metal (84) entries.
   - anchor `inference/seismic/backends/vulkan/src/lib.rs:L2 (Vulkan backend: authored GLSL -> glslang SPIR-V, no TileLang) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "//! (`specs/26-09-24/vulkan-native-backend.md`). Authored GLSL compute"
   - anchor `inference/engine/kernels/kernels/dense_rows.seismic:L464 (per-entry ordered tunable candidate list for the Vulkan GEMV/GEMM) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "params (SIMDGROUPS in [16, 8, 4, 2, 32], ROWS in [1, 2, 4], arithmetic LANES in [16, 32],"
5. Quant coverage in the Vulkan kernels (and the CPU native library) is wide and includes our low-bit priority: Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K, Q8 (g32s), Q4/Q5 g32 variants, IQ4 (g32/NL), MXFP4, NVFP4, BF16/F16/F32 and the grid-coded IQ2_XS, IQ2_XXS and IQ3_XXS (no IQ1, no TQ). Weights are repacked to a planar 'rows16' resident layout (code plane, scale plane, super-factor plane; one 16-byte load per 32-element packet) and dotted in factored form scale*dot(code,x)+bias*sum(x). The CPU side (inference/seismic/native-cpu) has runtime-detected tiers x86v2/v3/v4/v4vnni/neon with an AVX-512-VNNI int8 dot and a CPU rows16/rows8 IQ2_XS/IQ3_XXS/IQ2_XXS grid decode. This CORRECTS wave-1 claim 3: the 'CPU is scalar-only' statement came from inference-v4/README.md (an older tree); the current inference/ tree has vectorized AVX2/AVX-512/VNNI CPU tiers. No performance number for either is published.
   - anchor `inference/seismic/lang/native-library/vulkan/packets.glsl:L49-L55 (IQ grid-coded kinds) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "#define PACKETS_IQ2XXS 16"
   - anchor `inference/seismic/native-cpu/src/weights.rs:L1271 (CPU IQ grid reps, rows16 resident layout) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "/// A vector-coded representation (the GGUF IQ grids: registry `iq2xs`,"
6. Vendor-neutrality and MI210: nothing in the Vulkan path is AMD-specific; it is a Vulkan 1.3 floor (bufferDeviceAddress, Vulkan memory model, subgroup size 32 required or fixed 32/64, 8/16-bit storage, workgroup_memory_explicit_layout, RTE fp32) with optional tiers (cooperative matrix, fp16, shader_fma). The compatibility doc lists AMD GCN4+ (incl. Vega) on RADV as supported but also states the wave-64 'AMD GCN' Vulkan path has been 'validated offline but not yet run on those devices'. The cooperative-matrix (WMMA) GEMM path is enabled only on RDNA3+ (accelerated mixed int8 dot as RDNA3 detector); gfx90a/CDNA2 is not named anywhere in the current inference/ (Seismic) tree, so on an MI210 (inference, not source text) the GEMM class would fall back to the F32 FMA 4x8 register tile and MFMA is unreachable through this path. Whether an MI210 on RADV passes the floor was NOT tested (no run allowed).
   - anchor `inference/docs/compatibility.md:L187-L188 @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "- **Unqualified hardware:** the Vulkan paths for 64-wide hardware (AMD GCN), for devices without"
   - anchor `inference/seismic/backends/vulkan/src/facts.rs:L643 (cooperative-matrix path gated off RDNA2; AMD requires accelerated mixed int8 dot) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "// RDNA2 has no WMMA units whatever its driver reports; accelerated"
7. The TileLang fork (magnitudedev/tilelang, branch magnitude @26ef76e, VERSION 0.1.14) belongs to the FROZEN v3 engine (old-inference/ and inference-v3/), not the current Seismic engine. Measured delta against the merge-base 030556de with upstream tile-ai/tilelang main: 45 commits, 124 files, +5696/-935, overwhelmingly Metal (adapter/kernel cache, simdgroup fragments/GEMM, fast-math lowering, native submissions) plus profiler timing, cache-key identity and a few cross-backend fixes (runtime-valid GEMM rows, HIP/Metal FP literal precision). Upstream TileLang's own README lists AMD ROCm/HIP as 'Supported' (Linux, CDNA and RDNA incl. gfx942/gfx950 paths, CI on gfx942 only); the tree has tilelang/rocm with MFMA and WMMA macro generators and wave-64 handling for gfx9*, and gfx90a IS named in the fork's HIP support (Dockerfile.rocm PYTORCH_ROCM_ARCH includes gfx90a; docs/get_started/targets.md gives a hip target example with mcpu gfx90a; src/tl_templates/hip/common.h guards on __gfx90a__) - corrected by main after the second reader (the dive had stated gfx90a/gfx908 were not named). TileLang has no Vulkan target in this tree (cuda, rocm, metal, webgpu, cpu). Magnitude's v3 AGENTS.md forbids per-backend kernels, so v3 was a Metal-first TileLang schedule set, and is superseded.
   - anchor `inference-v3/README.md:L18 @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "forks that carry our changes ahead of upstream. `pyproject.toml` installs"
   - anchor `tilelang/README.md:L122 (upstream-derived ROCm row; gfx942 CI only) @ 26ef76e` @ magnitudedev/tilelang@26ef76e1cc9101e02dd4e591e21113fce917c2ea: "CI runs on a self-hosted gfx942 (MI300X) runner; gfx950 is not yet covered."
   - anchor `inference-v3/tilelang/docs/get_started/targets.md:L18 @ 26ef76e` @ magnitudedev/tilelang@26ef76e1cc9101e02dd4e591e21113fce917c2ea: "| `hip` | AMD GPUs via ROCm. Use a config dict for options such as `{"kind": "hip", "mcpu": "gfx90a"}`. |"
8. Stopping statistic (statistics.rs): per round d_r (challenger minus incumbent cost, in-round ratio); a round is 'ahead' if d<0, 'behind' if d>0, ties neither. Tally::verdict returns Leads when the uniform-prior mixture likelihood ratio M_n(ahead)=sum_{j<=s} C(n+1,j)/((n+1)C(n,s)) times alpha >= 1, Trails when the same on 'behind'. It is a nonnegative supermartingale under 'P(ahead)<=1/2' (so distribution-free; ties count as rounds but not successes, which is conservative), and by Ville's inequality holds over every look and any optional-stopping/continuation rule, across sessions. No effect-size margin is asked of a lead. Fewest all-ahead rounds to Lead: 5/7/9 at alpha 0.1/0.05/0.01 (my recomputation matches the source's 7 and 9). Block-of-rounds shuffling of sample order per round controls clock-state drift.
   - anchor `inference/seismic/measure/src/statistics.rs:L124-L128 (Tally::verdict) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "if wealth(self.rounds, self.ahead) * rate >= 1. {"
   - anchor `inference/seismic/measure/src/statistics.rs:L44-L45 (anytime-valid guarantee) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "//! it holds over every look at a comparison together, however many rounds"
9. Early-reject is a BUDGET rule, not a verdict: a comparison session is admitted exactly Tally::fewest(alpha) rounds and ends at its first round that is not ahead (tune.rs:L2123-L2128), so most (slower) challengers cost one round; the challenger is formally dropped (ContenderStanding::Trailing) only if its behind-mixture reaches 1/alpha, and otherwise stays measured and may be continued in later sessions without invalidating the guarantee. Wave-1 W1-1979-01 described this as 'slower challengers rejected after 1 round'; that overstated it.
   - anchor `inference/seismic/runtime/src/native/tune.rs:L2123 (session-end rule, not a verdict) @ 940e51b` @ magnitudedev/magnitude@940e51bd3c16b4b98935d04e855fe8685031ae90: "// Its part ends when it is resolved, and with its first round"
10. Versus AK: AK already has an anytime-valid sign test-martingale (evaluator/statistics.py, construction sign_martingale_predictable_lambda/v1: X=+1/-1 on oriented block effect vs a contribution-floor boundary, ties charged to the null, predictable lambda=mean/second-moment capped at 0.5, init 0.1; wealth threshold 1/alpha_sel for selection and 1/alpha_conf for confirmation, calibration solve accepted alpha_sel=0.1, alpha_conf=0.05, B_min=12, A/A false-crossing 1.4%). The two tests are the same family (sign null P<=1/2, Ville). In all-ahead runs Magnitude's mixture crosses sooner (5/7/9 vs AK 7/9/13 blocks at 0.1/0.05/0.01 by my recomputation), but in a pure-python Monte Carlo (1500 runs, alpha 0.05, 80 rounds, pass2/power_sim.out) crossing rates and median crossing rounds are nearly identical for P(ahead) 0.7-0.9 (mixture 0.83/32, 1.0/17 vs AK 0.81/33, 1.0/17 at 0.7/0.8) and both stay under alpha under the null (2.7% vs 1.9%). Differences that remain are policy, not statistics: Magnitude asks no margin and runs a symmetric trail/futility verdict plus fewest-rounds admission; AK binds a contribution floor and B_min from A/A calibration. Successive halving (research-loop §9.3 item 4) is AK's allocation across candidates; Magnitude's analogue is per-unit exhaustive/budgeted search with demand-weighted objective, not halving.
   - anchor `evaluator/statistics.py:L793 (AK sign test-martingale, Ville) @ f1c6fef56` @ epyc-inference-research@f1c6fef56c54d1c9532e964658c8eebedce5b8b7: "`P(sup_b W_b >= 1/alpha) <= alpha` at ANY stopping time, which is what"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: tuned dimensions are small enumerated schedule families, not open kernel search; the stopping rule is an anytime-valid sign test; drafters are catalog pairings (DFlash2 for Qwen3.8-27B, mirrored from z-lab); the 2x chart pits different drafters per arm; Seismic path claims no performance and has scalar-only CPU. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 pass 2: (1) premise 'no HIP backend therefore nothing to port' replaced by: a Vulkan backend exists (hand-authored GLSL, vendor-neutral, wave-64 path unrun, MFMA unreachable on gfx90a); (2) TileLang fork identified as v3/Metal-first, 45 commits/124 files/+5696-935 vs upstream merge-base; upstream TileLang supports HIP incl. MFMA but names gfx942/gfx950 only; (3) wave-1 'CPU scalar-only' OVERTURNED for inference/ (x86v4vnni tiers, IQ2/IQ3 grid CPU decode); (4) exact stopping statistic read and compared with AK: same Ville sign-test family, equal power in simulation, so W1-1979-01's premise that AK lacks an anytime-valid test was wrong (AK has one); early-reject is a session budget rule, not a verdict. Main correction 2026-10-20 after second reader: claim 3's 'gfx90a/gfx908 not named in any file' was FALSE for the TileLang fork (9 files name gfx90a, e.g. targets.md L18, hip/common.h L128, Dockerfile.rocm L20); rewritten. 5/7/9 vs 7/9/13 rounds-to-Lead and the power Monte Carlo are agent-computed (pass2/), not source text. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): arXiv 2504.17577 TileLang paper - Already in the index; cross-referenced instead. [wave3 declined].

### Verdict

Magnitude is NOT TileLang-on-Vulkan: TileLang/v3 is the frozen Metal-first engine; the live Seismic engine ships hand-written GLSL (Vulkan), Rust (CPU, AVX2/AVX-512/VNNI tiers), CUDA and Metal sources behind one declarative tunable-entry format. The MI210 (gfx90a) is not a stated target: Vulkan wave-64 GCN is 'validated offline, not run', and WMMA is RDNA3+ only so MFMA is unreachable. Do NOT port these kernels or cite them as validated on AMD. DO treat the repo as free, readable prior art for (a) IQ2/IQ3 planar decode and VNNI dot layouts and (b) the candidate-list+constraint schema. AK's statistic is already the same family as Magnitude's; do not propose swapping it (power equal in simulation). The earlier 'CPU is scalar-only' conclusion is wrong for inference/ (right only for inference-v4).

## intake-1980#record — RSI-Anything (recursive self-improvement with world-model prediction feedback)

- **URL**: https://github.com/Gen-Verse/RSI-Anything
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only

### Claims (as verified)

0. RSI-Anything's pre-screening world model in the only domain with a controlled baseline (code repair) is a task-specific trained 3B classifier ranking 4 candidate patches; it does not reduce the number of expensive evaluations (online test calls ~3.7/task in every arm) but spends the same budget better.
   - *narrowed*: Predictor is trained, task-specific; selection ranks among 4 proposals, saves no evaluations.
   - anchor `README.md @ 8ba44cd (docs/benchmarks.md:L24)` @ Gen-Verse/RSI-Anything@8ba44cd73ec6f57b151ec06e2f71a0726adfb687: "RSI-Anything exceeds random selection and the capacity-matched static verifier under the shared coder and candidate budget."
1. Repo ships runnable pipelines for code repair and GUI only (configs, training stages, verify scripts); other domains are reported with HF resources.
   - *unaffected*: Confirmed from file tree.
2. README numbers are copied from the paper; baselines are random-K and a capacity-matched static verifier, with no ablation isolating the JEPA design.
   - *narrowed*: Ablations/baselines are thin; see intake-2000#record.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: predictor identified (trained, not prompted); evaluation-count savings are zero by design; ablation set is thin.

### Verdict

Treat the repo as the implementation of intake-2000#record's code-repair arm only. Do NOT carry 'surrogate pre-screening saves expensive evaluations' from it: the code-repair arm holds evaluation count constant (3.71 vs 3.72 calls) and improves outcome by choosing which of 4 candidates to run, using a domain-specific trained 3B predictor. For AutoKernel the relevant lesson is the evidence-tier/reliability-gate design, not the predictor.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Read arXiv 2610.13912 ablations for predictor-on/off; if solid, draft an AK trial-ranking surrogate experiment under handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md.

## intake-1981#record — sgrep (local semantic plus lexical code search CLI)

- **URL**: https://github.com/context-dot-dev/sgrep
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: search_retrieval, tool_implementation, rag_alternatives, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Embedder is a Model2Vec static-embedding lookup (minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2 and potion-base-8M, pinned revisions, 256-d f16 tables): token vectors are averaged and normalized, with no transformer pass at search time. The default path indexes the tree itself (ripgrep only lists files); the 'rerank ripgrep candidates' behaviour is the separate --stdin pipe mode, which does not use the index.
   - *narrowed*: Static-embedding claim confirmed; 'reranks ripgrep candidates' describes only --stdin mode. Default mode owns its index.
   - anchor `README.md:L81 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "sgrep looks up those vectors, averages them, and normalizes the result to 256 numbers."
1. Fusion is 0.25*BM25 + 0.75*semantic after per-list 0-1 scaling over the union of top-200 lexical and top-200 semantic chunks, with exact case-sensitive identifier matches ranked first (README rule).
   - *unaffected*: Weights/top-200 verified in source.
   - anchor `src/main.rs:L534 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "let score = |i: usize| 0.25 * bm25_relative[i] + 0.75 * semantic_relative[i];"
2. Chunking: Chonkie + Tree-sitter for Python, C/C++, C#, Go, Java, Rust, JS/JSX, TS/TSX (other text split generically), 2,048-character target keeping whole lines; each embedding covers file path plus passage text; content-checked on-disk index cache (256 MiB / 128 entries default) holds copies of source passages; --json --explain emits lexical/semantic/combined scores.
   - *unaffected*: Verified in README and splitter.rs.
   - anchor `README.md:L159 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "Passages target 2,048 characters and keep whole lines."
3. SWE-bench Verified (50 tasks, 12 repos, 2 repeats, one agent policy GPT-6 Luna on 4-core CPU sandboxes): piped sgrep mean LLM API cost $0.00475 vs ripgrep $0.00604 (-21.3%, paired CI excludes 0) with 70/100 vs 68/100 solved (+2pp, CI -3..+8, supports only a 5pp non-inferiority margin). The compute-inclusive total estimate is -0.4% (no saving), mean agent seconds are +8.5%, and the vendor's own ledger shows the ripgrep arm hit tool-output truncation on 88 of 261 searches versus 4 of 268 for piped sgrep (token-volume, not retrieval-quality, mechanism not isolated).
   - *narrowed*: Cost saving is API-only, one model/agent policy, tiny absolute dollars; compute-inclusive total -0.4%, wall time +8.5%; +2pp accuracy is not evidence of improvement (CI -3..+8).
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md:L7 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "Piped sgrep reduced mean LLM API cost by **21.3%** versus ripgrep, solving **70/100 versus 68/100** attempts."
4. Identity: context-dot-dev/sgrep IS the same code lineage as mrmps/sgrep (Cargo.toml repository = github.com/mrmps/sgrep; LICENSE copyright mrmps; benchmarks README links the context-dot-dev repo; mrmps/sgrep/releases carries the 12 MB / 10%-sample / literal-OR caveat text). The caveat belongs to the older RepoQA/CoSQA release notes; the current full-suite RepoQA uses all 60 snapshots and CoSQA all 500 test questions, but its ripgrep baseline is STILL a fixed literal-OR query (hit@10 = 1.0% on CoSQA, 19.2% on RepoQA), so those retrieval tables say nothing about ripgrep driven by an agent.
   - anchor `Cargo.toml:L7 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "repository = "https://github.com/mrmps/sgrep""
5. Offline/license: first search downloads the model (32-34 MB) and Tree-sitter parsers from HF/network, later searches can run offline (HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1); no hosted API or model server is used at search time. License MIT (Cargo.toml, LICENSE, with Chonkie and Kreuzberg notices); the two embedding models are stated MIT in the README (model cards NOT read). Rust 1.90+, macOS Apple Silicon binary only in releases; Linux needs cargo build.
   - anchor `README.md:L61 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "The first search downloads a model (32–34 MB) and any required code parsers. Later searches can run offline."
6. Retrieval benchmark (vendor, self-run, both datasets used during development): RepoQA complete-function Full@8k sgrep 89.8% vs CK-semantic 79.5% (paired +10.3pp, CI +6.0..+14.8); CoSQA Hit@10 sgrep 67.6% vs CK-semantic 79.6% (-12.0pp, CI -16.4..-7.6); median CLI latency 53 ms (RepoQA) / 239 ms (CoSQA) on a shared M5 Max.
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md:L172 @ dec6122` @ context-dot-dev/sgrep@dec6122c05be553c5a60bd647d1350b221b7b9c3: "Ripgrep used a fixed literal-OR query, not patterns chosen by a person or agent."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: confirmed identity with mrmps/sgrep (Cargo.toml/LICENSE); settled design (Model2Vec static embeddings, 0.75/0.25 fusion, top-200+200, 2,048-char Tree-sitter chunks, persistent index, online first-run model download); narrowed the 'reranks ripgrep' wording to --stdin mode; narrowed the SWE-bench result (API-only saving, compute-inclusive -0.4%, wall time +8.5%, +2pp within noise, rg arm agent-chosen); confirmed the literal-OR baseline caveat applies to the RepoQA/CoSQA tables, not SWE-bench. Binary built/run: none.

### Verdict

sgrep is a small (1.8k LoC Rust) MIT hybrid code searcher: Model2Vec static embeddings (no neural forward), BM25 25 / semantic 75 fusion, Tree-sitter 2,048-char chunks. It is the same project as mrmps/sgrep. Its SWE-bench headline is a real but narrow API-spend saving on one metered model with NO compute-inclusive saving and a +2pp accuracy tie; its RepoQA/CoSQA tables use a literal-OR ripgrep that is a strawman for agent grep. Carry forward: the static-embedding + BM25 recipe as a cheap, forward-pass-free candidate arm (relevant to the REPL embedder-pool decode-contention problem). Do NOT carry forward: any claim that it beats ripgrep for agents, that it fills a gap kb-search/GitNexus leave, or that the 21.3% transfers to local inference (our inference cost is not per-token API spend).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Run sgrep as a baseline arm in the REPL embedding retrieval evaluation under handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md (CPU-only, region-lock).

## intake-1982#record — PersonaPlex: real-time full-duplex speech-to-speech conversational model

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA/personaplex
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: monitor

### Claims (as verified)

0. PersonaPlex-7B-v1 is a full-duplex speech-to-speech fine-tune of Kyutai Moshi (Moshiko weights; Helium LLM backbone), not a new architecture; the HF safetensors file holds 8.37B BF16 params although the card labels it 7B.
   - *narrowed*: Add: card parameter label '7B' vs 8.37B counted BF16 params; base checkpoint is kyutai/moshiko-pytorch-bf16 (CC-BY-4.0 'additional information' on the gate).
   - anchor `NVIDIA/personaplex README.md:L8 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: "PersonaPlex is based on the [Moshi](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00037) architecture and weights."
1. Persona control = text role prompt + audio voice prompt; the repo ships 18 fixed voice embeddings and the paper describes zero-shot cloning from a speech sample.
   - *unaffected*: Fixed voice set in repo; arbitrary cloning is paper-level.
   - anchor `NVIDIA/personaplex README.md:L97 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: "PersonaPlex supports a wide range of voices; we pre-package embeddings for voices that sound more natural and conversational (NAT) and others that are more varied (VAR)."
2. The README gives no results; the HF model card gives FullDuplexBench numbers for the released checkpoint (smooth-turn latency 0.170, user-interruption latency 0.240, SSIM 0.650) measured on one NVIDIA A100 80 GB with PyTorch.
   - *narrowed*: 'No numeric results' holds for the README only; model card has numbers (see corrected claim 2).
   - anchor `HF model card, Inference section` @ HF nvidia/personaplex-7b-v1@fdaf4090a61cb315c138a1faee287ffd6c716309 (rendered blob page; raw README is gated): "Test Hardware: NVIDIA A100 80 GB"
3. Released-checkpoint training data = synthetic customer-service/assistant dialogs (Qwen-3-32B, GPT-OSS-120B text; Chatterbox TTS audio) + 7,303 Fisher English conversations (LDC corpus).
   - *narrowed*: Fisher only in released checkpoint; synthetic generators named; Fisher is an LDC corpus (not open data) so the weights carry LDC-derived training exposure.
   - anchor `NVIDIA/personaplex README.md:L107 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: "The model is trained on synthetic conversations for a fixed assistant role and varying customer service roles."
4. SERVING PATH AND PORTABLE MECHANISM: the shipped stack is plain PyTorch (torch>=2.2,<2.5) using the torch.cuda API (default device 'cuda'; CUDA-graph replay through torch.cuda.CUDAGraph, disabled by env NO_CUDA_GRAPH) in a CUDA 12.4 Docker image; no ROCm or llama.cpp path is documented by NVIDIA. torch.cuda calls map to HIP on a ROCm PyTorch build, so a gfx90a run is plausible but untested here; the README documents --cpu-offload (accelerate) and says offline evaluation can run with CPU-only PyTorch. Size/mechanism for a port: Moshi temporal transformer 32 layers, dim 4096, text vocab 32000, 12.5 Hz frame rate, 16 audio streams in/out (n_q 16); depth transformer 6 layers, dim 1024, 8 codebooks (dep_q 8) per frame; Mimi codec at 24 kHz. Derived estimate (not measured): 8.37B BF16 params = 16.7 GB read per 80 ms frame ~ 209 GB/s if the weights are streamed once per frame; whether the EPYC CPU sustains 12.5 such frames/s plus the 8-step depth transformer is unmeasured, MI210 HBM bandwidth (spec, general knowledge) is a large multiple of it, and quantization would cut it.
   - anchor `moshi/moshi/models/loaders.py:L97 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: ""dep_q": 8,"
   - anchor `moshi/moshi/models/loaders.py:L112 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: ""depformer_num_layers": 6,"
   - anchor `moshi/moshi/utils/compile.py:L285 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: "self._graph = cuda.CUDAGraph()"
   - anchor `NVIDIA/personaplex README.md:L70 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: "Or install cpu-only PyTorch for offline evaluation on pure CPU."
   - anchor `moshi/pyproject.toml:L13 @ 3428dfd` @ NVIDIA/personaplex@3428dfd95309a7f3c84fd93259ded0f810d1ff91: ""torch >= 2.2.0, < 2.5","
   - anchor `s5 Conclusion` @ arXiv:2602.06053v1 (html): "Future work will explore post-training alignment and integration with external tools."
5. SCOPE/FIT: English-only (card: 'PersonaPlex can generate English speech response for English speech input'; HF tag 'en'), which fails conversation-stack D2 (English mandatory, Italian strongly wanted) at bake-off gate G1; the card lists only NVIDIA Ampere (A100) / Hopper (H100) as supported and tested on one A100 80 GB; NVIDIA states no VRAM figure; no tool-call output is documented (paper conclusion lists tool integration as future work), so conversation-stack D5/G3 (tool-call slot quality) is untested for it.
   - anchor `HF model card, Use Case` @ HF nvidia/personaplex-7b-v1@fdaf4090a61cb315c138a1faee287ffd6c716309 (rendered blob page; raw README is gated): "PersonaPlex can generate English speech response for English speech input."
   - anchor `HF model card, Software Integration` @ HF nvidia/personaplex-7b-v1@fdaf4090a61cb315c138a1faee287ffd6c716309 (rendered blob page; raw README is gated): "Supported Hardware Microarchitecture Compatibility: NVIDIA Ampere (A100) NVIDIA Hopper (H100)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: architecture dims read from loaders.py (temporal 32x4096, depformer 6x1024, dep_q 8, 12.5 Hz); params corrected to 8.37B from HF safetensors metadata; 'no numeric results' narrowed to README (card has FDB numbers, A100 80 GB test hardware); English-only established from the card (fails D2/G1); torch.cuda/CUDA-graph usage and the pure-CPU offline-eval note recorded as the porting surface; no VRAM figure from NVIDIA; no tool-call output documented.

### Verdict

PersonaPlex-7B-v1 is a Moshi fine-tune: 32-layer dim-4096 temporal transformer at 12.5 Hz plus a 6-layer depth transformer (8 codebooks/frame) and the Mimi codec, 8.37B BF16 params. Its published stack is PyTorch/CUDA-documented (A100/H100 tested), but it uses only torch.cuda APIs and CUDA graphs that PyTorch-ROCm maps to HIP, so a gfx90a reference run is the cheap first probe; a CPU port would mean re-implementing a Moshi-style two-transformer streaming loop plus Mimi in ggml (qwentts.cpp already carries a codec path, not Mimi). It is English-only, so under conversation-stack D2 it cannot be a bake-off winner, but it is the only open full-duplex model with published FDB numbers, which makes it a harness known-answer target and an English-only reference backend for the CS-20 interface. Do NOT carry: 'no numeric results' (card has them), '7B' as an exact count, or any serving claim for ROCm (none is documented or tested).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Adopt FullDuplexBench categories as interlocutor eval rubric; conversation-stack.md

## intake-1983#record — FluidAudio: CoreML catalogue of on-device speech models (ASR/EOU, VAD, diarization, AEC, TTS) with no duplex policy

- **URL**: https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. FluidAudio is a Swift/CoreML CATALOGUE of ~25 converted speech models (batch ASR, streaming ASR, VAD, diarization, AEC/enhancement, TTS) plus thin managers; it ships NO duplex/turn-taking policy: no barge-in, no full-duplex logic anywhere (repo grep for barge/duplex/turn-taking finds only a diarization-sensitivity note).
   - *narrowed*: conversion catalogue; LocalVQE CPU not ANE
   - anchor `Documentation/Models.md "Streaming Transcription" Parakeet EOU row` @ FluidInference/FluidAudio@61d41b3ca04833d1cb80632c82ba401f117a01ef: "Streaming speech-to-text with end-of-utterance detection (120M params). Three chunk-size variants — 160ms / 320ms / 1280ms — for ultra-low-latency to higher-accuracy streaming."
   - anchor `Documentation/Models.md "Evaluated Models (Not Supported)"` @ FluidInference/FluidAudio@61d41b3ca04833d1cb80632c82ba401f117a01ef: "Now 1.1GB but too slow. Needs further testing."
1. Turn-end signal = the Parakeet-Realtime-EOU-120M RNNT head emitting an <EOU> token (id 1024), confirmed by a wall-clock silence debounce (eouDebounceMs default 1280 ms; anchor starts at first EOU emission with no new tokens, any newly decoded word resets). Chunks 160/320 ms; LibriSpeech test-clean WER 8.23%/4.88% (FluidAudio benchmark, M2) vs HF card 8.29%/4.87%.
   - *narrowed*: Kokoro is one of six TTS; large ASR set omitted by Stage-1; Qwen3-TTS listed as unsupported/too slow
   - anchor `Sources/.../EOU/StreamingEouAsrManager.swift:L292-L299 @61d41b3` @ FluidInference/FluidAudio@61d41b3ca04833d1cb80632c82ba401f117a01ef: "`debounceMs` measures wall-clock silence — the elapsed time since the EOU head first"
   - anchor `HF FluidInference/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-coreml README (card)` @ HF FluidInference/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-coreml@40a23f4c0b333aa17ad8c0f2ea47ec2347f2f355: "| `160ms/` | 160ms | 8.29% | 4.78x | | `320ms/` | 320ms | 4.87% | 12.48x |"
2. VAD = Silero v6.2.1 converted 256 ms-hop model with hysteresis segmentation (defaults minSpeech 0.15 s, minSilence 0.75 s, speechPadding 0.1 s, negativeThreshold = base - 0.15).
   - *narrowed*: per-model licenses differ (EOU NVIDIA Open Model License); irrelevant to project
3. Echo/noise: LocalVQE v1.3 (4.8M) / v1.2 (1.3M), fp32, CPU-only, 16 ms algorithmic latency, mic + far-end reference input; FluidAudio states the port is numerically equivalent to the UPSTREAM PYTORCH and GGML (localvqe-v1.3-4.8M-f32.gguf) engines, so portable non-CoreML weights already exist; Beta, upstream AEC benchmark only partially reproduced, live-integration (reference timing) untested.
   - anchor `Documentation/Enhancement/LocalVQE.md "Parity"` @ FluidInference/FluidAudio@61d41b3ca04833d1cb80632c82ba401f117a01ef: "Upstream GGML CLI (`localvqe-v1.3-4.8M-f32.gguf`)"
   - anchor `Documentation/Models.md LocalVQE row` @ FluidInference/FluidAudio@61d41b3ca04833d1cb80632c82ba401f117a01ef: "fp16 rejected (parity 102 → 5 dB); ANE not used."
4. Portable weights outside CoreML: Parakeet EOU/TDT/Nemotron/Sortformer are NVIDIA NeMo models (PyTorch .nemo upstream), Silero is PyTorch/ONNX upstream, Kokoro/Supertonic/StyleTTS2/LS-EEND/pyannote have upstream PyTorch or ONNX releases; FluidAudio HF repos hold CoreML only. Export routes to ONNX/GGML were not verified in this dive.
   - anchor `NVIDIA card nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 (Description)` @ HF nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 (raw README main, sha256 618bce4a..): "It achieves low latency (80ms~160 ms) and signals EOU by emitting an `<EOU>` token at the end of each utterance. The model supports only English and does not output punctuation or capitalization."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: re-scoped to catalogue; EOU is an RNNT <EOU> token + 1280 ms silence debounce (not a standalone detector); no barge-in/duplex logic exists; LocalVQE already has upstream PyTorch+GGML; Stage-1 model list was a small fraction; Qwen3-TTS was tried and rejected as too slow.

### Verdict

Mine FluidAudio as a MODEL MENU and a source of tuned thresholds, not as a turn-taking implementation: it has no barge-in or duplex logic. Cheapest fills for conversation-stack gaps: CS-22 (VAD/turn detection) <- Parakeet-Realtime-EOU-120M (NVIDIA NeMo, PyTorch) + Silero VAD; echo cancellation (deferred D7, browser AEC judged inadequate) <- LocalVQE which already has PyTorch and GGML builds upstream. Hold FluidAudio's WER/RTFx numbers as Apple M2/M5 numbers only. Do not assume ONNX/GGML for the EOU model without verifying.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Note Parakeet EOU, Silero VAD, LocalVQE as turn-taking/echo-cancel component candidates for the cascade baseline; handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md

## intake-1984#record — Fish Audio Drama 3 (multi-speaker MoE TTS)

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/fishaudio/drama-3
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, moe_optimization, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: monitor

### Claims (as verified)

0. Drama 3 (Fish Audio) is a 35.9B-parameter MoE TTS (3B active): Qwen3.5-35B-A3B-derived hybrid backbone + 13-layer codebook-MTP audio decoder over 10 codebooks; experts stored in block-scaled FP8 (32.2B params, 1 byte each), the remaining 3.7B params in BF16, ~39.6 GB total weights.
   - *narrowed*: Exact size 35.9B (not '36B'); ~39.6 GB weights.
   - anchor `README.md:L105 @ b2b5ee9` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd: "- **35B parameters, 3B active per token**: MoE text backbone with hybrid (full + linear) attention"
   - anchor `hfapi.json#/safetensors/parameters` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd (HF API /safetensors/parameters): ""parameters":{"BF16":3687388288,"F8_E4M3":32212254720}"
1. Card-level capabilities: speaker tags 0-15 (up to 16 characters), reference-clip voice cloning with transcript, inline point tags ([sighs], [laughs]), edit-as-continuation via previous_takes, automatic chunking of long text with per-chunk streaming.
   - *unaffected*: 16-speaker limit is card-only.
   - anchor `README.md:L108 @ b2b5ee9` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd: "- **Speaker tags** `<|speaker:0|>` … `<|speaker:15|>` — one text can carry up to 16 characters"
2. Served through SGLang-Omni (sgl-omni serve) and vLLM-Omni (vllm serve --omni); all published performance numbers are on NVIDIA H200 (B300 / RTX PRO 6000 in the report), CUDA kernels, FP8; no ROCm/CPU/llama.cpp path is published.
   - *unaffected*: Add: engines are CUDA-only as published.
   - anchor `README.md:L121 @ b2b5ee9` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd: "sgl-omni serve --model-path fishaudio/drama-3 --port 8000"
3. PORTABLE MECHANISM (report s2-s4, config.json): per frame one backbone step (Qwen3.5-35B-A3B-derived, hidden 2048, 40 layers, 3 DeltaNet : 1 full attention, 8/256 experts + shared) then 13 single-position decoder steps (4 shared + 9 per-codebook full-width layers) and a causal DAC-based codec decode; the report counts ~6.0 GB of weights read per frame (backbone without experts 2.9 GB, 8 active FP8 experts 1.0 GB, decoder 2.1 GB), i.e. ~129 GB/s at the 21.5 frames/s real-time rate, before any further quantization. Experts are block-scaled FP8 (128x128 blocks): MI210 has no FP8 matrix hardware (general knowledge, not from a source), so a gfx90a/CPU path needs dequantization to BF16/FP16 or re-quantization to a ggml K-quant; the ~32.2B expert params are ~64 GB at BF16, too big for one 64 GB MI210, and audio quality after requantization is unvalidated. A llama.cpp-family port needs: the backbone as an LLM graph with 4,096 added semantic tokens and the partial-codebook-fusion embedding (codes 0 and 1 averaged, Eq. 1); a separate 13-layer decoder graph run once per frame with per-codebook sampling; a causal DAC codec decoder (qwentts.cpp carries a different codec); a streaming chunk protocol. Whether frozen v10 already covers the DeltaNet and MoE ops used here is not verified.
   - *reattributed*: Substance is the serving/portability situation (corrected claim 3).
   - anchor `s4.1` @ arXiv:2610.18010v2 (html): "A frame of Drama 3 thus reads about 6.0 GB of weights (Table 1), less than two fifths of what S2 reads, with more than seven times as many parameters."
   - anchor `s4.1` @ arXiv:2610.18010v2 (html): "Second, its routed experts are stored in block-scaled FP8, with one scale per 128×128 block, so each expert parameter takes one byte instead of two."
4. Card H200 streaming numbers (Seed-TTS English prompts; mean of 4 runs; 21.5 codec frames = 1 s of audio): concurrency 1: 166.7 frames/s, TTFA p50 88.9 ms / p95 90.8 ms, RTF 0.131; 16: 927.6 frames/s, TTFA p50 210.1 ms, RTF 0.373; 64: 1,687.3 frames/s, TTFA p50 450.9 ms / p95 1,020.1 ms, RTF 0.801; 256 streams: vocoder on engine GPU 2,422 frames/s, TTFA p50 3,403 ms, vocoder on second GPU 4,160 frames/s, TTFA p50 2,146 ms. Vendor-measured, single hardware class, not comparable with MI210 or EPYC.
   - anchor `README.md Table 1:L204 @ b2b5ee9` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd: "| 1 | 166.7 | 88.9 ms | 90.8 ms | 0.131 |"
   - anchor `README.md Table 2:L221 @ b2b5ee9` @ HF fishaudio/drama-3@b2b5ee998034b2975720906c815e7fb0e9f007bd: "| Second GPU | 256 | 4,160 | 2,146 ms | 2,469 ms |"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: param split computed from HF safetensors metadata (35.9B; 32.2B FP8); card numbers verified row-by-row against README tables 1-2; 16-speaker limit shown to be card-only; per-frame weight traffic and port requirements derived from report s4.1 Table 1; no non-CUDA path found.

### Verdict

Drama 3 is a TTS, not an interlocutor: in the conversation-stack it can only occupy the TTS slot of the cascade (D8 baseline) or serve as a quality ceiling, and its streaming-text-input training stage (report s3.1 Stage 3) is relevant to the D11 latency-hiding ladder (stream orchestrator text into TTS). Mechanism-wise it is a Qwen3.5-35B-A3B-class hybrid MoE backbone plus a 13-layer per-frame decoder and a DAC codec: ~6 GB weights read per 46.5 ms frame (21.5 fps), FP8 experts that MI210 cannot execute natively, no published ROCm/CPU/llama.cpp path. A port is a research-sized build, not a config change. Do NOT compare its H200 TTFA/RTF with our CPU/MI210 qwentts numbers, and do NOT carry the 36B figure (35.9B counted).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-1988#record — The COUNT: Counting N-grams Unlocks Scaling on Host Memory and Training-Free Adaptation

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20456
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, retrieval_augmented_decoding, training_distillation, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. COUNT = frozen host-RAM table mapping each n-gram history (orders 1-6, Dolma2 tokenizer ids) to its top-32 next tokens with 8-bit stupid-backoff log scores; the model input embedding adds the score-weighted, order-averaged sum of the model's OWN unembedding rows (K=8, J=6 in training). The recipe also applies a learned-LayerNorm 'embedding amplification'.
   - *narrowed*: learned-LayerNorm amplification is part of the reported recipe
   - anchor `§2.2 Eq.1 / §3.3` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "We add the COUNT embedding and the token embedding et together as the input to the first transformer block, ht(0)=ft+et."
   - anchor `§2.2` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "We use the LM’s unembedding head as a basis B∈ℝ|𝒱|×d, whose rows we weight by these scores to form the COUNT embedding ft."
1. Gains are measured only for models PRETRAINED with COUNT (60M-1B, D/N 10/20/40, Dolma 3): equivalent to 46-89% more compute on LM loss and 6-56% on task loss by fitted scaling laws (abstract headline: 57%). Small absolute effect at 1B/D-N=20: 0.010 BPB task loss.
   - *unaffected*: Eq.1
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "In our experiments, using COUNT improves LM loss as much as using 46–89% more compute. Meanwhile, task loss with COUNT improves comparably to a 6–56% increase in compute."
   - anchor `§5.1` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "Figure 4 shows absolute changes in individual task results at λ=0.25, the interpolation value that makes loss decrease for all 3 domains."
2. 'Training-free' covers exactly two things: (i) swapping/mixing/shrinking the table of an already COUNT-pretrained model (domain tables ~80 GB; general tables 459/142/68/17 GB all still beat the baseline at 1B); (ii) inference-only LATE FUSION (kNN-LM/infini-gram-style output interpolation, lambda=0.1) on a STANDARD transformer, which the paper reports (App B.1, Fig 9) as weaker than COUNT but nonzero. Nothing shows the COUNT INPUT embedding helping a model not trained with it; changing K/J at inference on a COUNT model already loses most of the gain (App B.4).
   - *narrowed*: 57% (abstract) vs 46-89% (body) not reconciled; scaling-law fits, <=1B
   - anchor `§5 intro / App B.1 (inference-only late fusion on a standard transformer)` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "For the inference-only comparisons below, we freeze the standard transformer without embedding amplification and fix λ=0.1 or α=0.1."
   - anchor `App B.4` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "Both show some tolerance in LM loss and especially task accuracy. However, larger departures from the trained settings lose most of COUNT’s improvement over the standard transformer."
3. Table/serving cost: row = (n-1)*4 B prefix + 32*4 B tokens + 32 B scores + 4 B mask (order 6: 184 B, from pack/reader code); 797.5 GB for history-count>=265, order-4..6 n-gram count>=7, K=32. Inference overhead is ~4% on 1B/ONE H200 (decode 326.3 vs 340.2 tok/s at batch 1, i.e. ~0.13 ms/token absolute with 8 lookup threads); 8xH200 numbers are training only.
   - *narrowed*: requires a COUNT-pretrained model; lambda selected ex post on the reported evals; biomed accuracy mixed
   - anchor `Table 5 (prefill) and Table 6 (decode) configuration` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "Hardware One H200 GPU Batch sizes 1 and 8 sequences Prompt length 8,192 tokens COUNT table 797.5 GB; K=8 per order, orders 1–6"
   - anchor `Table 9 decode throughput (batch 1, host-resident)` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "COUNT — — 326.3 (95.9%) 18,586 (96.7%)"
   - anchor `App A.2` @ arXiv:2610.20456v1 (html): "Our primary general-domain table stores the top K=32 continuations for every history of order one through six seen at least 265 times, after dropping order-4 to order-6 n-grams with fewer than seven occurrences, and occupies 797.487 GB."
4. Reference code uses mmap + NumPy np.searchsorted on big-endian byte-string keys (first call per order builds a key copy ~1/10 of the table); the compiled multithreaded prefetching lookup behind the paper's throughput numbers is NOT in the repo.
   - *overturned*: inference figures are ONE H200 (decode batch 1: 95.9%); 8xH200 was training only

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: inference throughput is ONE H200 not 8xH200; 'training-free' scoped to table swap on COUNT-pretrained models + inference-only late fusion on standard models; 57% vs 46-89% inconsistency flagged; recipe includes learned LayerNorm amplification; compiled lookup not released.

### Verdict

COUNT is a pretraining-time input-embedding method; do NOT carry 'training-free adaptation' as applying to arbitrary GGUFs - it is table-swap on COUNT-pretrained models, plus ordinary output-side late fusion (kNN-LM-style) that works on any model but is the weaker, older baseline. The 96% throughput figure is one-H200 1B, not 8xH200. Portable ideas for EPYC: (1) the table format/pipeline (exact sort-based counting -> stupid backoff -> top-32 8-bit packed rows, binary-searched in RAM) as a corpus-scale n-gram store; (2) a zero-init-gated COUNT-style input for a Dolma2-tokenizer backbone as a cheaper-than-Engram retrofit derisk. Do not carry any throughput or compute-equivalence number beyond 1B / Dolma2 tokenizer. No independent replication found (only restatement blog).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/engram-conditional-memory.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add COUNT as a comparison point in Engram Track B proxy derisk (handoffs/active/engram-conditional-memory.md).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Check whether the n-gram tables could serve as a corpus-side drafter source for prompt-lookup revalidation (handoffs/completed/corpus-augmented-prompt-lookup-revalidation.md); table is top-32 continuations to order 6, so a drafting use is speculative.

## intake-1989#record — COUNT (Counting Offline Unlocks N-gram Transformers) reference implementation

- **URL**: https://github.com/IanMagnusson/COUNT
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. README states it is companion code for arXiv 2610.20456 and says the simple reference lookup runs on the trainer main process; it is explicitly not the optimized runtime behind the paper's throughput numbers.
   - *unaffected*: verified at HEAD 31fd41ef
   - anchor `README.md Scope paragraph @31fd41e` @ IanMagnusson/COUNT@31fd41ef4be88327d656af48463883628ceb8598: "it is not the optimized runtime behind the paper's throughput measurements. In particular the n-gram table lookup here runs on the trainer's main process before each forward pass rather than in the data-loader workers"
1. Table-building pipeline (portable, CPU/RAM only): count_corpus (exact sort-based n-gram counts to order 6 over headerless uint32 token shards, range-partitioned into 256 parts, document separator may end but never sit inside a history) -> stupid_backoff_scoring (alpha 0.4, history-count floor tau, kmax candidates per history) -> pack (K=32, per-order linear 8-bit log10-score quantisation, per-order binary-searchable sorted prefixes, 'COUN' header format id 2, 64-byte section headers). Counting loads shard data in memory at 4 B/token; optimized variants mmap/chunk.
   - *unaffected*: verified at HEAD 31fd41ef
   - anchor `src/thecount/tables/pack.py (K, quantisation, format) via README "Pack" paragraph` @ IanMagnusson/COUNT@31fd41ef4be88327d656af48463883628ceb8598: "Scores are stored in 8 bits, linearly quantized per order between the smallest retained finite score and zero."
2. Reader format (count_table.py): per order, uint32 prefixes (n-1 tokens) sorted numerically, uint32 top-K tokens, uint8 score codes, bitmask of finite candidates; lookup resolves the longest matching orders by batched binary search.
   - *unaffected*: verified at HEAD 31fd41ef
   - anchor `olmo-core/src/olmo_core/data/count_table.py:L76-L93 @31fd41e` @ IanMagnusson/COUNT@31fd41ef4be88327d656af48463883628ceb8598: "rows = np.searchsorted(sorted_keys, keys)"
3. License Apache-2.0 (one-line fact). Needs the OLMo-core fork + PyTorch only for training/eval; the table tools and reader are NumPy-only.
   - *unaffected*: verified at HEAD 31fd41ef
   - anchor `README.md Environment/Training` @ IanMagnusson/COUNT@31fd41ef4be88327d656af48463883628ceb8598: "The table should be on a RAM filesystem such as `/dev/shm`."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: confirmed all four claims; added that the repo ships NO compiled/multithreaded lookup (numpy searchsorted only), so the Stage-1 phrase 'host lookup code are the portable parts' is wrong - only the reference reader + format are; vocab is a pack parameter.

### Verdict

The repo is the right artifact to copy the TABLE FORMAT and counting/backoff/pack pipeline from; it is NOT a lookup runtime - rewrite lookup in C++ (or numba) for any serving use. Tokenizer is a build parameter (--vocab-size), so tables can be rebuilt for a Qwen/gemma vocabulary from tokenized shards on EPYC; cost scales with corpus tokens x 6 orders and the in-memory counting design must be checked at our corpus size.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] If Engram Track B proceeds, read the pack/stupid_backoff_scoring code for the table format (handoffs/active/engram-conditional-memory.md).

## intake-1991#record — An Empirical Study of Harness Design for Coding Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20804
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: context_management, agent_architecture, tool_implementation, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. Holding the execution loop fixed and varying planning, action space and context management over 176 matched settings isolates per-component effects.
   - *narrowed*: 176 settings exact; component isolation only at T4/128k; action space bundled.
   - anchor `Abstract; §3.1 Ablation Settings` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "we evaluate 176 matched settings spanning five context-management strategies, four context-window budgets, and targeted ablations of planning and action space."
   - anchor `§3.1 Ablation Settings` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "The 20 context-management settings and the two additional component ablations produce 22 settings per model–benchmark pair, for a total of 176 experimental settings across four models and two benchmarks."
1. Context management matters more as the window budget shrinks, mostly by eliminating context-overflow failures.
   - *unaffected*: Numbers exact; interpretive caveat that T0 hard-terminates.
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results, 'value of context management'` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "the managed–T0 gap shrinks steadily across 32k, 64k, 96k, and 128k windows: from 35.7 to 15.9, 5.5, and 2.7 percentage points on SWE-Bench, and from 9.5 to 7.5, 4.8, and 2.8 on Terminal-Bench (Tables 3 and 4)."
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results, Figure 3 text` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "the model-averaged T0 overflow rate falls from 78.7% to 8.7% on SWE-Bench and from 61.0% to 12.1% on Terminal-Bench, while all managed tiers have zero overflow failures throughout."
2. Rule-based elision of stale tool observations before LLM summarization is the cheapest effective strategy; making elided content recoverable (recall tool) gave no accuracy gain and is often never called.
   - *narrowed*: Recall null confirmed; 'cheapest' is among managed tiers, T1 ~ T4 on accuracy, T0 cheaper at wide windows.
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results, T4 paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "Figure 4 shows that T4 achieves success rates comparable to T1–T3, with the lowest cost in seven of eight model–benchmark panels."
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results, Recall (M2) paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "Across 32 model–benchmark–window comparisons, T2 outperforms T1 in 15 settings, underperforms in 14, and ties in three; the equal-weight mean difference is −0.36 percentage points (+0.40 on SWE-Bench, −1.12 on Terminal-Bench)."
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results, Recall (M2) paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "Among the 64 T2 and T4 settings, 36 (56.3%) never call recall_event, the median invocation rate is zero,"
   - anchor `§3.1 Implementation Details` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "Tool results are truncated to 24k characters, and up to eight read-only tools may run in parallel per step."
   - anchor `§2.3 Context management` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "Tier 0 disables context management; trajectories that outgrow the window terminate with an error."
   - anchor `§3.2 Table 3, 128K / T4 row` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "T4 | 25.20 | 0.09 | 44.00 | 0.34 | 65.80* | 2.33 | 68.60 | 3.14"
3. Planning helps weak models' accuracy and mainly saves cost for strong ones; bash-only action space can beat predefined tools for bash-capable models at lower cost but hurts a model with weaker bash skill.
   - *narrowed*: Confirmed; add Mistral Terminal-Bench reversal and bundled-interface confound.
   - anchor `§3.2 Planning paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "For Nemotron-3 30B, planning increases success rate by 11.6 percentage points on SWE-Bench and 4.5 points on Terminal-Bench, at higher cost on both benchmarks."
   - anchor `§3.2 Planning paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "On SWE-Bench, their costs fall by approximately 30% and 32%, respectively, while success rates decrease by 2.0 and 0.4 percentage points (Tables 3 and 4)."
   - anchor `§3.2 Action-space paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "For Nemotron-3 550B, bash-only improves success rate by 3.6% on SWE-Bench and 5.6% on Terminal-Bench while reducing cost by 53% and 30%, respectively."
   - anchor `§3.2 Action-space paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "The full tool set improves success by 23.2% on SWE-Bench, but bash-only adds 6.7% on Terminal-Bench."
   - anchor `§3.2 Action-space paragraph` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "On Terminal-Bench, 66% of bash-only trajectories terminate after such out-of-interface emissions, shortening the average trajectory from 71 to 15 turns."
   - anchor `§2.2 Action space` @ arXiv:2609.20804v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 17 Sep 2026): "The comparison should therefore be interpreted as the effect of the complete action interface, including tool availability, interface instructions, state tracking, and validation support, rather than as the isolated effect of tool count or action granularity."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 dive (FULL read of arXiv:2609.20804v1 HTML, sha256 f2d4814c...; Stage-1 read 100000 of 163580 chars and did not reach Appendix 7-9). Stage-1 numbers all reproduce. Changes: (a) key_claim 0 narrowed - planning/action-space ablated only at T4/128k, action-space arm bundled, no factorial; (b) key_claim 2 narrowed - 'cheapest' means lowest mean cost among MANAGED tiers (7/8 panels) at comparable accuracy; T1 elision-only is within noise of T4 on accuracy, T3 wins some 32k cells, T0 is cheaper at >=96k; (c) T0 control hard-terminates at the window, so the managed-vs-T0 gap is overflow avoidance by construction; (d) Mistral Terminal-Bench reversal for bash-only (+6.7pp) added; (e) cost is OpenRouter list price x tokens on BF16 SGLang models (Nemotron-3 30B/120B/550B, Mistral-Medium-3.5-128B), no local-lineup or CPU number; (f) paper cites intake-2007#record (Lewis 2026) and intake-2008#record (Liu 2026) as related work only - no evidential dependency in either direction. Stage-1 'dive: no' superseded: relevance medium-high for tool-output-compression recall decisions. credibility_score 2 -> 3. Surfaced sources: ACON (arXiv 2510.00615) = intake-2150#record, VISTA (arXiv 2606.30005) = intake-2151#record and LCM (arXiv 2605.04050) = intake-2152#record were ingested-and-dived as Stage-2b entries; the LCM paper tests no agent recovery (only OOLONG aggregation; losslessness is storage, not recovery), so the recall null stands unrefined by it. Declined: arXiv 2604.02547 (monitor; method-only trajectory encoding), arXiv 2509.10769 AgentArch (decline; enterprise tasks, tangential), arXiv 2609.00006 Harness Engineering source-code study (decline for now; descriptive survey, no number to verify). 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-23: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: Stage-2 sources recorded in the dive_corrections of intakes 1991, 1995, 1999, 2007 and 2008: arXiv 2604.02547 (monitor), 2509.10769, 2609.00006, 2603.00729, 2603.26830, 2601.22047 (id unverified), 2608.19013, MemoryArena and MemoryBench -> M14. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite the overflow-avoidance decomposition (managed-vs-T0 gap 35.7pp at 32k down to 2.7pp at 128k on SWE-Bench; T0 hard-terminates at the window) and the recall-tool null (T2-T1 -0.36pp; 56.3% of T2/T4 settings never call recall_event). Do not cite 'cheapest strategy' without 'among managed tiers' (T1 elision-only is within noise of T4 on accuracy; T0 is cheaper at >=96k). Do not cite planning or action-space effects as isolated (ablated only at T4/128k; the action-space arm is a bundled interface change). Do not transfer its Nemotron/Mistral thresholds or OpenRouter list-price costs to the EPYC lineup.

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`
