## intake-2006#record — Rethinking the Evaluation of Harness Evolution for Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12227
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Existing harness-evolution evaluations compare against no matched feedback/inference-budget task-level search baseline; this paper matches by rollout count (K=5, m=1) and excludes the harness-modification (meta-agent / debugger) cost, which it argues would only strengthen its conclusion.
   - *narrowed*: Matching is by rollout count; harness-modification cost excluded (A.1 Cost).
   - anchor `abstract (v4)` @ arXiv:2607.12227v4 (html): "First, prior work does not compare these approaches with simple task-level search baselines under matched feedback and inference budgets."
1. Prior work searches harnesses with verification signals from the same benchmark on which final results are reported (no train/test separation).
   - *unaffected*: CONFIRMED: v4 abstract: search uses verification signals from the same benchmarks on which final performance is reported.
   - anchor `abstract (v4)` @ arXiv:2607.12227v4 (html): "Second, prior work searches for harness configurations using verification signals (e.g., unit test cases) drawn from the same benchmarks on which it reports the final performance of the evolved harnesses, violating the standard separation between training and test data."
2. Terminal-Bench 2.1 (89 tasks; Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.4, GPT-5.4 mini; two runs averaged): harness evolution (AHE, explore agent disabled) does not beat parallel sampling or sequential refinement with or without unit tests (avg pass@1 no-tests 67.4 vs parallel 72.3 vs direct 68.2; with tests 69.7 vs 82.0). On a disjoint 45/10/34 split the evolved harness changes the 3-model average by exactly 0.0 (63.2 -> 63.2; +1.2/+0.0/-1.4). In v1/v2 this row had two models and read +0.6 (67.7 -> 68.3).
   - *narrowed*: Harness scaling is a near-tie with parallel sampling in the no-test setting (71.8 vs 72.3); generalisation result is n=34 test tasks, flat +0.0 over 3 models in v3/v4 (was +0.6 over 2 models in v1/v2).
   - anchor `§4.4 / Table 3 (v4)` @ arXiv:2607.12227v4 (html): "Specifically, we split Terminal-Bench 2.1 into 45 training tasks, 10 validation tasks, and 34 held-out test tasks."
3. Long-horizon games are where task-specific harness scaling beats sequential refinement: ARC-AGI-3 +79.0%/+81.0% (Claude Opus 4.8 / GPT-5.6 Terra, RHAE 43.3->77.5, 21.1->38.2) and EdgeBench games +13.3%/+8.5% (Claude 48.3->54.7, GPT-5.4 45.9->49.8). Per-task same-instance adaptation, not held-out, no CIs.
   - *narrowed*: The abstract 80%/11% are means of two per-model relative gains; the comparison is not held-out and baselines are VISTA*/vendor CLIs.
   - anchor `§5.2 Table 4 text (v4)` @ arXiv:2607.12227v4 (html): "The [full harness] variant improves over [VISTA*] from 43.3 to 77.5 RHAE with Claude Opus 4.8 (+79.0% relative) and from 21.1 to 38.2 with GPT-5.6 Terra (+81.0%)."
4. Recommended protocol: matched-budget task-level baselines (parallel sampling, sequential refinement, harness scaling) and held-out tasks for harness evolution; separate optimisation feedback from final measurement.
   - *unaffected*: CONFIRMED: v4 abstract last sentence and §6; the paper itself holds out only in the Terminal-Bench Table 3 experiment.
   - anchor `abstract (v4)` @ arXiv:2607.12227v4 (html): "Together, these findings highlight the need for matched-budget baselines and held-out evaluation to distinguish genuine harness improvements from benchmark-specific search and overfitting"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: read v4 body+appendices A.1-A.3,B.1 config,C table 5 from disk and diffed Table 1/2/3 and abstract against v1 and v2 (v3 inspected via streamed html text only). Found: Table 3 changed (v1/v2 two models, avg 67.7->68.3 +0.6; v3/v4 three models, 63.2->63.2 +0.0). Table 2 averages changed likewise. v1/v2 had no games section; v3 abstract 80.0%/10.9%, v4 rounded 80%/11%. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): github.com/rethinking-harness-evolution - Reproducibility of the K=5 setup does not change the version-staleness finding the dive settled. [wave2 declined]; VISTA visual harness (Han et al. 2026) - ARC-AGI-3 baseline provenance only; no arXiv ID observed. [wave2 declined].

### Verdict

Use v4 only. The "average held-out gain of just 0.6 points" and "GPT-5.4 and Claude Opus 4.6 only" wording in existing records is v1/v2 and is STALE: v3/v4 add GPT-5.4 mini to Table 3 and the three-model average is exactly +0.0 (+1.2 Opus alone). Direction of the caution is unchanged (held-out gain ~0), but quote it as "+0.0 avg over 3 models, n=34 tasks, no CI" and never as +0.6. The v1/v2 abstract sentence "does not consistently outperform ..." is not in the v4 abstract. Positive result for harness scaling exists only on long-horizon games with same-task adaptation. Do not treat as a rebuttal of Harness-R1/MemoHarness: v4 text has zero occurrences of those names.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Add matched-budget + held-out checklist to harness-improvement-loop.md experiment template.

## intake-2007#record — Same Model, Different Harness: Different Coding-Agent Results

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26218
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1991#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. With model and tasks fixed, changing only the harness changes coding-agent results.
   - *unaffected*: Paired design as described.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "We ask whether changing the harness changes the result when the model and task stay fixed."
   - anchor `§8 Conclusion` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "The same model produced different coding-agent results under different configurations of the same harness."
1. Treatment keeps the same record but shortens older tool results as context fills and reacts to repeated or stalled work; control keeps the full conversation.
   - *narrowed*: Treatment is a 3-part package; Stage-1 described only view shortening.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "The control supplies the full conversation in time order, while the treatment keeps the same record but mechanically shortens older tool results as the context fills and responds to repeated or stalled work."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "The control configuration presents the history as one growing transcript in time order and stops the run when the transcript fills the context window, even if the patch is incomplete."
   - anchor `§3.1 One paired change` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "so pairing estimates the total effect of assigning the treatment condition as run. It does not identify the separate effect of any one treatment rule."
   - anchor `§2.2 Figure 2 caption` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "The treatment starts shortening only after the estimated full prompt reaches half the configured window. The newest four tool results remain full."
   - anchor `§3.1 One paired change` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "We froze the operating point before the reported paired campaigns but did not select it through a task-disjoint hyperparameter study."
2. Gains concentrate under tight context windows; with wide windows outcomes on Verified and Pro are close.
   - *narrowed*: Verified/Pro parity at 262k holds; FeatureBench retains gain; 43k window not significant.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "on this cohort, treatment raises mean per-task F2PF from 28% to 49% and complete solutions from 43 to 72."
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "On Verified and Pro, the treatment advantage seen under pressure largely disappears: mean per-task F2PF and complete solutions are close between arms."
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "FeatureBench differs. On the same 183 tasks, mean per-task F2PF rises from 23.9% to 30.7%."
   - anchor `Table 7, Verified 43,008 row` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "SWE-bench Verified | 43,008 | 169 | 76 | 87 | 20:9 (0.0614)"
   - anchor `Appendix A.3 Resource use` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "Treatment used more model work in every comparison and more often remained active after control reached its context limit."
   - anchor `Appendix A.3 Table 9, Verified row` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "SWE-bench Verified | 169 | 3,280 / 6,517 | 37.8 / 80.7 | 1.3 / 4.6"
   - anchor `Appendix A.4` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "treatment served 7.2% fewer prompt tokens per turn;"
3. The same frozen treatment improved three other models without retuning.
   - *narrowed*: Model-specific profiles required; Nemotron resolution p=0.0636; single cell.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "Without model-specific retuning, the same frozen treatment also raises both endpoints on the same cohort for three additional models with different designs."
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "The transfer models required model-specific profiles and serving settings, but no harness-code fork or treatment retuning."
   - anchor `Appendix Table 11, Nemotron row` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "Nemotron | 12.0/18.3 | +6.4 [+1.4, +11.4] | 0.0169 | 0.0169 | 16/25 | 0.0636"
   - anchor `Appendix A.10` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "No altered setting improved resolution beyond the observed rerun difference."
4. Evaluations should treat model plus harness as the tested solver.
   - *unaffected*: Author implication stated as such.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.26218v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 26 Aug 2026): "Because changing the harness changed what unchanged model weights could accomplish, coding-agent evaluations should treat the model and harness together as the tested solver."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 dive (FULL read of arXiv:2608.26218v1 HTML, sha256 2b91328e...; Stage-1 read the abs page digest only). Abstract numbers confirmed (28->49%, 43->72, 169 tasks, 20,480 window). Changes: (a) treatment is a three-part package (half-life view shortening + stall detector + command safeguards); no single-rule attribution; (b) control stops the run when the transcript fills the window - a hard-stop baseline, and the treatment arm consumed 2.0-3.1x turns and 3.5-5.9x solver wall time, so the tight-window gain is largely 'kept running' rather than efficiency; (c) wide-window parity holds on Verified/Pro only; FeatureBench keeps +6.8pp F2PF at 262k (task-paired p=0.00022, repo-level p=0.096); (d) transfer models used model-specific profiles/serving settings; Nemotron resolution p=0.0636; (e) operating point was set during earlier development on the same benchmark families, not by a task-disjoint study; Table 18 probes (n=15) never beat the frozen setting; (f) single greedy trajectory per task, no run-to-run variance; 480 s fixed attempt budget makes the endpoint speed-dependent (does not transfer to CPU decode speeds); (g) engine fact: primary model Qwen3.6-35B-A3B Q4_K_XL served by llama-server (llama.cpp) - the same serving family as EPYC. Stage-1 credibility 'treat as 1-2' kept at 2. No evidential dependency on intake-1991#record despite expanded_from; 1991 cites this paper as related work only. Endpoint/opportunity discrepancy (from the yuj repo dive, intake-2153#record): the repo records the best finalized outcome across a registered ladder (initial wall cap 180 s; treatment 212 vs control 170 finalized opportunities at Verified 43,008; Pro 533 vs 338; FeatureBench 346 vs 189) while the paper states a fixed 480 s. The tight-window gain is therefore measured at a best-of-ladder endpoint with unequal numbers of finalized opportunities per arm, and the public files do not allow separating selection from effect; this lowers the weight of the 28 -> 49% headline. Surfaced sources: yuj repo = intake-2153#record and ACON (arXiv 2510.00615) = intake-2150#record were ingested-and-dived as Stage-2b entries. Declined: arXiv 2603.00729 Qwen3-Coder-Next technical report (model background only).

### Verdict

Cite the tight-window gain only as measured for a BUNDLED treatment (half-life view shortening + stall detector + command safeguards) against a control that hard-stops at the window, with 2.0-3.1x more model turns and 3.5-5.9x solver wall time in the treatment arm. Do not cite 28% -> 49% as an efficiency gain, as attributable to any single rule, or as transferable to CPU decode speeds (480 s attempt budget). The repo shows a best-of-ladder endpoint with unequal finalized opportunities per arm. Credibility stays 2.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/context-folding-progressive.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [unverified] Consider an old-tool-result shortening arm in REPL context management tests; owner agentic-serving-harness-fixes.md.

## intake-2008#record — More Is Not Always Better: Cross-Component Interference in LLM Agent Scaffolding

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05716
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1991#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Full factorial over 32 subsets of five scaffolding components (planning, tools, memory, self-reflection, retrieval) shows the all-components configuration consistently underperforms the best subset.
   - *narrowed*: Multi-seed support in one cell only; GSM8K 79% single seed, best-of-32; abs-page vs paper-body abstract mismatch (96 vs 118 conditions).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "We find that in every setting tested, the best proper subset matches or exceeds the five-component All-In system:"
   - anchor `§4.2 Multi-Seed Validation` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "T alone achieves F1=0.233±0.039 [95% CI: 0.209, 0.257], while All-In achieves F1=0.177±0.049 [0.147, 0.207]."
   - anchor `§5.1 GSM8K` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Best subset outperforms All-In by 79% (McNemar p=0.010)."
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Excluding the discovery seed (42): p=0.046."
   - anchor `§3.3 Protocol / Limitations` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Only 8B HotpotQA has 10-seed validation (paired t-test p=0.023, Wilcoxon p=0.014)."
   - anchor `§3.3 Experimental Protocol` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Configurations. Our main experiment evaluates all 32 subsets on 100 questions per benchmark."
   - anchor `arXiv abs page (citation_abstract meta; v1)` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "(96 conditions, up to 10 seeds)"
   - anchor `Abstract (paper body, v1)` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "yielding 118 controlled configurations with up to 10 random seeds on the primary setting."
1. Optimal component count varies by task (1 to 4) and model scale; some combinations hurt at 8B but help at 70B.
   - *narrowed*: Sign flip confirmed but 70B is single-seed; k*=2 and 4 not shown in body.
   - anchor `§5.2 Capacity-Dependence` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "At 8B, adding R to T hurts (Δ=−0.072); at 70B, it helps (Δ=+0.072)."
   - anchor `§5.2 Capacity-Dependence` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Best-vs-All-In gap narrows from 32% (8B, 10-seed validated) to 19% (70B, single seed, p=0.13)."
   - anchor `§5.2 Capacity-Dependence` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Two data points cannot establish a scaling law;"
   - anchor `§7.3 Claude Haiku` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Tool Use alone reaches F1=0.396, while All-In reaches F1=0.398"
2. 56.3% of tested cases violate submodularity, so greedy component selection is unreliable.
   - *narrowed*: 56.3% confirmed; rate CI overlaps 50%; triples dependent.
   - anchor `§6.2 Non-Submodularity` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Violations (gain at superset > gain at subset): 183 (56.3%); cluster-bootstrap 95% CI on violation rate: [0.43,0.66]"
   - anchor `§6.2 Non-Submodularity` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "The bootstrap CI on the violation rate overlaps 50%, so we do not claim the rate itself differs from chance."
   - anchor `§6.2 Non-Submodularity` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "These 325 triples are not statistically independent: they are derived from only 32 underlying f⁡(S) measurements,"
   - anchor `§6.2 Non-Submodularity` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "We therefore characterize greedy as empirically unreliable on this benchmark suite, though not provably suboptimal in general."
3. Effect replicates on Qwen2.5 and under prompt paraphrasing; a three-way Tool/Reflection/Retrieval synergy is exploratory only.
   - *narrowed*: Directional replication only; config-level rankings paraphrase-sensitive; three-body term is T/SR/R and exploratory.
   - anchor `§5.3 Cross-Family Replication` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Only Llama-8B is backed by multi-seed validation (p=0.023); other results are directional."
   - anchor `§7.2 Template Paraphrase` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "Individual configuration rankings are template-sensitive (Spearman ρ=0.14–0.49 across variant pairs over the 6 tested configurations),"
   - anchor `§6.3 Three-Body Catalysis` @ arXiv:2605.05716v1 (only version as of 2026-10-20; submitted 7 May 2026): "We report this as an exploratory observation: the effect is statistically significant at the per-task level but the wide CI warrants caution."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 dive (FULL read of arXiv:2605.05716v1 HTML, sha256 24bf613a...; Stage-1 read the abs page digest only). Changes: (a) 'Abstract: 96 conditions ... Llama-3.1 8B and 70B' came from the arXiv abs-page abstract; the paper body states 118 controlled configurations over five models (Llama-3.1-8B/70B, Qwen2.5-3B/7B, Claude Haiku 4.5) - corrected to 118; (b) key_claim 0 narrowed - only Llama-8B/HotpotQA is multi-seed validated; GSM8K 79% is a single-seed best-of-32; at Claude Haiku the gap is ~0; (c) key_claim 1 partial - 70B is single-seed (p=0.13) and 4-bit; k*=2 and 4 not shown in the body; (d) key_claim 2 narrowed - violation-rate CI [0.43,0.66] overlaps 50% and the authors disclaim the rate; evidence is the ratio and sign flips; (e) three-body term is (Tool, Structured Reasoning, Reflection), exploratory; (f) scope: prompt-block scaffolds, 4 ReAct steps, 100 questions per cell, no agentic coding task, Tool Use component bundled with the Finish[] protocol (confound), paper's own list of limitations; (g) author affiliation Amazon (data scientist), personal work, no venue. credibility_score 2 -> 1. integration_disposition monitor retained. Surfaced sources: GAIA scaffold effects (arXiv 2606.08529) = intake-2154#record and Arbiter (arXiv 2603.08993) = intake-2155#record were ingested-and-dived as Stage-2b entries. Declined: arXiv 2603.26830 (regression framework for prompt component impact; method precursor, arithmetic tasks only) and arXiv 2601.22047 (cited by the paper; id NOT verified by the dive, so it is kept out of structured fields until resolved).

### Verdict

Cite only as a design caution to ablate scaffolding subsets per role and model size, never as quantitative evidence about orchestrator scaffolding: prompt-block scaffolds on HotpotQA/GSM8K, 4 ReAct steps, only the Llama-8B/HotpotQA cell is multi-seed. The stated condition count is 118 configurations, not 96. Do not cite the 79% GSM8K figure (single-seed best-of-32) or the 56.3% submodularity-violation rate (CI overlaps 50%; authors disclaim the rate). Credibility 1.

## intake-2009#record — MTEB leaderboard and embeddings-benchmark/results: maintainer-run EmbeddingGemma 2 scores

### Claims (as verified)

0. The leaderboard UI (leaderboard.mteb.org/models) is a JavaScript app shell with no machine-readable scores; its backing data is the git repo embeddings-benchmark/results, which at HEAD 6df800d0 holds google__embeddinggemma-2 revision 914f7f89 (the same revision as the HF model snapshot used for intake-1964#record), 247 files (131-task multilingual set, code, and other suites).

   - anchor `results/google__embeddinggemma-2/914f7f89.../model_meta.json /revision` @ embeddings-benchmark/results@6df800d0d3b2d581ac716a054fce8b248833f975: ""revision": "914f7f89142e33e77833254d9c9b90c3cef7303b","
1. The listed EmbeddingGemma 2 results were NOT submitted by Google: Google's own results PR #745 (reporting 61.36 multilingual / 78.68 code) was closed unmerged by its author on 2026-10-12 ('adjusting our pipeline'), and the files present came from PR #793 by MTEB maintainer Kenneth Enevoldsen (merged 2026-10-19), run with mteb 2.26.10, sentence-transformers 6.1.0, transformers 5.20.1 and the EmbeddingGemma2Wrapper reference loader; the PR checkbox states the results were obtained with the reference implementation.
   - *unaffected*: New entry. Reattributes provenance: independent maintainer run, not publisher-submitted.
   - anchor `embeddings-benchmark/results PR #745 comment 4 (issue-comments API) and PR #793 body checklist` @ embeddings-benchmark/results PRs #745/#793 (GitHub API, fetched 2026-10-20): "Closing this for now as we are adjusting our pipeline."
   - anchor `embeddings-benchmark/results PR #793 body checklist` @ embeddings-benchmark/results PR #793 (GitHub API, fetched 2026-10-20): "- [x] The results submitted are obtained using the reference implementation"
2. Reading the maintainer-run files, an unweighted re-aggregation (mean of per-task mean main_score over every stored split/subset; NOT the official leaderboard formula) gives EmbeddingGemma 2: MTEB(Multilingual, v2) 60.52 over 131 tasks and MTEB(Code, v1) 77.88 over 12 tasks, versus publisher 61.36 and 78.68. The same re-aggregation reproduces EmbeddingGemma-300m at 61.14 (publisher 61.15) and 68.76 (publisher 68.76), so the method tracks the official numbers, yet EmbeddingGemma 2 lands about 0.6 points BELOW the 300m predecessor on the multilingual suite, not 0.2 above it, while the code gain over the predecessor (+9.1) is reproduced.
   - *narrowed*: Own aggregation; flagged as approximate.
   - anchor `dive-mteb-20261020/agg-output.txt (agg.py over embeddings-benchmark/results@6df800d0d3b2d581ac716a054fce8b248833f975)` @ embeddings-benchmark/results@6df800d0d3b2d581ac716a054fce8b248833f975 + mteb benchmarks.py fetched from main 2026-10-20 (sha256 93170349...): "google__embeddinggemma-2 ml tasks listed 131 found 131 mean 60.52"
3. The maintainers' model_meta records 271,002,624 parameters, 768-d, 8,192 max tokens, license identifier 'gemma', open_weights true, and lists MIRACL, FEVER, NQ and HotpotQA hard-negative retrieval tasks as training datasets (training-data overlap with evaluation tasks).
   - *unaffected*: New entry.
   - anchor `model_meta.json /license` @ embeddings-benchmark/results@6df800d0d3b2d581ac716a054fce8b248833f975: ""license": "gemma","

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: located backing data (embeddings-benchmark/results), identified submitter (MTEB maintainer after Google's PR was closed), and compared with publisher numbers.

### Verdict

EmbeddingGemma 2 IS listed on the MTEB data repo, and it was run by MTEB maintainers (PR #793, mteb 2.26.10), not self-reported by Google; Google's own submission (PR #745) was withdrawn. The independent run puts the multilingual score near the 300m predecessor (about 60.5 vs 61.1 by an unweighted re-aggregation), i.e. no multilingual gain, while the code-retrieval gain (~+9 points) reproduces. Do NOT cite 61.36 as independently confirmed and do NOT cite 60.52 as the official leaderboard number (it is this dive's aggregation; read the leaderboard UI or mteb's own summary before quoting). Nothing here speaks to GGUF/llama.cpp, quality at truncated dimensions, or latency on EPYC.

## intake-2010#record — Stateless Language Agents: Scaling Long-Horizon Automated Research

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07625
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, context_management, swarm_techniques)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1998#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SLA (Stateless Language Agents) splits state from context: the harness owns the research state (candidate solutions and measured outcomes, a full chronological record) and rebuilds a fresh role-specific context for every invocation. A stateless Advisor (new session each epoch) sees the task, the current global-best code+score and a harness-built evidence summary grouped by search direction that separates measured non-improvements from inconclusive implementation/runtime/correctness failures, and assigns one concrete experiment per Worker; each Worker (new session per trial, W=3 default, 3 local trials per epoch) sees only task, its assignment, its retained local candidate and compact feedback (previous status, score, correctness diagnostics). Workers cannot read other Workers workspaces or the full history.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "no agent carries its conversation across invocations; instead, the harness owns the research state (candidate solutions and measured outcomes) and reconstructs a fresh and role-specific context for every invocation."
   - anchor `§3.1 Worker context` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "Each trial, a Worker receives the task specification, its assignment, its retained candidate, and compact local feedback: the previous trial’s status, measured score, and available correctness diagnostics."
1. Statelessness is enforced, not requested: Landlock filesystem restriction, restricted network, saved sessions and temp files cleared between trials, evaluators protected and run outside Worker environments, built-in subagents disabled. The Advisor prompt keeps a sanitized scalar ledger ("Reset means fresh reasoning from measured state, not amnesia") so SLA is a harness-managed memory design, NOT the keep-nothing-but-the-best-candidate reset of intake-1998#record.
   - anchor `App E Anthropic Kernel Advisor Prompt` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "Reset means fresh reasoning from measured state, not amnesia."
2. Token-matched evaluation (one external cumulative-token counter across all roles, 1B tokens on Anthropic VLIW kernel, 700M per FrontierSWE task): on the VLIW kernel with Codex+GPT-5.5, 3 runs each, SLA 1112.0+-8.9 cycles vs SwarmResearch 1275.7+-133.9 (best baseline mean), CORAL 1350.0+-134.2, EvoX 1343.3+-4.0; every SLA run beats every baseline run (worst SLA 1122 vs best baseline 1191). SLA needs 67.9M tokens to reach the best baseline final (986.3M for SwarmResearch). Every other configuration (Claude Code arm, FrontierSWE x4, SOL-ExecBench x3, FrontierCS) is ONE run per method.
   - anchor `§4.1 Baselines (common protocol)` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "All methods share the same coding agents, models, seed solutions, public task materials, and protected evaluators, and the same external cumulative-token counter stops every run."
   - anchor `§4.2 Table 1 text` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "On the Anthropic kernel task with Codex, SLA needs only 67.9M tokens, compared with 986.3M for SwarmResearch, a 93.1% reduction."
   - anchor `§5 Limitations` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "Because long-horizon runs are costly, most configurations use a single run. Only Anthropic kernel optimization with Codex and the checkpoint ablations are replicated."
3. Ablations resumed from shared checkpoints (3 continuations, +100M tokens): removing Advisor context reconstruction, Worker isolation or Advisor assignments each reduces mean progress in all 8 comparisons (e.g. kernel 100M->200M: full -25.7 cycles vs -23.3% / -42.8% / -10.5% of that progress), but the continuations are short and several differences are small relative to std; a single full 1B-token run without Worker isolation ends at 1336 vs 1112.0 cycles (n=1). Rankings flip with horizon: SLA trails the best baseline on all four FrontierSWE tasks at 25% budget and leads at 100%.
4. Relation to intake-1998#record (forgetting): neither reproduces nor contradicts it. The paper never ablates conversation retention versus reset and its Limitations say so; it studies where state LIVES and who chooses experiments. Its mechanisms overlap in spirit (fresh contexts every invocation; the Advisor prompt calls its design "frontier-reset"), but it retains all measured outcomes in harness state and gives the Advisor a summary of them, whereas 1998 discards everything except the best candidate. Its own CORAL trace (App D.1) shows a discarded-then-recovered feature (a note from ~60 attempts earlier added +0.55 to a leading candidate), i.e. a counter-datum to discarding all history. Both papers use the same Anthropic VLIW kernel task but different models, budgets (tokens vs dollars) and baselines, so 1112 vs 1097 cycles are NOT comparable.
   - anchor `§5 Limitations` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "Our ablations change what each agent sees, not whether it keeps its conversation across invocations."
   - anchor `App D.1 Implications` @ arXiv:2610.07625v1 (html, sha256 dd362939...9c17): "Discarding older results would have lost it."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2 (NEW source, combined Stage-1+2): paper read from arXiv HTML on disk (main body Tables 1-7, App A-D, App E prompts through the FrontierSWE advisor prompt). Settled that SLA neither reproduces nor contradicts 1998 (no retention-vs-reset ablation; keeps measured outcomes in harness state and gives the Advisor a summary). Replication is limited: only the Codex VLIW kernel arm and the checkpoint ablations have 3 runs.

### Verdict

Take away: a Stanford/SambaNova design in which ALL durable state lives in the harness and every agent invocation (including the planner/Advisor) starts a fresh session beat three published frameworks at matched tokens on the Anthropic VLIW kernel (3 runs, large margin) and on FrontierSWE (1 run each); AK already has stateless actors and a lessons store, so the transferable pieces are (a) the harness-built evidence summary that separates measured non-improvement from inconclusive failure, (b) explicit per-lane assignment by a stateless planner, (c) reporting at several cumulative budgets. Do NOT carry: that SLA reproduces or refutes the "forget everything except the best" result of intake-1998#record (it never tests conversation retention); that gains transfer to noisy, expensive real-hardware judges (all tasks have fast deterministic evaluators; the kernel task is a simulated VLIW machine; the NVIDIA SOL-ExecBench tasks are single-run); any claim that Worker isolation matters at short horizons (differences after 100M tokens are small, the 1B contrast is n=1). No code release was verified and no independent replication exists.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2011#record — Beneath the Diff: Diagnosing and Mitigating Algorithmic Mode Collapse in Code-Level Autonomous Research Loops

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00077
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1998#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Algorithmic mode collapse is measured as a DISSOCIATION between axes over a run: surface diversity S_t (mean pairwise normalized Levenshtein distance of the last W=20 accepted diffs) stays flat (0.66-0.68) while semantic diversity (HDBSCAN cluster count of Sentence-BERT embeddings of per-edit one-sentence intent descriptions; falls 50-70% over 500 iterations) and mechanism entropy H_t (Shannon entropy over a frozen 9-category ML-edit taxonomy; 1.76 -> 1.18 nats on T2) collapse. The conclusion is that surface-only monitoring misses it.
   - anchor `§3.2 Surface diversity` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "For each accepted edit, we extract its unified diff and compute the normalized Levenshtein distance between every pair of diffs in a sliding window of the W=20 most recent accepted edits."
   - anchor `§3.2 Semantic diversity` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "We embed all descriptions accumulated up to iteration t with Sentence-BERT (Reimers and Gurevych, 2019) and cluster them with HDBSCAN (Campello et al., 2013) at a fixed minimum cluster size of 5."
   - anchor `§3.2 Mechanism entropy` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "Ht is the Shannon entropy (Shannon, 1948) of the category distribution over a window of W accepted edits."
1. Collapse co-moves with a faithfulness gap: faithfulness ratio rho = audited gain / in-loop gain is 0.38-0.49 for vanilla autoresearch (T=300, four single-A100 NLP tasks, proposer Claude Opus 4.7, 3 seeds / 5 on T1), in-loop gains overstate held-out gains 2.0-2.6x by iteration 300; the construct is validated with 12 known-good edits (rho 0.93) vs 8 proxy-only edits (rho 0.11). Longer horizon (T=1000, one seed) deepens collapse (H_t 0.94, rho 0.31).
   - anchor `§4.4 (a)` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "St stays in [0.66,0.68] for both methods throughout, while Ht under Vanilla AR drops from 1.76 nats at t=30 to 1.18 nats at t=300."
   - anchor `§1` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "By iteration 300, average in-loop gains overstate held-out gains by a factor of 2.0 to 2.6 across our tasks."
2. Mitigation DAPS = category-coverage reweighting of N sampled candidates (w = exp(-log p_t(c)/tau_c)), FIFO memory of the last 200 accepted edit-description embeddings with cosine rejection (tau_m 0.85), and a revert gate every K=10 iterations when in-loop minus audit gain exceeds a threshold (80th percentile of vanilla gap over first 30 iterations). Measured effect (avg of four tasks, T=300): cluster decay 0.68 -> 0.21 (-69.1%), rho_audit 0.44 -> 0.79, rho_blind 0.41 -> 0.74, in-loop gain 0.983x of vanilla; +1.4% wall-clock (reported for task T2 only); raising temperature does nothing (HiTemp rho 0.43-0.51); the audit-gated revert alone gives rho 0.67 but leaves dC at 0.55, and ccr+pem alone (no audit access) gives rho 0.71, dC 0.28.
   - anchor `§4.3` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "with zero audit access ccr+pem already surpasses both published diversity mitigations on both faithfulness tiers"
   - anchor `§4.2 Third` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "Increased proposer entropy does not, in our experiments, translate to increased semantic diversity"
3. Scope: all main results are ML training-pipeline loops on one A100 with authors-built baselines; the only non-ML evidence is a 150-iteration, 2-seed probe (App M) in which 66% of late accepted edits were caching or micro-optimisations. No GPU-kernel/CPU-kernel loop, no independent replication, code released (MIT) at BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse.
   - anchor `App M` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "surface diversity is flat (0.71 to 0.70) while semantic clusters fall from 11 to 5"
   - anchor `Limitations` @ arXiv:2609.00077v1 (html, sha256 cf65c855...7ca1); code BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "Our study is confined to model and data scales accessible on a single A100 machine"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2 (NEW source): paper read from arXiv HTML on disk (all of §1-5, App A-N text; figures are images). Settled the measurement (four axes; the headline dissociation is S_t flat vs H_t / C_t collapsing), the mitigation effect sizes and their attribution (revert gate vs ccr+pem), and found an early performance-engineering probe (App M) but no kernel-search loop. Repo cloned and README/LICENSE read, nothing executed. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: +1.4% wall-clock is a T2-only figure, not a four-task average. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): arXiv:2602.13103 R-Diverse - Origin of the surface-vs-semantic dissociation only; would not change an AK decision (dive rec). [wave3 declined].

### Verdict

Take away: the DIAGNOSTIC is the reusable part. Edit-surface diversity is blind to collapse; counting clusters of per-attempt intent descriptions and the entropy of a frozen mechanism taxonomy over a sliding window exposes it, and an in-loop-vs-held-out gain ratio exposes the Goodhart cost. All of it is computable offline from logged proposals and metrics. Do NOT carry: the 9-category taxonomy (ML pipeline edits: optimizer, scheduler, loss...) - AK needs a kernel taxonomy derived the same way, from pilot logs, frozen before analysis; the DAPS numbers as a prediction for AK (single A100, small ML tasks, authors-tuned baselines, no replication); the idea that more diversity is automatically better (the paper itself notes DAPS edits are "not necessarily better edits", and the audit-gated revert accounts for most of the faithfulness gain on its own). The rho analog for AK is already structurally present as screen-vs-serving-gate divergence.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2012#record — Ansor: Generating High-Performance Tensor Programs for Deep Learning

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.06762
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2000#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Ansor is a sample-complete-programs-then-fine-tune tuner: it samples whole tensor programs from an automatically generated hierarchical space (sketches + random annotation), then iterates evolutionary search scored by a learned cost model, measures only a small top batch per round on hardware, and re-trains the model on all measurements so far. The cost model is a gradient-boosted decision tree predicting a per-innermost-statement score (summed over the program) from a 164-dim hand-engineered feature vector (op counts, vectorize/unroll/parallel annotations, thread bindings, buffer bytes/lines/reuse, arithmetic-intensity curve), trained with a loss that weights faster programs, a new model each round from scratch (<30,000 samples).
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "We train a gradient boosting decision tree [9] as the underlying model f."
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "Because we mostly care about identifying the well-performing programs from the search space, we put more weight on the programs that run faster."
   - anchor `App B` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "The length of a feature vector including all the listed features for one statement is 164. We use the same set of features for both CPU and GPU."
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "Finally, the profiling data got from measurement is used to re-train the cost model to make it more accurate."
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "Training a gradient boosting decision tree is very fast on such a small data sets, so we train a new model every time instead of doing incremental updates."
1. The paper deliberately does NOT use the cost model on incomplete programs: Fig.3 shows a model trained on complete programs has near-chance pairwise accuracy/top-k recall on partial programs, which is why Halide beam-search style pruning of partial programs loses good programs ("Beam Search" ablation) - the surrogate is applied only to complete candidates.
   - anchor `§2 (Fig.3 discussion)` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "the cost model trained on complete programs cannot accurately predict the final performance of incomplete programs."
2. Measured accuracy of the surrogate: RMSE 0.079, R2 0.958, pairwise-comparison accuracy 0.851, recall@30 of top-30 0.624 (25k ResNet-50 CPU programs, random 80/20 split, test set biased to good programs); in other words it ranks well but misses 38% of the true top-30.
   - anchor `§7.5` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "The model archives 0.079 RMSE, 0.958 R2 correlation, 0.851 pairwise comparison accuracy, and 0.624 recall@30 of top-30 programs"
3. Measured search savings are against AutoTVM, not against a cost-model-free search: 1.0-21.8x end-to-end speedup, and 2.7x-16.5x fewer measurements (3.3x-88.6x less wall-clock) to match AutoTVM on Intel CPU; the paper credits scheduler + fine-tuning + coverage jointly. The only cost-model-adjacent ablations are "No fine-tuning" (random sampling only, loses final performance) and "Beam search" (cost model used on partial programs, loses). Single-op ablation is median of 5 runs on one convolution.
   - anchor `§7.4` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "Ansor can match the performance of AutoTVM with an order of magnitude less search time."
   - anchor `§7.4` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "In Table 3a the saving in search time comes from the task scheduler, efficient fine-tuning, and comprehensive coverage of effective optimizations."
4. Limits that matter here: static dense shapes only; relies on LLVM/NVCC for instruction selection and "comes short of utilizing the special instructions ... for mixed-precision and low-precision operators"; evaluated on Intel 18/20-core CPU, V100, ARM A53 (not AMD, not HIP). The search space is a parametric schedule space (tile sizes, annotations), not LLM-written source patches.
   - anchor `§9 Limitations` @ arXiv:2006.06762 (ar5iv rendering, version not stated on page, sha256 6055529d...d92fd; v5 PDF sha256 44a4d779...3a3 saved but text not extractable locally): "Ansor comes short of utilizing the special instructions, such as Intel VNNI, NVIDIA Tensor Core, and ARM Dot for mixed-precision and low-precision operators"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2 (NEW source): read the ar5iv rendering of the paper from disk (full text, version not stated; the v5 PDF was saved but local text extraction failed, so v5-identity is unverified; this is an OSDI 2020 paper, the arXiv abs page lists Comments "OSDI 2020"). Settled the cost-model design and its measured quality, and corrected the "trial savings vs measurement-only" framing: the paper reports savings only vs AutoTVM with no cost-model-off ablation.

### Verdict

Take away: Ansor is the canonical measured-surrogate loop: rank many cheap candidates with a learned model, measure only a small top batch, retrain on every measurement. It is useful to AK only for the PARAMETRIC part of the search (tile/block sizes, unroll, thread/affinity and OMP knobs, pragmas), where a 164-dim-style statement feature vector + GBDT could be fitted from AKs own measurements and used to pre-rank configurations. Do NOT carry: "N-fold fewer trials" as a prediction (savings are vs AutoTVM and jointly credited to scheduler, fine-tuning and coverage; no cost-model-off ablation); the surrogate for LLM-written patches (Ansor features come from an IR it generates; AK candidates are source diffs); the cost model on partial/unbuilt programs (the paper shows it fails there); anything about AMD CPU, MI210/HIP or low-bit quantised kernels (not evaluated; the paper lists low-precision instruction use as a limit). The surrogate saves MEASUREMENT time only; where builds dominate an iteration (handoff-recorded AK breakdown: build 75.7%, measured 3.9%, not judged by this dive) a pre-build predictor is the prerequisite.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

## intake-2013#record — Full-Duplex-Bench v1.5: Evaluating Overlap Handling for Full-Duplex Speech Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23159
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2003#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. FDB v1.5 replaces v1's pause/backchannel/smooth-turn/interruption set with four scripted OVERLAP scenarios injected while the model is speaking: user interruption (200 synthesized clips), user backchannel (99), user talking to others (100, off-axis filtering: -8 dB, >4 kHz -5 dB, reflections at 45/120 ms), background speech (100, different voice, -15 dB, 3 kHz low-pass, 100 ms echo). All stimuli are TTS-synthesized.
1. Metrics are scenario-conditioned, not TOR/JSD: (a) GPT-4o classifies the post-overlap response from Parakeet-TDT transcripts into Respond/Resume/Uncertain/Unknown; (b) stop latency t_stop = t_model_stop - t_user_start and response latency t_resp = t_model_start - t_user_end, in seconds via Silero-VAD; (c) pre- vs post-overlap prosody (WPM, pitch, intensity, UTMOSv2) with paired t-tests. Directions per Table 1/2: interruption wants Respond high with low stop and response latency; backchannel/talking-other/background want Resume high with HIGH stop latency (hold the floor) and low response latency.
   - anchor `§2.3.2` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "Both tstop and tresp are in seconds, with speech activity detected by Silero-VAD [28]."
   - anchor `§2.3.1` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "Crucially, the text-based GPT-4o evaluator operates solely on ASR transcripts from (Parakeet-TDT) [22] to perform objective semantic categorization"
2. v1's near-random backchannel JSD is NOT carried forward: v1.5 has no JSD or backchannel-frequency metric and no human-timing reference; backchannel is graded by the categorical Resume rate and stop latency. The successor therefore removes, rather than repairs, the non-discriminating metric. (Not-in-paper inference: Resume rate for a listener backchannel is scored on 99 TTS clips with no human reference, so it measures filtering, not backchannel PRODUCTION.)
3. Five systems (Table 2): Freeze-Omni, Moshi, gemini-2.0-flash-live-001, Nova Sonic, gpt-4o-realtime-preview-2024-12-17. user_intr Respond: 0.72/0.50/0.33/0.24/0.78; stop(s): 1.42/1.16/2.20/2.25/0.23; resp(s): 1.35/1.47/2.62/2.75/1.50. user_backch Resume: 0.80/0.06/0.93/0.98/0.70 (Moshi Unknown 0.92). talking_other Resume: 0.25/0.19/0.99/0.90/0.02 (GPT-4o Respond 0.91). bkg_speech Resume: 0.25/0.07/0.30/0.98/0.04. Two strategies: fast-yielding GPT-4o vs floor-holding Gemini/Sonic; Freeze-Omni (a VAD/cascaded design) is the most balanced.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "Benchmarking five state-of-the-art agents reveals two divergent strategies: a responsive approach prioritizing rapid response to user input, and a floor-holding approach that preserves conversational flow by filtering overlapping events."
   - anchor `§4.1.1` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "GPT-4o exhibits the strongest responsiveness (Respond=0.78) and extremely fast yielding (tstop=0.23 s), with Freeze-Omni closely following (Respond=0.72) and achieving the shortest tresp (1.35 s)."
   - anchor `§4.1.2` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "Sonic and Gemini are most reliable (Resume=0.98 and 0.93, Respond close to 0), with Sonic also keeping response latency modest (1.45 s)."
4. CASCADE SCORABLE: yes, offline. The framework is model-agnostic and needs only a time-synchronous output.wav per stimulus (clean_input/clean_output references are also expected for the adaptation analyses); scoring needs Parakeet-TDT ASR, Silero-VAD, UTMOSv2 and a GPT-4o call for the behavior classifier. A whisper->LLM->qwentts cascade needs a streaming harness that plays the stimulus in real time against an already-speaking assistant and records the cancel/resume behavior; the stop-latency metric maps directly onto CS-23 barge-in cancel latency. Only the GPT-4o behavior classifier is a cloud dependency (replaceable by a local judge, which then breaks comparability with the published numbers).
   - anchor `§2.1` @ arXiv:2507.23159v4 (html): "This approach is model-agnostic, making no assumptions about internal architecture and thus supporting both open-source models and closed commercial APIs."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected the w1 impression that v1.5 inherits v1's metrics: JSD/Freq/TOR are gone, replaced by behavior classes + stop/response latency + prosody; backchannel JSD problem removed not fixed. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed to the corrected claim list (dive had shifted indices); claims 0 and 2 are unanchored.

### Verdict

v1.5 is the useful FDB successor for CS-23/CS-22: scenario-conditioned stop/response latency and Respond/Resume labels over a speaking assistant, scorable on a cascade offline. Do NOT carry v1's JSD forward (v1.5 drops it); do NOT read Table 2 as a ranking of architectures (five systems, TTS-synthesized stimuli, gemini-2.0 and gpt-4o-realtime-preview endpoints since superseded, repo README now lists newer models). The Respond/Resume labelling needs GPT-4o; swapping the judge voids comparability with the published numbers. The ~1.5 s repair-gap threshold is an author heuristic with no human-data support in the paper. Stop-latency 'direction' is scenario-dependent (high is good for backchannel/off-talk/background).

## intake-2014#record — Full-Duplex-Bench-v2: A Multi-Turn Evaluation Framework for Duplex Dialogue Systems with an Automated Examiner

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07838
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2003#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. FDB-v2 evaluates MULTI-TURN dialogue: an automated Examiner (itself a speech LM, gpt-realtime, synthesized voice) drives staged goals against the Evaluatee over WebRTC in two pacing modes (Fast: may interrupt/backchannel at stage completion; Slow: replies only after end-of-turn or long pause; no barge-in) across four task families: Daily, Correction, Entity Tracking, Safety (11 policy classes).
   - anchor `§2.1` @ arXiv:2510.07838v2 (html): "Importantly, in FDB-v2 the Examiner itself is a spoken language model. We adopt gpt-realtime"
1. Metrics are LLM-judge scores, not v1-style signal metrics: Turn-Taking Fluency (TT, 1-5 per event), Multi-Turn Instruction Following (IF, 1-5 per event) and one global task-specific 1-5 score (Correction / Entity / Safety); higher is better throughout. Transcripts come from Parakeet-TDT; the judge is gemini-2.5-flash-preview-09-2025. There is NO TOR, JSD, latency-in-seconds or VAD timing metric in v2: v1's JSD problem is not addressed but made moot (backchannel timing is no longer measured; backchannels only appear as examiner behavior).
   - anchor `§2.4` @ arXiv:2510.07838v2 (html): "We leverage Gemini (gemini-2.5-flash-preview-09-2025) (Google DeepMind, 2025), conditioned on the Examiner’s system prompt and stage-level semantic goals"
2. Judge validity: on 120 human-scored sessions, Pearson r / Krippendorff alpha between judge and humans are TT 0.6137/0.6143, IF 0.6807/0.6833, Correction 0.5877/0.5879, Entity 0.6330/0.6383, Safety 0.6914/0.6931, i.e. moderate; the paper reports no judge-vs-judge or judge-seed variance and no CI on Tables 1/3 (3 systems, run counts not stated in the text read).
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2510.07838v2 (html): "The results show moderate to strong agreement across all metrics (Pearson’s r = 0.59–0.69)."
3. Systems (Table 1 task-specific, Fast): Correction/Entity/Safety FreezeOmni 2.74/2.62/3.94, Moshi 2.88/2.76/3.67, GPT-Realtime 4.02/4.51/4.44; (Slow): FreezeOmni 3.50/2.86/4.27, Moshi 3.46/3.84/3.51, GPT-Realtime 3.94/4.12/4.53. Appendix Table 3 TT/IF Fast, Daily: FreezeOmni 3.14/2.34, GPT-Realtime 3.74/3.81, Moshi 3.73/2.67. All systems degrade over a 75 s session; IF is less stable than TT. Pacing matters: Slow improves Entity IF by about 0.5-1.0 for GPT-Realtime and Moshi.
   - anchor `§3.2.3` @ arXiv:2510.07838v2 (html): "Under Fast pacing, GPT-Realtime scores above 4.0 on all three tasks, while Moshi and Freeze-Omni remain below 3.0 on Correction and Entity."
4. CASCADE SCORABLE: only through a WebRTC adapter. The cascade must be wrapped as a streaming 48 kHz/16-bit/mono 10 ms-frame peer; the framework itself is cloud-bound (OPENAI_API_KEY for the gpt-realtime Examiner, GEMINI_API_KEY for the judge per the v2 README) so a fully local run would need both a local examiner SLM and a local judge, which is an unvalidated substitution. The 1-5 TT score is an LLM impression of timing, not a measured latency, so it cannot supply a CS-2 latency gate; it could supply CS-36 end-to-end conversational acceptance after the judge is cross-checked.
   - anchor `§2.2` @ arXiv:2510.07838v2 (html): "To add a new model, implement a corresponding adapter that receives and forwards audio to its API/server"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected wave-1 framing 'v2 = FDB with timing': v2 has no TOR/JSD/latency; all metrics are 1-5 Gemini-judge scores; no cascade evaluated in the paper. Appendix text cites a nonexistent '§2.5' (should be §2.4), cosmetic.

### Verdict

v2 is a LLM-judged multi-turn conversation harness, not a timing benchmark. It answers 'does the interlocutor keep goals, corrections and entities across turns under overlapping speech', with 1-5 scores at moderate human agreement (r about 0.6). It does NOT repair v1's backchannel JSD problem (it simply drops timing metrics), and only three systems were evaluated (GPT-Realtime, Moshi, Freeze-Omni; no cascade). Treat the Examiner as a cloud dependency (OpenAI) and the judge as a cloud dependency (Gemini). Do not cite TT scores as latency evidence.
