- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2015#record — Full-Duplex-Bench-v3: Benchmarking Tool Use for Full-Duplex Voice Agents Under Real-World Disfluency

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04847
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2003#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. FDB-v3 is a tool-use benchmark: 100 recordings from 12 human speakers (native and Korean/Russian non-native English, built-in mics, ambient trailing silence), annotated for fillers, pauses, hesitations, false starts and self-corrections, over four domains with locally executed deterministic mock APIs (Travel & Identity, Finance & Billing, Housing & Location, E-Commerce Support) in Easy/Medium/Hard tiers.
   - anchor `§3.3` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "The dataset comprises 100 recordings from 12 speakers, including native and non-native English speakers (Korean and Russian backgrounds) with varying accent strengths."
1. Metrics and directions: Tool Selection F1 (up); Argument Accuracy judged by GPT-4o (up); Pass@1 requires exactly the expected tools AND perfect args on every call (up); Response Quality GPT-4o (up); Turn-take rate (up); Latency = t_agent_start - t_user_end in seconds with negative values counted as Interruptions (Interrupt rate down); Filler rate down (a filler lowers perceived latency but can overlap the user). Latency is decomposed into first-response, tool-call and task-completion (a GPT-4o pass isolates the informative sentence from filler).
   - anchor `§4.1` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "Task Completion (Pass@1): A binary metric requiring all three conditions simultaneously: the agent invokes exactly the expected tools, and scores perfect argument accuracy for every call."
2. Headline Table 2 (Tool Sel/Arg/Resp/Pass@1/Take-turn/Latency/Interrupt/Filler): GPT-Realtime 0.876/0.680/0.792/0.600/96.0%/6.89 s/13.5%/16.9%; Gemini Live 2.5 0.786/0.593/0.554/0.490/92.0%/7.26/14.1%/8.9%; Gemini Live 3.1 0.817/0.588/0.718/0.540/78.0%/4.25/19.2%/31.7%; Grok 0.797/0.542/0.617/0.430/94.0%/6.65/25.5%/44.3%; Ultravox 0.794/0.513/0.510/0.410/96.0%/8.40/47.9%/88.0%; Cascaded (Whisper->GPT-4o->OpenAI TTS) 0.803/0.562/0.600/0.450/100%/10.12/33.0%/26.9%.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "GPT-Realtime leads on Pass@1 (0.600) and interruption avoidance (13.5%); Gemini Live 3.1 achieves the fastest latency (4.25 s) but the lowest turn-take rate (78.0%); and the Cascaded baseline, despite a perfect turn-take rate, incurs the highest latency (10.12 s)."
3. A CASCADE IS EXPLICITLY SCORED AND IS THE WEAK POINT ON SELF-CORRECTION: Cascaded Pass@1 by disfluency (Table 3) is Filler 0.448, Pause 0.444, Hesitation 0.600, False start 0.500, Self-corr 0.176 (the lowest; GPT-Realtime 0.588). By difficulty (Table 4) Easy/Medium/Hard 0.639/0.441/0.233. The authors' explanation (Whisper may finalize the pre-correction transcript before the correction arrives) is an unverified hypothesis, not an ablation. Latency Table 6 (s): Cascaded first word 8.78, tool call 3.15, task completion 10.12; the same LLM family gives Pass@1 0.450 vs GPT-Realtime 0.600. All six systems run through LiveKit, which needs a LiveKit Cloud account; the shipped cascade uses OpenAI Whisper/gpt-4o/TTS and is not local.
   - anchor `§6.5` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "The gap is starkest on self-corrections: Cascaded scores only 0.176—the lowest of all systems—versus GPT-Realtime’s 0.588."
4. SCOPE: no TOR, no backchannel metric and no JSD; v1's near-random backchannel JSD problem is not addressed and is not relevant here. v3's turn-taking readout is only response timing and interruption counting on single-utterance queries; 'pauses are the weakest category' (Pass@1 0.333 for Grok and Ultravox) is the only end-of-turn signal. Sample size is small (100 scenarios total): per-cell Pass@1 differences of 0.05-0.10 are within a few scenarios and no CIs are reported.
   - anchor `§5.3` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "Pauses are the weakest category across most systems (Grok and Ultravox both at 0.333), highlighting a shared difficulty in detecting whether a user has finished speaking."
5. PORTABLE VALUE FOR OUR CASCADE: the mock-API tool-call scoring, the self-correction/state-rollback scenarios and the first-response/tool-call/completion latency decomposition are independent of LiveKit and OpenAI and can be re-hosted on a local cascade whose only tool is the orchestrator; the filler-rate/interrupt-rate readout directly measures the 'let me check on that' behavior of CS-24 routing.
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2604.04847v1 (html): "Rather than routing queries to live web services, we use locally executed mock APIs with deterministic, zero-latency responses."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Wave-1 said the shipped cascade uses Silero VAD; the paper itself describes Whisper->GPT-4o->TTS via LiveKit (Silero appears only in the repo README). Added: cascade scored in Tables 2-4/6 with self-correction 0.176; no CIs; Hard-tier is the only large discriminator. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: removed '21 of 100 self-corrections' and '11 mock APIs' (not found in the paper text on disk; pass rates imply ~17 self-correction items).

### Verdict

v3 is the one FDB successor that scores a cascade (Whisper->GPT-4o->TTS) head-to-head and shows it is slow (10.12 s) and worst at self-correction (Pass@1 0.176); this is a published failure mode of cascades that finalize ASR early, which matters for our whisper endpointing. Carry the mock-API tool scoring, disfluency categories and latency decomposition; do NOT carry the LiveKit/OpenAI runtime, the 'why' for the cascade deficit (hypothesis only), or small-cell rankings (100 scenarios, no CIs). The baseline cascade here is an OpenAI cloud stack, so its numbers say nothing about a local whisper.cpp+qwentts cascade. v1's JSD problem is irrelevant here (no backchannel metric).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2016#record — Same Words, Different Actions: Paired Turn-Taking Evaluation under Rewritten Dialogue Contexts

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17360
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty high, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2003#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The title is 'Same Words, Different Actions: Paired Turn-Taking Evaluation under Rewritten Dialogue Contexts'; ECHO ('Evaluating Context-conditioned Handling of Overlaps') IS in the paper (21 occurrences) as the NAME OF THE DATASET, so the wave-1 label 'ECHO benchmark' was a real alias, not an error (search snippets show v1's title as 'ECHO: A Matched-Contrast Benchmark for Context-Sensitive Turn-Taking in Full-Duplex Dialogue'; v2 retitled).
   - anchor `§1` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "We introduce ECHO (Evaluating Context-conditioned Handling of Overlaps), a paired diagnostic set of Chinese multi-turn dialogues."
1. ECHO is a CHINESE-only, synthetic, event-level diagnostic: 266 groups, 549 audio instances (183 each of interruption, backchannel, off-talk), 300 pair relations (100 each of I-B, I-O, B-O). Dialogue skeletons from DuplexDrama, insertions by DeepSeek-V4-Pro, context rewrites by Claude-3.5-Sonnet, audio by IndexTTS2, 97.45% of reviewed candidates judged label-consistent. The authors state it does not evaluate closed-loop interaction or latency.
   - anchor `§2.4` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "ECHO contains 266 groups, 549 unique audio instances, and 300 pair relations"
2. Metric definitions (all higher is better): Yield accuracy on interruptions, Keep accuracy on backchannel (BC Keep) and off-talk (OT Keep), macro accuracy; PASR_X = fraction of action-flip pairs where BOTH members are right (constant-Yield and constant-Keep score 0); PKC = fraction of backchannel-off-talk pairs with Keep on both (constant-Keep maximizes it, so it must be read with PASR); PRSR for three-way role prediction.
   - anchor `§3.2` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "Consequently, deterministic constant-Yield and constant-Keep policies both obtain zero PASR."
3. Table 5 (%): Int. Yield / BC Keep / OT Keep / Macro / PASR_I-B / PASR_I-O / PKC_B-O: Easy Turn 98.91/1.09/0.55/33.52/0.00/0.00/1.00; SoulX-Duplug 91.26/6.56/15.30/37.71/4.00/12.00/2.00; Lychee-FD 55.19/12.02/8.20/25.14/4.00/6.00/0.00; MiniCPM-o 4.5 63.39/65.57/53.55/60.84/54.00/53.00/49.00; Gemini-3.1-Pro (text-only reference) 90.71/86.34/71.58/82.88/79.00/52.00/70.00. Three speech systems yield on nearly everything (BC Keep <13%) so interruption accuracy alone looks excellent while paired success on interruption-vs-backchannel pairs is at most 4% (interruption-vs-off-talk: SoulX 12%, Lychee 6%).
   - anchor `§3.3 Finding 2` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "Easy Turn and SoulX-Duplug correctly yield on 98.91% and 91.26% of interruptions, respectively, but keep the floor on only 1.09% and 6.56% of backchannels."
4. CAVEATS THAT LIMIT WHAT PASR PROVES: the paired waveforms are independently synthesized (not acoustically matched), interruptions receive onset emphasis that backchannels do not, systems get different context (Easy Turn gets the insertion only; MiniCPM-o gets teacher-forced assistant text), and the authors say a waveform-matched, gain-controlled evaluation would be needed to isolate context sensitivity. Off-talk Keep is a scenario-labelled diagnostic, not a false-trigger estimate.
   - anchor `§3.3 Interpretation caveat` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "A waveform-matched, gain-controlled evaluation would be required to isolate contextual sensitivity."
5. CASCADE SCORABLE: only as an event-level yield/keep detector. The harness replays fixed dialogue trajectories and extracts a binary action (stop event during insertion) from the system, so a whisper+VAD+qwentts cascade needs (a) an overlap-time stop signal and (b) a semantic gate on the transcribed insertion; Chinese-only data means English use requires re-authoring pairs (the construction recipe is transferable). The dataset on Hugging Face (shuofeng123/ECHO) is gated (manual approval), so data access needs a request. No cloud key is needed to SCORE (binary actions), only to generate new pairs (DeepSeek/Claude).
   - anchor `§4 Limitations` @ arXiv:2609.17360v2 (html): "Moreover, replaying fixed dialogue trajectories, including teacher-forced assistant turns where required, cannot simulate real-time full-duplex interaction or evaluate latency, mutual adaptation, post-yield recovery, and subsequent response quality."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Resolved wave-1 label: 'ECHO' is the paper's own dataset name (21 text occurrences) and v1's title; current v2 title is 'Same Words, Different Actions...'. Content: Chinese, 4 speech systems plus a text-only Gemini reference, PASR/PKC/PRSR definitions, strong caveats read from §3.3 and Limitations. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: narrowed the 'at most 4%' paired-success statement to interruption-vs-backchannel pairs.

### Verdict

Use PASR/PKC as a METRIC DESIGN (paired, constant-policy-proof) for any yield/keep gate on our cascade; do not use ECHO's numbers as a speech-model ranking (Chinese, synthetic, unequal context per system, not acoustically matched, interruptions carry onset emphasis). It supplies the independent critique of event-independent scoring that wave 1 sought: it shows a 98.91% interruption Yield score coexisting with 1.09% backchannel Keep and 0% PASR. It is not a cascade benchmark per se (Easy Turn / SoulX-Duplug are the modular systems tested). 'ECHO' is the dataset name inside the paper; the arXiv title is the long one. The HF dataset is gated, so no data was read.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2017#record — parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1983#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Architecture: cache-aware streaming FastConformer-RNNT, 17 encoder layers, attention context [70,1], 120M params, English-only, no punctuation/capitalization; signals end of utterance by emitting an <EOU> token inside the RNNT output.
   - anchor `Model Architecture` @ HF nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1@a7e2b4629593dce0ec19f600e00e9904353fda2d: "cache-aware streaming FastConformer [2] with 17 encoder layers (attention context = [70,1]) and RNNT decoder."
1. Latency: card states 80ms~160ms ASR latency; input needs >=160ms of 16kHz mono audio. WER 9.30 avg on HF OpenASR leaderboard measured in a 160ms streaming setting (LS-clean 3.61, LS-other 7.79, AMI 15.62).
   - anchor `Benchmark Score / Speech Recognition` @ HF nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1@a7e2b4629593dce0ec19f600e00e9904353fda2d: "| WER (%) | 9.30 | 15.62 | 15.76 | 13.31 | 3.61 | 7.79 | 3.79 | 5.48 | 9.07 |"
2. EOU detection is reported ONLY as a latency distribution (p50 160ms, p90 280ms, p95 320ms) on TTS-generated DialogStudio audio with 3 s of appended silence. The card publishes NO precision, recall, false-cut or premature-cut rate, so the EOU quality (the thing CS-22 measures) is unreported by the vendor.
   - anchor `Benchmark Score / End-of-Utterance Detection (Latency)` @ HF nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1@a7e2b4629593dce0ec19f600e00e9904353fda2d: "The latency metrics are evaluated on TTS generated audios from DialogStudio, and a 3-second silence is appended to each sample."
3. Export/runtime: the official artifact is a single .nemo file; card lists NeMo 2.5.3+, CUDA acceleration, tested on V100/A100/A6000 and no CPU claim. Community ONNX conversions exist (ysdede: fp32/fp16/int8 encoder and decoder_joint ONNX with vocab.txt; decoder-only INT8 token-exact on one smoke fixture only, encoder INT8 not exact) - independent third-party exports, not vendor-validated.
   - anchor `Inference` @ HF nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1@a7e2b4629593dce0ec19f600e00e9904353fda2d: "**Acceleration Engine:** CUDA<br>"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Settled: card has no EOU precision/recall; EOU latency is synthetic-TTS-only; no ONNX in the official repo (only .nemo), community ONNX/CoreML ports exist. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: INT8 exactness is on a single smoke fixture.

### Verdict

A 120M streaming RNNT whose turn-end signal is an in-band <EOU> token, so ASR and end-of-turn share one forward pass. Vendor evidence is latency-only on synthetic TTS audio with clean 3 s silence; there is NO precision/recall/false-cut number, so it cannot be cited as a quality claim for CS-22 and must be measured on the CS-4 hesitation clips. Do NOT carry 'low latency 80ms' as EOU latency: EOU p50 is 160 ms, p95 320 ms (and measured on synthetic audio). CPU is untested by the vendor; the portable path is the community ONNX export (not vendor-validated).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2018#record — LocalVQE

- **URL**: https://github.com/localai-org/LocalVQE
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1983#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. LocalVQE is a causal streaming 16 kHz AEC+NS+dereverb family, 256-sample hop (16 ms latency), F32 GGML C++ inference with a PyTorch reference (model definition only), derived from DeepVQE. Sizes: v1.3 4.8M params ~19MB; v1.2 1.3M; v1.4-AEC 203K (echo only); v1.4-AEC 2.7K (linear filter only); a separate 49K GTCRN-AEC line.
   - anchor `README Models table` @ localai-org/LocalVQE@7f61cd05095d42e56a135b303f5bb4d761da22b9: "| **v1.3** *(current)* | AEC + NS + dereverb | 4.8 M | ~19 MB | 3.2 ms · 5.0× RT |"
1. CPU latency per 16 ms hop on Ryzen 9 7900 (Zen4), 4 threads: v1.3 3.21 ms p50 (5.0x RT); v1.2 1.65 ms; v1.4-AEC 0.83 ms (1 thread 1.29 ms p50, 1.89 p99); 2.7K 0.36 ms. Pi 5 single core GTCRN line ~0.78 ms/hop. All vendor-reported, not reproduced; Zen5 EPYC 9655 untested.
   - anchor `README Latency, v1.4-AEC` @ localai-org/LocalVQE@7f61cd05095d42e56a135b303f5bb4d761da22b9: "| 1 | 1.29 ms | 1.89 ms | 12.2× |"
2. Quality: full 800-clip ICASSP 2022 AEC Challenge blind test set, AECMOS echo/deg plus blind ERLE. v1.3 doubletalk echo 4.73 / deg 2.62; farend-singletalk ERLE 50.9 dB; v1.4-AEC (keeps noise/room) farend-singletalk echo 3.80, ERLE 14.6 dB. Unprocessed-mic echo MOS 2.67/2.56/1.90/2.13/5.00. ERLE of joint (noise-deleting) and AEC-only models are explicitly not comparable. Self-reported: no independent replication, no WER/ASR-downstream or barge-in-detection metric.
   - anchor `README Performance, v1.3/v1.2 table` @ localai-org/LocalVQE@7f61cd05095d42e56a135b303f5bb4d761da22b9: "| doubletalk | 115 | 4.73 / 2.62 / 8.5 dB / 2.89 | 4.72 / 2.37 / 8.4 dB / 2.83 |"
3. Every model needs a far-end reference signal (loopback of the speaker output) in addition to the mic; weights trained on DNS Challenge 2023 and fine-tuned on AEC Challenge 2022/23. Builds: CMake GGML CPU (multi-ISA libggml-cpu-*.so incl. AVX-512, runtime-selected), optional Vulkan, C API (localvqe_process_frame_f32 per-hop). No HIP/ROCm option; Vulkan is the GPU route (MI210 supports Vulkan), but the README says the neural stage is too small for GPU to pay.
   - anchor `README Models bullets` @ localai-org/LocalVQE@7f61cd05095d42e56a135b303f5bb4d761da22b9: "Every model needs a far-end **reference** signal (a loopback of what your speakers play) in addition to the mic."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Settled sizes, builds (GGML+PyTorch reference, GGUF f32/bf16), per-hop CPU latency, AECMOS/ERLE tables, test set; HF weights and the Space not fetched. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://huggingface.co/LocalAI-io/LocalVQE - Weights hashes/card only; GitHub repo already dived. [wave3 declined].

### Verdict

Small, CPU-real-time, Apache-2.0 AEC with a C API and GGUF weights; plausible as a server-side AEC stage for CS-23, BUT CS-23 currently defers echo cancellation to the client (D7) and the model needs a clean far-end reference, which the controller does have (it generated the TTS audio). Numbers are self-reported on ICASSP-2022 blind clips; they say nothing about barge-in detection accuracy or downstream WER. Do not carry '16 ms latency' as end-to-end: it is algorithmic hop latency, excluding buffering/alignment of reference to mic.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2019#record — Model2Vec: Fast State-of-the-Art Static Embeddings

- **URL**: https://github.com/MinishLab/model2vec
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: search_retrieval, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1981#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Model2Vec distills a sentence transformer into a static token-embedding table: the vocabulary is forward-passed through the teacher (no dataset needed, ~30 s on CPU), then post-processed (PCA, Zipf weighting); output embeddings beat input embeddings, and a non-sentence-transformer teacher (BERT) is much worse (ablation table).
   - anchor `README What is Model2Vec?` @ MinishLab/model2vec@dc1ff382f2794d31f3b5932ced8a858335e3ca17: "The core idea is to forward pass a vocabulary through a sentence transformer model, creating static embeddings for the indiviudal tokens."
1. potion-base-8M (7.5M params, teacher BAAI/bge-base-en-v1.5) is further trained with Tokenlearn (mean teacher embeddings over a C4 corpus, then re-regularised with frequency weighting, PCA and SIF); it is NOT plain distillation. MTEB: potion-base-8M avg 51.08, Retrieval 31.11 vs all-MiniLM-L6-v2 55.93 / 42.92. The bge-base TEACHER is not in the repo tables, so 'vs the teacher' is unreported; the comparison is to all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (the 8M card states 91.96% of all-MiniLM-L6-v2's Avg(All): 51.32 vs 55.80 - a different average from the MTEB 51.08 vs 55.93 above).
   - anchor `HF potion-base-8M card How it works` @ HF minishlab/potion-base-8M@bf8b056651a2c21b8d2565580b8569da283cab23: "Post-training re-regularization: after training, the model is re-regularized by weighting the tokens based on their frequency, applying PCA, and finally applying [SIF weighting](https://openreview.net/pdf?id=SyK00v5xx)."
2. potion-code-16M-v2 (16.2M params, teacher nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed 137M): vocab extended by 43k code tokens (~63.5k), distilled to 256-d, Tokenlearn then contrastive fine-tune on 1.2M CornStack pairs. CoIR NDCG@10 avg: teacher 59.14, potion-code-16M-v2 39.08, BM25 42.31, hybrid RRF(k=60) potion+BM25 43.36. So the static code model is BELOW BM25 alone and 20 points below its teacher; only the hybrid edges BM25 (+1.05).
   - anchor `HF potion-code-16M-v2 card Results` @ HF minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2@e9d2a44ca6a05ac6685f3b23709ea57eb7352d5b: "| CodeRankEmbed | 137M | 59.14 |"
3. Retrieval is the static models' weak axis: best static retrieval model potion-retrieval-32M scores 35.06 vs 42.92 for all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (81.69%); speed claim 'up to 500x faster on CPU' is vendor-reported, measured with multiprocessing disabled on an unstated machine.
   - anchor `results/README.md Retrieval Results` @ MinishLab/model2vec@dc1ff382f2794d31f3b5932ced8a858335e3ca17: "| [potion-retrieval-32M](https://huggingface.co/minishlab/potion-retrieval-32M) | 35.06 |"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Settled provenance of potion-base-8M (bge-base-en-v1.5 + Tokenlearn, not plain distill) and potion-code-16M-v2 (CodeRankEmbed + 43k code tokens + Tokenlearn + contrastive); static code model underperforms BM25 on CoIR. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: 91.96% belongs to Avg(All) 51.32 vs 55.80, not the MTEB averages.

### Verdict

Static embeddings are a legitimate CPU-trivial dense arm (numpy only, lookup+mean) but they are measurably weaker retrievers: ~72% of all-MiniLM on MTEB retrieval at 8M, and the code variant loses to plain BM25 (39.08 vs 42.31) and wins only fused with it (+1.05). Credible for REPL-EMB-2.2 as a cheap-cost anchor arm that tests whether ANY dense signal beats lexical, NOT as a likely winner; do not carry '500x faster' (unspecified hardware, vendor-run) or the MTEB numbers as evidence about spill-file/context-bundle retrieval. Vendor does not report the bge-base teacher on the same table.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md`

## intake-2020#record — count-checkpoints-standard-transformer

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-standard-transformer
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: memory_augmented, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1988#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The standard (non-COUNT) baseline IS released: seven OLMo-core ladder sizes 60M-1B, each at 10/20/40 tokens per parameter (1B: steps 8134 / 16268 / 32537, global batch 1,572,864 tokens), trained on Dolma 3 with the same batch as the COUNT and learned-ngram models of that size. Each checkpoint carries model weights, optimizer state and trainer state (OLMo-core distributed format, 8 train ranks for 1B).
   - anchor `README Checkpoints table` @ HF IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-standard-transformer@748e5de4cb046bb9963c37bb7133ea6b0802eac3: "| 1B | step 8134 | step 16268 | step 32537 | 1,572,864 |"
1. The 1B standard model matches the COUNT 1B training budget by design (same step numbers and batch in the COUNT repo table: 1B COUNT steps 8134/16268/32537 on table general-797gb). Matched on tokens and batch; the COUNT 190M checkpoints are a rerun, not the paper's exact weights.
   - anchor `README Checkpoints` @ HF IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-standard-transformer@748e5de4cb046bb9963c37bb7133ea6b0802eac3: "Each size uses the same global batch as the COUNT and learned n-gram models of that size."
2. Tokenizer: allenai/dolma2-tokenizer (vocab_size 100278 in the tokenizer config; model vocab_size 100352), 1B config d_model 2048, 16 layers, 16 heads, SwiGLU ff 8192, seq len 8192, WSDS schedule, trained world size 8. This is the same Dolma 2 tokenizer used by every COUNT table (vocab 100278), so the table and the standard checkpoint are token-compatible.
   - anchor `model-ladders/vanilla-1b/1B/step32537/config.json#/instance_sources/0/sources/0/tokenizer` @ HF IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-standard-transformer@748e5de4cb046bb9963c37bb7133ea6b0802eac3: ""identifier": "allenai/dolma2-tokenizer","
3. Executability of the wave-1 Engram Track B 'COUNT arm' (zero-init gate on frozen standard 1B + released table): the checkpoint and the tables (general-17gb, 142gb, 797gb; ODC-By) exist, so NO pretraining is needed to start. BUT the COUNT embedding is not a free add-on in the card's own description: it averages rows of the model's OWN output head weighted by n-gram scores, so it needs the COUNT-lookup code (olmo_core.data.count_table.CountLookup) and a patched forward. Caveats: (a) the paper's COUNT models were trained with the table from step 0, so a frozen-backbone retrofit measures something the paper does not; (b) the 1B COUNT checkpoint used general-797gb (797.5 GB, needs RAM) while the 17 GB table was used only for 60M-190M models, so 'standard 1B + 17 GB table' is an off-recipe pairing; (c) training uses OLMo-core torchrun with --cluster local/h200 examples (CUDA); a gate-only train on 1B needs the same data pipeline or a custom loop, and an MI210 port was not assessed.
   - anchor `HF IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-count README Checkpoints` @ HF IanMagnusson/count-checkpoints-count@b3522bbbe693069f55ed61582701d2cc35f88807: "| 1B | `general-797gb` | step 8134 | step 16268 | step 32537 | 1,572,864 |"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Settled that the standard 1B baseline exists at matched D/N and batch, tokenizer = dolma2, and that the Track B COUNT arm is executable w/o pretraining but off-recipe on table size. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/IanMagnusson/COUNT - Already ingested and dived as intake-1989#record (wave 1 answered: NumPy mmap/searchsorted reader, no compiled lookup). [wave3 declined].

### Verdict

The controlled baseline exists and is budget-matched, so a COUNT-vs-standard comparison needs no pretraining; the wave-1 'zero-init gate on frozen standard 1B' arm is therefore START-able, but it is an off-recipe retrofit (paper trained COUNT from scratch; 1B used the 797 GB table) and needs the COUNT lookup code. Do NOT read this as evidence that frozen-backbone COUNT works - no such result is released. Hardware: examples are CUDA/H200 OLMo-core; EPYC/MI210 port unassessed. Weights not downloaded.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/engram-conditional-memory.md`

## intake-2021#record — Eval bug: Speculative decoding (draft-mtp / draft-dspark): greedy output diverges from vanilla on quantized targets

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/25618
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: speculative_decoding, inference_serving, local_inference, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2004#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Issue #25618 (opened 2026-07-13 by Ankk98, label bug-unconfirmed, still OPEN at updated_at 2026-10-18; 31 comments, not the 15 intake-1288#record saw) reports that greedy speculative output differs from vanilla on quantized targets. Its original Vulkan/Strix-Halo matrix (Qwen3-8B Q4_K_M + draft-dspark, Gemma-4-12B Q4 + draft-mtp) showed ngram-simple/ngram-mod matching vanilla - but those ngram arms accepted 0 and 4/8 tokens on one prompt, so that control had almost no exposure (same defect our DF2-6b-bis already names).
1. The 'six-defect kernel-selection analysis' is VULKAN-ONLY (comment #3 and the 2026-09-06 summary #19, which grows it to 7): GQA-packed FA used for N>1, N-dependent FA tile/split_k, FA-off KxQ p021 vs GEMM, FA-off V*kq DMMV dual-stride and holey-view stride, F32-DMMV vs Q8_1-MMVQ choice by N (should_use_mmvq returns true for n>1), and the fused ADD->RMS_NORM partial-reduction path at N=1 only. It is not an analysis of CUDA/HIP kernels. The author's own summary: no fix was merged; PR #26358 (FA GQA gate) was CLOSED UNMERGED by its author on 2026-08-27; PR #28489 (MMVQ selection independent of N) and PR #28488 (tests) are open as of the fetch, with maintainer 0cc4m warning the MMVQ change 'will cost a lot of performance'.
   - anchor `comment #3 (Ankk98, 2026-07-13) Issue 6` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "Fix: disable auto MMVQ for DMMV entirely so both N=1 and N>1 use F32 DMMV (prefill GEMM MMQ unchanged; force-reenable via `GGML_VK_FORCE_MMVQ`)."
   - anchor `comment #19 (Ankk98, 2026-09-06)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "Vulkan spec verify vs vanilla - master@7620399 - all 6 reported bugs still present, none merged"
2. The raw-logit instrumentation is frizikk's (Vulkan RADV Strix Halo, Qwen3.8-27B Q6_K + Q8_0 MTP): at the first mismatch baseline token 548 = 24.319630 vs 320 = 24.295303, while the speculative row had 320 = 24.301819 vs 548 = 24.298832, i.e. a ~0.02-logit batch-dependent shift flips an argmax near-tie. His later bisect located the earliest boundary at Q8_0 x F32 MUL_MAT (N=1 mul_mat_vec_q8_0_f32_f32 vs N=2 quantize_q8_1_x4 + MMVQ; forcing the N=1 path made row 0 bit-exact) and a second at ADD->RMS fusion (2 ULP); FA was exonerated for his remaining prompt.
   - anchor `comment #12 (frizikk, 2026-08-20)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "The small batch-dependent logit change flips the greedy argmax."
3. Backends with a reproduction in the thread: Vulkan (Strix Halo, RADV RDNA4 R9700, RX 7900 XTX, NVIDIA GTX1660S via Vulkan), Metal (M4, M1 Max with two isolated causes: mul_mv vs mul_mv_ext and flash_attn_ext_vec simdgroup count), and CUDA (sm_86 x2, sm_120 x several, 4080 laptop). The sole ROCm/HIP report (gfx1151, aic0d3r) was RETRACTED by its author one day later as a DSpark draft-path/acceptance problem and 'not the quantized-verify bug'; nothing in the thread concerns gfx90a/CDNA or HIP on a discrete Instinct card.
   - anchor `comment #10 (aic0d3r, 2026-08-16) ROCm/gfx1151 retraction` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "Acceptance does **not** recover on the unquantized target — so the low acceptance is *not* the quantized-target verify drift this issue describes."
4. Root-cause consensus that emerged: this is a BATCH-INVARIANCE violation, not a quantization or drafter defect. Evidence: divergence already at width 2 (thc1006: 57/75 requests at n-max 1), drafter-independent (MTP and DFlash2 at three drafter quants fork at the same byte, snick525), flip position independent of width in one case (tczank), BF16 targets lossless in three reporters' small samples (Metal 10/10, Vulkan Strix Halo BF16 identical, one F16 LFM2 target) but the quantization-only reading is NOT established (Q8_0, Q6_K_XL and Q8_K_XL also diverge). A no-drafter batched-prefix-scoring control (msb-msb, CUDA) flips the same argmax in 2/6 cases.
   - anchor `comment #5 (dbsanfte, 2026-08-08)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "A kernel that does a batched/grouped decode for e.g. M=3 must be bitwise identical to the serial decode result (unbatched) for rows 1,2,3."
5. CUDA/HIP-relevant mechanisms and fixes (shared ggml-cuda source with our gfx90a build): thc1006 showed fork positions group by verification width {3,4} vs {5,6,8} matching mmvq.cu calc_nwarps (4 warps for ncols 1..4, 2 for 5..8) but his forced-warp intervention moved kernel time up to 26.68% and changed zero output bytes, so the warp table is NOT the cause for his Q4_K_XL/sm_86 setup; separately issue #28111 (open) shows MUL_MAT with IQ3_S/IQ4_XS is bitwise batch-variant at n=5 and n=8 on SM120 (n=1..4 pass) and forcing a fixed warp count restores invariance. Apoze's unmerged branch plus makoni's modification gives 15/15 answers matching control for Qwen3.8-27B UD-IQ3_S, KV q4_0, MTP n-max 1..3 (n-max above 3 not covered; DFlash2 n-max 7 outside patched range). PR #28108 (CUDA batch-invariant FA, SM120) was closed unmerged.
   - anchor `comment #18 (thc1006, 2026-08-26)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "The forced builds moved the kernel's runtime by up to **26.68%** and changed **not one output byte** in 150 records each."
   - anchor `PR #26358 comments (Ankk98, 2026-08-27)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR/issue API bodies+comments fetched 2026-10-20 (extra.txt sha256 551a941e...): "We can close this PR and if someone wants to pick this up, they can."
   - anchor `comment #29 (makoni, 2026-10-09)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "**Qwen3.8-27B, change 1 only, ctx 114688: 15/15 answers match control**"
   - anchor `issue #28111 body (SM120)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR/issue API bodies+comments fetched 2026-10-20 (extra.txt sha256 551a941e...): "On SM120, `mul_mat` with IQ quants is not batch-invariant for `n` of 5 and 8: evaluating `n` columns in one call gives a bitwise different result from `n` calls of one column."
   - anchor `comment #17 (thc1006, 2026-08-24)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "sm_86 is not RDNA, GCN, CDNA or DGX Spark, and the Turing table is gated on `arch >= TURING && arch < AMPERE`, so it falls through to `MMVQ_PARAMETERS_GENERIC`"
6. Per-GPU generalisation is weak: tczank found the #26358 FA gate had zero effect on RDNA3 (0/16 files differ), and frizikk found FA exonerated for his residual case - the dominant defect differs by backend, quant and prompt. Practical operator-side reports in the thread: sjvrensburg keeps speculation off for tool-calling endpoints (a 3-probe code set went 12/12 to 10/12 in a DFlash thinking-on test on Vulkan, and 8/8 to 7/8 in a separate n-max 3 parser test; very small samples); other reporters judge flips to be near-ties with coherent text.
   - anchor `comment #25 (tczank, 2026-09-24) RDNA3` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #25618 via GitHub API, updated_at 2026-10-18T19:11:45Z, 31 comments (comments.txt sha256 921ebb64...; issue.json sha256 48f4a58b...): "**The #26358 head has zero effect here**: outputs are bit-for-bit identical between master and master+fix"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b NEW source: read all 31 comments via the GitHub API (was 15 when 1288 was filed). Separated what the 'six-defect analysis' is (Vulkan-only, author-compiled, later 7) from CUDA findings (mmvq warp grouping, refuted as cause; IQ-quant invariance failure at n=5/8 on SM120; FA invariance on SM120); established fix status (no merged fix; #26358 and #28108 closed unmerged, #28489/#28488/#28111 open); established that no gfx90a/CDNA/discrete-ROCm data exists in the thread and that the one ROCm report was retracted. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: removed 'thc1006 repo BF16 9B diverging 36/75' from claim 4 (cross-contaminated from intake-2004#record; not in the #25618 files); fixed sjvrensburg test attribution in claim 6; claim_anchors re-indexed to the 7 corrected claims (claim 0 = issue metadata, unanchored). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/28489 - Initially declined at wave-3 close; overridden and ingested in wave 4 as intake-2045#record (Vulkan MI210 relevance). [wave3 decline revised].

### Verdict

Take away: upstream's own best diagnosis is that greedy speculative verify is lossless only if every kernel is batch-invariant (row 0 of an N>1 batch bit-identical to N=1), that the invariant is violated in many places that differ by backend/quant/prompt, and that NO fix is merged for Vulkan (PRs closed or open) or CUDA (branches/PRs unmerged or closed) as of 2026-10-18. This matches and strengthens our DF2-6/DF2-8 finding that the non-parity is the 1-row vs multi-row numeric split and not a DFlash2 defect; it also means no exact-greedy contract can be promised for any speculative arm on any backend, ours included. Do NOT carry: that 25618 settles HIP/gfx90a (zero gfx90a/CDNA data; the lone ROCm report was retracted); that the 'six defects' are CUDA/HIP defects (they are Vulkan shader paths); that ngram is a lossless control (it barely speculated); that quantization causes it (BF16 evidence is small-sample and contradicted); or that a fix exists. Treat intake-1288#record's phrasing 'four backends, two contributor reproductions' as partly stale: of the two CONTRIBUTOR reproductions one (aic0d3r, ROCm) was retracted; CUDA arrived later from non-contributors.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/qwen38-27b-replace-qwen36.md`

## intake-2022#record — LittleBit-2: Maximizing the Spectral Energy Gain in Sub-1-Bit LLMs via Latent Geometry Alignment

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00042
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: quantization, hardware_optimization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-1977#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. LittleBit-2 changes ONE thing relative to LittleBit: the initialisation. It inserts Joint-ITQ (an alternating sign / Procrustes rotation R of the concatenated SVD latent factors, 50 iterations, ~3 s extra per layer per Appendix F.1) between truncated SVD and Dual-SVID scale extraction. The deployed layer (two residual paths of h * U_b * l * V_b^T * g) is unchanged; BPW accounting is identical to LittleBit (M = 2r(din+dout+16) + 32(din+dout) bits per layer, blocks only; embeddings/LM head stay FP16).
   - anchor `§2.1 Extreme Quantization` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "Building on this protocol, our work specifically tackles the initialization bottleneck via geometric alignment."
   - anchor `§4.4 Joint-Iterative Latent Quantization` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "The process incurs negligible overhead, converging within 50 iterations with few seconds (Appendix F.1)."
   - anchor `repo README (SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0) intro` @ SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "LittleBit-2 initialization is available as an opt-in (`--use_itq`) and produces no additional inference overhead."
1. It is STILL quantisation-aware training with knowledge distillation, not PTQ: the paper says 'Initialize Phase' then 'QAT Phase', states it adopts LittleBit's identical training configuration, and the official repo trains per model (5 epochs, c4_wiki dataset, bs 4/GPU, lr 4e-5, multi-GPU DeepSpeed example). The paper reports no GPU-hours or token budget, and no pre-QAT (init-only) perplexity, so whether the rotated init alone is usable without training is not measured.
   - anchor `§5.2 Baselines` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "we adopted the identical training configuration (including the number of tokens, epochs, and learning rate) as detailed in (Lee et al., 2025)."
2. Quality gains over LittleBit at matched BPW (WikiText-2 ppl, Table 1 / Table 3): Llama-3 8B 1.0 bpp 16.30 -> 11.53 (OneBit 13.09; FP16 6.10), 0.55 bpp 18.47 -> 14.01, 0.1 bpp 26.11 -> 23.74; Llama-2 7B 1.0 bpp 9.08 -> 8.27 (OneBit 8.36; FP16 5.47), 0.55 bpp 10.47 -> 9.65, 0.1 bpp 15.92 -> 14.70 (about 2.7x FP16); Llama-2 13B 0.1 bpp 15.09 -> 13.94; Gemma-3 27B 0.1 bpp 17.72 -> 16.38 (FP16 4.58; zero-shot avg 47.06 vs 79.48 FP16). Zero-shot average at 0.1 bpp is 42.06 on Llama-2 7B, vs FP16 68.88: functional but far from usable. The only sub-1-bit PTQ baseline is STBLLM (Llama-2 7B 0.55 bpp ppl 32.93); no comparison to GGUF IQ/K-quants at their native 1.5-2.5 bpw is given.
   - anchor `§5.2 Performance Analysis` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "It achieves a perplexity of 23.74 on Llama-3 8B, outperforming the baseline LittleBit (PPL 26.11)."
   - anchor `Table 1 caption / §5.2` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "At 0.1 bpp, LittleBit-2 compresses body weights to <1%, leaving the footprint dominated by the fixed LM Head with Embedding."
3. Comparisons that look favourable are against QAT peers trained at equal budget (LittleBit, OneBit); the 2-bit PTQ baselines (GPTQ 52.2 ppl on Llama-2 7B; EfficientQAT 7.17 at 2.25 effective bpw with group 128) are listed with the group-scale overhead, and EfficientQAT at ~2.25 bpw beats LittleBit-2 at 1.0 bpp (7.17 vs 8.27) at a very different training cost. Training data (repo: c4_wiki) includes WikiText, so WikiText-2 ppl may not be held-out (the paper defers to Lee et al.; not verified).
4. Inference claims are inherited, not re-measured: the 11.6x kernel speedup (Llama-2 70B MLP layer, 0.288 -> 0.025 ms vs cuBLAS FP16) and a 2.46x end-to-end (82.6 -> 203.2 tok/s, 0.1 bpp Llama-2 7B, 128-token decode, batch 1) are quoted from Lee et al. 2025; 'it guarantees the same level of acceleration' is an assertion from shared graph structure. No kernel is released (repo: python/torch QAT + eval only; CC BY-NC 4.0 repo, CC BY-NC-ND paper - facts only).
   - anchor `§6.2 Inference Efficiency` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "Since LittleBit-2 shares this underlying computational graph, it guarantees the same level of acceleration."
   - anchor `Appendix H.3` @ arXiv:2603.00042v2 (html, 03 May 2026, ICML 2026) paper.html sha256 2567b925...; text paper.txt sha256 43af0fbd...; code SamsungLabs/LittleBit@42d658b0c79f76450b34b6a3547462c7cdc3e1a0: "The memory requirements for LittleBit-1 and LittleBit-2 are identical, governed by the Residual Low-Rank Binary Factorization."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b NEW source: read v2 HTML in full text (abstract, §3-§6, Algorithm 1, Tables 1-3, Appendix F.1/H.3/I start) and the official repo README. Settled the three wave-1 questions: not PTQ (QAT+KD, identical training config, repo trains per model), no cheaper-training claim (only a ~3 s/layer init overhead; no GPU budget or init-only ppl), no kernel release; quantified the quality delta vs LittleBit and OneBit at matched BPW. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed to the 5 corrected claims; claim 3 unanchored. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): LittleBit end-to-end 2.46x figure (arXiv 2506.13771) - Already in intake-1977#record; inherited, not re-measured. [wave3 declined].

### Verdict

Take away: LittleBit-2 does NOT change the intake-1977#record conclusion. It is a better initialisation (Joint-ITQ) for the same QAT+KD recipe, same deployed structure, same BPW accounting (blocks only, embeddings/LM head FP16), still no released inference kernel, still per-model training with no stated GPU budget, and no pre-QAT ppl reported. Quality at 0.1 bpp remains roughly 2.7x FP16 ppl on Llama-2 7B and 3.6x on Gemma-3 27B. Do NOT carry: 'sub-1-bit PTQ', the 11.6x/2.46x speed figures as measured for LittleBit-2 (inherited, A100 CUDA), or 0.1 bpp as whole-model size (embeddings dominate). The one transferable idea is the diagnostic, not the format: SVD latent factors are spiky (kurtosis ~16.8, max lambda 0.88) and a shared orthogonal rotation of U and V costs nothing at inference - an incoherence-processing observation in the QuIP#/QuaRot family, relevant only if someone wants a rotation-based preconditioner for existing low-bit lanes.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/tq3-quantization-evaluation.md`

## intake-2023#record — When Agents Slow Down: Understanding LLM Agents' Test-Time Strategies via Elo-per-token Analysis

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15309
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty high, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research, context_management)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2010#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Elo-per-token rates a system at a token checkpoint: for each task it takes the best-so-far score at each cumulative-token budget, forms pairwise win/tie/loss games between checkpoint-players within a task (including across sessions and systems), pools games across tasks and fits Bradley-Terry by MM, anchoring the lowest budget at 1000. Only within-task orderings enter, so score scales never mix. Self-Elo drops same-session games (prefix maxima decide those in advance).
   - anchor `Abstract; §3.2 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "We propose Elo-per-token analysis, which tracks the best solution found at each token budget and uses a Bradley–Terry model to aggregate within-task orderings into Elo ratings across tasks with different score scales."
1. Independent best-of-n sampling gains exactly 400 Elo per tenfold token increase (Theorem 3.1, i.i.d. continuous scores, equal attempt cost). This line is the reference; a session whose local slope is below 400/decade is converting tokens worse than fresh independent attempts would.
   - anchor `§3.3 Theorem 3.1 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "If attempts have equal token cost, independent sampling therefore gains 400 Elo for every tenfold increase in total token budget."
2. Measured slopes: several agent systems exceed 400/decade early, peak, then decline; pooled over 14 tasks every system is below 400 at the largest budgets. The curves keep rising (marginal gain, not absolute regression) and the comparison is slope-vs-reference, not 'long session scores lower than a restart' at equal tokens. The intro's 'after the first few context-window compactions' is an attribution in prose; the sections read contain no compaction ablation.
   - anchor `§4.2 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "In the pooled panel, every system falls below 400 by the largest budgets, and most benchmark-specific curves show the same late-stage decline at different budgets."
3. Models and budgets: Kimi Code/K2.7, Codex/GPT-5.5, Claude Code/Opus 4.8, Gemini CLI/Gemini 3.5 Flash; 100M tokens per session, cache-inclusive (input+output+cached), 5 sessions per agent-task cell, watchdog resumes early exits in the same context; tasks need dense intermediate scoring via an agent-callable submit script (App. C.1).
   - anchor `§4.1 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "Each agent system receives a target budget of 100M tokens on every task."
4. Splitting evidence is one task: on Polyomino Packing (Kimi K2.7) b_inf = 38M so K=3, giving +264 joint-Elo over one 100M session and +355 over ten 10M sessions; MLS-Bench is said to replicate (App. E, not read). 'Restart' here means a fresh independent session with no carried memory, and the best of K is returned.
   - anchor `§6.2 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "The predicted three-session allocation gains +264 joint-Elo over one 100M-token session and +355 over ten 10M-token sessions."
5. The paper's own limit: b_inf is task-specific and not predictable from the domain-level curve or early-session statistics (authors list this as future work), so a restart policy needs a per-target curve.
   - anchor `§7 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "Predicting this point from task properties or early-session statistics, without profiling the full task-level curve, remains an important direction for future work."
6. Humans on AtCoder keep improving after agents flatten; the time-aligned comparison rests on locally re-hosted evaluators (AHC014 plus three more) and, for pooled human curves, only contests whose crawled scores reproduce official standings.
   - anchor `§4.3 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "Human progress thus persists after agent progress saturates on the reproduced task."
7. Theorem 6.1 (asymptotic): if the post-inflection slope stays strictly below 400, frozen-state repeated sampling from the agent's state eventually beats continuing the session. Conditional on a slope assumption and i.i.d. continuations, not an empirical finding at every budget.
   - anchor `§6.1 Theorem 6.1 (ar5iv text; no page numbers)` @ arXiv:2609.15309v1 (ar5iv html): "If the original harness has asymptotic post-inflection slope strictly below sref, then frozen-state repeated sampling approaches slope sref and eventually yields a larger Elo gain than continuing the original session."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Narrowed the popular framing ('falls below independent sampling') to slope-below-reference; split result is one task; restart = memoryless fresh session; compaction attribution not tested in the sections read.

### Verdict

Take away: a per-target slope test (local Elo per decade of tokens vs 400) is a cheap diagnostic of whether continuing a long session still beats fresh attempts, and K=round(B/b_inf) is a testable restart policy. Do NOT carry 'long coding-agent sessions are worse than restarts' as a measured fact at equal tokens. The measured fact is a SLOPE falling below 400/decade after a peak (curves still rise); the one split experiment is a single task with one model; the restart baseline is memoryless, whereas an AK campaign with strategy-store carry-over is not that baseline. Compaction as the cause is asserted in prose, not isolated.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2024#record — stateless-language-agents (preprint-only repository for "Stateless Language Agents: Scaling Long-Horizon Automated Research", arXiv:2610.07625)

- **URL**: https://github.com/Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: autonomous_research, agent_architecture, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2010#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The repository is NOT the harness: one commit (1271183, 'Add SLA preprint', created 2026-10-06) and one tracked file, assets/sla-preprint.pdf. No README, LICENSE, code, prompt files, schema or tests. The evidence-summary builder, Landlock wrapper and journal schema are not released here.
   - anchor `git log -1 @ 1271183 (sole commit; sole tracked file assets/sla-preprint.pdf sha256 875cdf5e7cbe6229a4e435ad1df7fe0a45b87d41172fce81b9946b8bfe9fdcae)` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "[paper] Add SLA preprint"
1. What the preprint specifies: stateful search with stateless agents; the harness owns candidates and outcomes and rebuilds a fresh role-specific context for the Advisor each epoch and each Worker each trial. Defaults: W=3 Workers, 3 local trials per Advisor epoch (App. B.1).
   - anchor `assets/sla-preprint.pdf p.1 Abstract / §3 @ 1271183` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "instead, the harness owns the research state (candidate solutions and measured outcomes) and reconstructs a fresh and role-specific context for every invocation."
2. The Advisor's evidence summary is described only in prose (recent attempts, outcomes grouped by search direction, Worker status, recent progress, task diagnostics) and separates measured non-improvements from inconclusive implementation/runtime/correctness failures. No builder code or field list is given.
   - anchor `assets/sla-preprint.pdf p.5 §3.1 @ 1271183` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "It separates measured non-improvements from inconclusive failures in implementation, runtime, or correctness, so a failed execution is not mistaken for evidence that a direction is exhausted."
3. Isolation: Workers run as unprivileged processes under Linux Landlock with restricted network; the harness clears saved sessions between trials and checkpoints candidate snapshots, evaluation outputs and event logs. The mechanism is named, the implementation is not published.
   - anchor `assets/sla-preprint.pdf p.6 §3.3 @ 1271183` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "Workers run as unprivileged processes under Linux Landlock filesystem restrictions, with restricted network access, and the harness clears their saved sessions between trials."
4. Full Anthropic-kernel Advisor and Worker prompts (and FrontierSWE, SOL-ExecBench prompts) are reproduced in the preprint's Appendix E with {placeholders}; they are the reusable artifact. The Advisor prompt caps incremental lanes at one, treats errors as inconclusive, and says reset is not amnesia.
   - anchor `assets/sla-preprint.pdf p.24 App. E Anthropic Kernel Advisor Prompt @ 1271183` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "Use the sanitized ledger to avoid repeating mechanisms with strong negative evidence. Reset means fresh reasoning from measured state, not amnesia."
5. Headline result (3 Codex runs, Anthropic VLIW SIMD cycles task, not a GPU kernel): SLA reaches the strongest baseline's final result with 67.9M vs 986.3M tokens (93.1% fewer); rankings reverse with horizon on FrontierSWE; Advisor uses <0.6% of tokens.
   - anchor `assets/sla-preprint.pdf p.7 §4.2 @ 1271183` @ Alex-q-z/stateless-language-agents@1271183f6c73729d0c5e1602e12951fba59cf657: "On the Anthropic kernel task with Codex, SLA needs only 67.9M tokens, compared with 986.3M for SwarmResearch, a 93.1% reduction."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Repo holds only the preprint PDF; contradicts any assumption that the builder, prompts-as-files, Landlock code or journal schema are published.

### Verdict

The harness is NOT released: this repo is a PDF host. Do not plan to 'reuse the SLA code'. Reusable now: Appendix E prompt text and the §3 design (re-derivable). AK would write its own evidence-summary builder, journal-to-context projection and sandbox; AK already has an append-only journal with shards and replay, so the new work is the projection and per-invocation prompt assembly, not storage. Re-check the repo before building (created 2026-10-06, may gain code).
