## intake-2025#record — Beneath the Diff: Diagnosing and Mitigating Algorithmic Mode Collapse in Code-Level Autonomous Research Loops (arl-mode-collapse)

- **URL**: https://github.com/BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2011#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Surface diversity S_t = mean pairwise normalized Levenshtein distance (distance / max length, python-Levenshtein) over the last W=20 accepted unified diffs; input is diff text; 0.0 until two diffs exist; O(W^2) per update.
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/surface.py:L4-L4 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "window-mean of pairwise normalized Levenshtein distances over the last"
1. Semantic diversity C_t: each edit DESCRIPTION is embedded with sentence-transformers all-mpnet-base-v2 (normalized), ALL accumulated embeddings are re-clustered on each call with HDBSCAN (min_cluster_size=5, min_samples=2, euclidean), and C_t counts distinct non-noise clusters among the last W=20 edits. Fallbacks: sklearn DBSCAN(eps=0.4, cosine); a hash-seeded random embedder if sentence-transformers is absent (uses Python hash(), not reproducible across processes without PYTHONHASHSEED; docstring says not for paper numbers).
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/semantic.py:L66-L68 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "min_cluster_size=self.min_cluster_size, min_samples=self.min_samples, metric="euclidean","
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/embedder.py:L34-L34 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "seed = abs(hash(t)) % (2**32)"
2. Mechanism entropy H_t: an ordered first-match-wins regex parser assigns each description to one of 9 categories (Optimizer, Scheduling, Architecture, Data, Loss, Regularization, Numerical, Decoding, Other); H_t is Shannon entropy in nats over the last 20 labels. The taxonomy is ML-training specific and would send nearly every kernel edit to 'Other'. The docstring's kappa=0.84 on 200 edits and 187-edit pilot are not backed by any file in the repo.
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/mechanism.py:L86-L89 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "for cat, pat in _KEYWORD_RULES: if pat.search(description): return cat return "Other""
3. Faithfulness: gap = |m_opt_t - m_opt_0| - |m_ho_t - m_ho_0|; ratio rho = held-out gain / in-loop gain (0.0 when in-loop gain <= 1e-9). Gains are absolute values, so a held-out regression counts as 'gain' and direction is ignored (higher_is_better is stored but unused in these methods).
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/faithfulness.py:L57-L57 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "return self.in_loop_gain() - self.held_out_gain()"
   - anchor `arl/diagnostics/faithfulness.py:L65-L68 @ bb64b6f` @ BokwaiHo/arl-mode-collapse@bb64b6f76bd0d8dc10fb07e2fca22f6a3dad08b6: "denom = self.in_loop_gain() if denom <= 1e-9: return 0.0 return self.held_out_gain() / denom"
4. Inputs per accepted iteration: description string, unified diff string, category, in-loop metric, and a periodic held-out metric (HVG interval K=10); loop emits diagnostics.csv with columns iter,accepted,S_t,C_t,H_t,V_t,m_opt,m_ho,gap,ratio. Offline replay over AK history is feasible for S_t, C_t, H_t and faithfulness if per-candidate description + patch + screen and confirmed scores are retained. Provenance V_t needs a 1.1M-abstract FAISS index and returns 0.0 if absent (not useful for kernels).
5. Kernel use needs a replacement taxonomy for _KEYWORD_RULES (tiling/blocking, vectorization/ISA path, prefetch/cache, threading/NUMA, quant dequant path, fusion, launch config, layout, algorithmic) plus a human or LLM spot check. This is design inference, not a claim in the repo.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Instrument fully released and offline-capable; two caveats found in code (abs-gain faithfulness; unsupported kappa claim).

### Verdict

The instrument is small (~400 lines in arl/diagnostics), self-contained, and needs only strings (description, diff) plus two metric series, so it can be vendored or reimplemented offline over a journal. S_t and C_t are generic; the taxonomy and provenance corpus are ML-training-specific and must be replaced for kernels. Carry these caveats: faithfulness uses absolute gains (direction-blind); C_t re-clusters the whole history each call and depends on the embedding model; hash-embedder fallback is non-deterministic; kappa=0.84 is unsupported by repo data; no logs ship, so paper numbers cannot be re-derived here, only the instrument.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

## intake-2026#record — TLP: A Deep Learning-based Cost Model for Tensor Program Tuning

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03578
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2012#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TLP replaces hand-engineered statement features (Ansor: 164 dims) with features read straight off the schedule-primitive sequence (primitive type one-hot, numeric parameters unchanged, name parameters tokenized), fed to a self-attention + residual regressor trained with LambdaRank. Because the primitive sequence exists before any tensor program is generated, the score is computed BEFORE lowering and BEFORE any build; only the top-k are then generated and measured. The same extractor serves CPUs and GPUs.
   - anchor `§4.1 last paragraph` @ arXiv:2211.03578v2 (html): "In addition, TLP directly extracts features from schedule primitives, eliminating the need to generate tensor programs and reducing tuning time."
1. Accuracy (TenSet top-k scores, held-out ResNet-50/MobileNet-V2/ResNext-50/BERT-tiny/BERT-base): TLP beats TenSet-MLP on all five CPUs (incl. AMD EPYC 7452: top-1 0.9055 vs 0.8510) and is mixed on GPUs (K80 0.9059 vs 0.9083; T4 0.8847 vs 0.8757).
   - anchor `§6.1.4 after Table 5` @ arXiv:2211.03578v2 (html): "It can be found that on all CPUs, TLP exceeds TenSet MLP by a large margin. On GPUs, TLP and TenSet MLP each have their strengths."
2. Search-time: reaching the quality of TenSet-MLP after 2,000 trials takes 9.1x (CPU) / 3.0x (GPU) less time with TLP; vs Ansor 16.7x / 16.0x. About 1.7-1.8x of the per-trial wall-clock gain is simply cheaper feature extraction (no program generation). Measured on 5 networks, one laptop CPU and one T4 - not on any AMD GPU.
   - anchor `§6.3 paragraph on Figs 12-13` @ arXiv:2211.03578v2 (html): "Compared with TenSet MLP, TLP can speed up the search time by an average of 9.1× and 3.0× on the CPU and GPU. MTL-TLP can speed up the search time by an average of 4.7× and 2.9× on the CPU and GPU."
   - anchor `§6.3 execution-speed paragraph (second anchor for claim 2)` @ arXiv:2211.03578v2 (html): "In terms of execution speed alone, TLP is on average 1.7× and 1.8× faster than TenSet MLP on the CPU and GPU, respectively, because TLP extracts features from schedule primitives without generating tensor programs."
3. Cross-hardware: TLP-alone offline models do not transfer; MTL-TLP gets TenSet-MLP-or-better accuracy with 500K target records (about 7% of TenSet) plus another platform of the SAME ISA, 4.7x/2.9x search-time gains. Authors state 500K records still take tens of hours to collect. Different-ISA helpers help less (Table 9); CPU<->GPU not tried.
   - anchor `§8 Discussion/Limitation` @ arXiv:2211.03578v2 (html): "MTL-TLP trained with only 7% of the target platform data outperforms TenSet MLP with all data. However, it still takes tens of hours to collect 500K data."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. The abstract headline 9.1x/3.0x is vs TenSet MLP (not Ansor); vs Ansor it is 16.7x/16.0x. Both are time-to-baseline-quality, 5 networks, 1 CPU + 1 GPU. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: removed a TenSet finding misattributed to TLP from claim 1.

### Verdict

TLP shows a learned model can score candidates from the knob/primitive sequence alone, before lowering - the property AK needs. But it is trained on hundreds of thousands to millions of labelled programs per platform (TenSet 8.6M/platform; MTL-TLP needs 500K target records) and every number is on TVM/Ansor schedule primitives for dense NN subgraphs on Intel/AMD/ARM CPUs and NVIDIA GPUs. Do NOT carry 9.1x/16.7x to AK: those are time-to-quality gains in a measurement-bound tuner; AK iterations are build-bound (handoff-recorded 75.7% build / 3.9% measure, via W2 intake-2012#record) and AK has ~10^1-10^2 labelled candidates, not 10^5-10^6. Take the idea (featurize the knob vector, score pre-build, rank by top-k not RMSE) not the numbers.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2027#record — Pruner: A Draft-then-Verify Exploration Mechanism to Accelerate Tensor Program Tuning

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02361
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2012#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Pruner splits each tuning round into DRAFT and VERIFY. Draft: a genetic algorithm explores the Ansor schedule space scored by a Symbol-based Analyzer - a closed-form latency estimate L = sum_i(S8/Up + S5/Um), where compute/memory utilisation Up, Um are the product of hardware penalty terms (L0/L1/L2 allocation vs capacity, ceil-quantisation of blocks vs parallel units, memory-transaction alignment) computed from 8 symbols read off the schedule and a per-device limits file - and keeps the best 512. Verify: a learned transformer cost model (PaCM) scores only those 512 instead of ~8,000, and only its top picks are measured. The draft needs no training data and no on-device run, but is evaluated on the schedule expanded to buffer statements, so it is cheaper than the learned model, not free of lowering.
   - anchor `§4.1 Symbol-based Analyzer` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "Pruner proposes an empirical formula named Symbol-based Analyzer (SA) instead of using a deep learning-based cost model during exploration."
   - anchor `§6.3 Compilation Cost` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "reducing the evaluation set from 8,000 candidates to 512"
1. What the draft buys: on TenSet T4 the 512-candidate drafted set contains a near-best program (Best-1 0.995; 0.914 at 50 candidates); removing the compute-utilisation penalty drops Best-1 to 0.880 at 512, so the analytic terms (not just the GA) carry the quality.
2. Measured savings (time to match baseline 2,000-trial quality, 14 DNNs): 2.6x online vs Ansor (MoA-Pruner 4.82x), 4.75x vs TenSet-MLP offline, 4.05x vs TLP (A100 only, because TLP failed to produce a program on several workloads), 4.08x vs MetaSchedule on A100 TensorCore with 16x16x16 WMMA. Total compile time for the same 2,000 trials falls only to 84.1% (Pruner) / 75.3% (MoA-Pruner) of Ansor on TITAN V; on Orin (Table 1) an Ansor run is about 85-97 min of which 44-51 min is on-device measurement, untouched by Pruner; the saving is the exploration/learned-model share (~40% on Orin).
   - anchor `§6.3 Compilation Cost` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "The data shows that the average compilation time of Pruner and MoA-Pruner is 84.1% and 75.3% of Ansor’s, respectively."
   - anchor `§2.3 Opportunities, Table 1 sentence` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "illustrating space exploration with learned cost model’s time occupies nearly 40%"
   - anchor `§2.3 Opportunities item 2 (TLP failure report)` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "In our experiments (§6.1), the TLP model fine-tuned on the target platform occasionally crashed, leading to tuning failures and the disappearance of the tuning curve."
3. Hardware: only three NVIDIA GPUs (A100, Titan V, Jetson Orin-AGX); no AMD GPU and no CPU. Porting needs a device-abstraction entry (memory capacities, parallel-unit counts, transaction length) - repo shows `--psa a100_40` for the A100 abstraction (code skimmed, not run).
   - anchor `§5 Platforms and baselines` @ arXiv:2402.02361v3 (html): "We evaluate Pruner on three representative NVIDIA GPU platforms: A100, Titan V, and Jetson Orin-AGX."
4. Code: github.com/qiaolian9/Pruner @0760c3f39d84b31b4b10c4d7971ff402f2c350ae, Apache-2.0, a TVM fork targeted at CUDA/A100; paper link in abstract and repo README agree. The arXiv listing has three versions with different titles: v1/v2 "A Speculative Exploration Mechanism..." and v3 (ASPLOS 2025) "Draft-then-Verify".

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Stage-1 (wave-2) title "Speculative Exploration Mechanism" is the v1/v2 title; current v3 title is "Draft-then-Verify". The wall-clock saving is 16-25%, not the 2.6-4.8x headline (which is time-to-quality). Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: 84.1% is TITAN V only; replaced '~50 of ~85-125 min' with Table 1 Orin figures; re-indexed two anchors (8,000->512 -> claim 0; TLP-crash -> claim 2). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): Roller (Zhu et al. 2022) - No arXiv/venue ID observed; TileLang carver already covers the analytic-tile idea. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

Pruner is the closest published analogue to "cheap analytic pre-filter, then expensive verifier": a closed-form hardware-penalty model picks 512 of ~8,000 candidates with Best-1 0.995 and no training data. But its target bottleneck is learned-model INFERENCE inside search (~40% of an Ansor run), not BUILD time; total wall-clock gain is only 16-25%, and the large headline factors are time-to-quality vs weaker baselines. It is NVIDIA-GPU only, so the penalty terms (warp/SM quantisation, L0/L1/L2 limits) would need a gfx90a device abstraction (64-wide wavefronts, 104 CUs, 64KB LDS - inference, not from the paper). For AK, take the pattern (an analytic roofline/occupancy pre-screen that costs nothing before a build), not the speedup figures. Paper read: body sections 1-8 FULL; figures 6-15 are images (numbers from prose/tables); repo only listed.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2028#record — TenSet: A Large-scale Program Performance Dataset for Learned Tensor Compilers

- **URL**: https://github.com/tlc-pack/tenset
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2012#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TenSet (NeurIPS 2021 Datasets & Benchmarks) is a public offline dataset of 51,577,248 Ansor-generated (task, program, measured latency or error code) records: 120 network configs, 2,308 unique subgraphs on each of 6 platforms (13,848 tasks, at most 4,000 random programs per task). Hardware: Intel Platinum 8272CL, Intel E5-2673v4, AMD EPYC 7452 (a 4-core Azure VM, AVX-2), ARM Graviton2, NVIDIA Tesla K80 and T4. No AMD GPU, no AVX-512-free Zen4/5 CPU, no CDNA; floating-point dense networks only. The paper itself prints two different record totals (Table 1: 51,532,994; text and README: 51,577,248).
   - anchor `Abstract / §1` @ NeurIPS 2021 D&B paper PDF (cl.cam.ac.uk copy, sha256 ef5d0c158bd5f7a13ad2c79ddbfbd8fa386d4f41b2cffa4966d7f2faa3112490) + tlc-pack/tenset@35774ed373f9693cc6efcbe83f5703141e8ab0c8: "52 million program performance records collected from real measurements on Intel CPUs,AMD CPUs, ARM CPUs, and NVIDIA GPUs"
1. Effect of pre-training: a cost model trained offline on the SAME platform cuts search time to reach the Ansor-default result "by up to 10x" - read from Fig 6a this is about 10-11x on CPUs but only ~1.9-6.7x on the K80 GPU. The baseline (Ansor default) starts from random search and trains its model online, so the figure measures the value of ANY prior model, not of the architecture. Held-out status of the search networks is not stated in §5.4 (§5.2 holds out five networks for the accuracy test).
   - anchor `§5.4` @ NeurIPS 2021 D&B paper PDF (cl.cam.ac.uk copy, sha256 ef5d0c158bd5f7a13ad2c79ddbfbd8fa386d4f41b2cffa4966d7f2faa3112490) + tlc-pack/tenset@35774ed373f9693cc6efcbe83f5703141e8ab0c8: "reduces the search time by up to10"
2. Transfer: a model trained on one CPU transfers to other CPUs with degraded quality (Fig 6b); adding a local correction model fitted to 40 target trials per task gives 3-10% lower result latency (Table 5). Only CPU-to-CPU transfer was attempted; the authors expect little CPU-to-GPU benefit and did not test it.
   - anchor `§5.5 (transfer scope)` @ NeurIPS 2021 D&B paper PDF (cl.cam.ac.uk copy, sha256 ef5d0c158bd5f7a13ad2c79ddbfbd8fa386d4f41b2cffa4966d7f2faa3112490) + tlc-pack/tenset@35774ed373f9693cc6efcbe83f5703141e8ab0c8: "Note that we only do transfer learning across different types of CPUs."
3. Dataset-design lessons: at a fixed record budget, more distinct tasks beat more programs per task (120,000 records: 600x200 > 300x400 > 200x600); ranking-trained MLP beats XGBoost/LSTM regression; use top-k score rather than RMSE/R2 (ranking losses make R2 meaningless, e.g. -1818 for the model with the best final latency).
   - anchor `§5.3` @ NeurIPS 2021 D&B paper PDF (cl.cam.ac.uk copy, sha256 ef5d0c158bd5f7a13ad2c79ddbfbd8fa386d4f41b2cffa4966d7f2faa3112490) + tlc-pack/tenset@35774ed373f9693cc6efcbe83f5703141e8ab0c8: "For example, for a dataset of size 120000, (600 tasks, 200 programs per task)works better than (300 tasks, 400 programs per task) and (200 tasks, 600 programs per task)."
4. What an AK-history dataset would need to support the same: (1) per-candidate record with a stable task key (kernel + shape + dtype + quant type + device), the full knob/edit vector, and the outcome incl. build failure / crash / numerics-fail class (TenSet records error_no for failures), (2) many distinct tasks with a modest number of candidates each rather than one task heavily sampled, (3) same-device records for the pre-trained model (cross-device transfer degrades), (4) order of 1e5 records for the regime TenSet tested (its 120k-record experiments); handoff-recorded AK volume is 17 benchmarked candidates in the audited campaign (via W2 intake-2012#record; not judgeable here). Inference: AK is therefore roughly four orders of magnitude short today.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. "Up to 10x" holds only on CPUs; GPU gains 1.9-6.7x (Fig 6a labels, order inferred). Paper Table 1 and text disagree on record total. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: dropped README-license anchor (supports no claim); re-indexed the §5.3 anchor to claim 3; corrected actionable reasons (TenSet does include AVX-512 CPU data). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/zhaiyi000/tlp - Another Intel-CPU TVM dataset; no AMD/CDNA content. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

TenSet is the evidence that an offline corpus of (task, program, outcome) records lets a ranking MLP replace online model-building: ~10x on CPUs, 2-7x on the GPU, against a baseline that has no prior. It also tells us what the corpus must look like (many tasks, error codes kept, same-device). Do NOT read it as a transfer story to MI210: no CDNA or AMD GPU exists in the data, and CPU->GPU transfer was never tried. The only AMD part is a 4-core Zen2 VM. PDF extraction note: this dive used a hand-written zlib/content-stream extractor on the Cambridge copy of the paper (no appendix pages in that copy, so appendices A-C - network list, metric definitions, training details - are UNREAD).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2029#record — tile-ai/tilelang

- **URL**: https://github.com/tile-ai/tilelang
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance high; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1979#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. HIP/ROCm status per the tree: AMD ROCm/HIP is a "Supported" backend (CUDA is "Primary"); the README names CDNA and RDNA with "gfx942/gfx950 paths". CI runs the full ROCm test suite and four example tests only on a self-hosted gfx942 (MI300X) runner with ROCm 7.2; gfx950 is stated as not covered. No CI job runs on gfx90a (MI210/MI250).
   - anchor `README.md backend table, AMD ROCm/HIP row` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "CI runs on a self-hosted gfx942 (MI300X) runner; gfx950 is not yet covered."
   - anchor `.github/workflows/ci.yml:L93-L97 @ 876a59e` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "tags: [self-hosted, amd, gfx942]"
1. gfx90a is present in the tree as (non-exhaustive): a documented target example (`{"kind": "hip", "mcpu": "gfx90a"}`), a compile-only HIP codegen test, one of five arches in docker/Dockerfile.rocm PYTORCH_ROCM_ARCH ("gfx90a;gfx942;gfx950;gfx1201;gfx1100"), arch guards in the HIP templates (buffer-resource descriptor word, sdot4), a carver test (check_sm_version("gfx90a") == -1) and a gfx950 test docstring. It is never named as a tested runtime device. This narrows the wave-2 statement that the README names gfx942/gfx950 only: gfx90a is buildable-for and codegen-tested, but not runtime-tested.
   - anchor `testing/python/transform/test_tilelang_codegen_import_source.py:L29 @ 876a59e` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "{"kind": "hip", "mcpu": "gfx90a"},"
   - anchor `docker/Dockerfile.rocm:L20 @ 876a59e` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "ENV PYTORCH_ROCM_ARCH="gfx90a;gfx942;gfx950;gfx1201;gfx1100""
2. Gfx9 MFMA generator exists: tilelang/rocm/intrinsics/mfma_macro_generator.py (WARP_SIZE = 64) picks 16x16 MFMA shapes by operand width (k=4 for 32-bit, k=16 for 16-bit and non-gfx950 8-bit, k=32 for fp8/int8 shapes and gfx950), with an explicit CDNA2/3 branch for bf16 (`__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_16x16x16bf16_1k`) versus gfx950 (`..16x16x32_bf16`). 32x32 shapes appear only as an int8/gfx950 special case. fp8 MFMA suffixes (fp8_fp8, bf8_bf8) are generated; inference from AMD ISA knowledge, not the tree: MI210 has no fp8 MFMA, so only the f16/bf16(_1k)/f32/int8 16x16 paths are candidates there.
   - anchor `tilelang/rocm/intrinsics/mfma_macro_generator.py:L198 @ 876a59e` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "# CDNA2/3: __builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_16x16x16bf16_1k"
3. Could TileLang be an AK kernel-authoring backend for MI210? Tree-supported facts: kernels are JIT-compiled by the host hipcc (Installation.md), a Python DSL with an autotuner that enumerates a user-supplied config list, compiles candidates in a CPU worker pool and benchmarks on the device, and a Roller-style analytic hint generator (carver) with a CDNA arch class whose hard-coded constants (bandwidth [1300, 14000], reg_cap 32768, sm_partition 4) are not MI210 values. No learned cost model exists in tuner or carver. Inference (not in tree): (a) a TileLang AK path would author standalone HIP kernels, not ggml-hip kernels, so a ggml integration/numerics bridge would be new work; (b) its per-config hipcc compile replaces a full llama.cpp rebuild per candidate and therefore attacks AK's build share (75.7% of iteration, handoff-recorded via W2 intake-2012#record); (c) its LLVM CPU backend is "Experimental" and gives no AVX-512/VNNI quant-kernel route for EPYC; (d) no tree evidence on IQ2/IQ3 grid-decode support (a dequant GEMM example exists under examples/dequantize_gemm but was not read).
   - anchor `docs/get_started/Installation.md L40 @ 876a59e` @ tile-ai/tilelang@876a59e6eb8091ea608c14a84d5300190c3735ce: "Kernels are JIT-compiled with the host `hipcc`"
4. Cross-reference: the TileLang paper (arXiv:2504.17577, title confirmed this run from arxiv.org/abs: "TileLang: A Composable Tiled Programming Model for AI Systems") is not a standalone entry in the lane index; it appears only as a cited ID inside intake-966#record (lane line 60994, a prior-art citation for wave specialization) and as a referenced ID in intake-1226#record (line 101567). The sibling repo entry tilelang-puzzles is at index line 24992 with a deep dive. This repo entry supersedes neither; do not re-ingest the paper for MI210 questions because intake-966#record records the paper line of work has no CDNA2 position.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Refines wave-2 note: gfx90a is not absent from the tree (compile-only test, docker arch list, HIP template guards, docs example), but it is not runtime-tested; MI210/CDNA2 named only in a code comment. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim 1 'only as' overstated; added carver test and docstring mentions. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/tile-ai/tilelang/releases/tag/v0.1.13 - Release notes; the tree itself was read at HEAD. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

TileLang is the only candidate seen so far with a maintained MFMA code generator for gfx9 (WARP_SIZE 64, CDNA2/3 bf16_1k branch) and a Python authoring surface; but upstream runtime-tests only gfx942, and gfx90a is codegen-tested at most. Whether it works on an MI210 is UNKNOWN - the tree gives no evidence either way - and answering it needs one bounded compile-and-run probe on the MI210 (a plain 16-bit GEMM), which this dive was forbidden to perform. License: MIT (one line). Do not state "supports MI210".

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`

## intake-2030#record — Game-Time: Evaluating Temporal Dynamics in Spoken Language Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26388
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2013#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The judge protocol is text-only: dual-channel audio is transcribed to time-aligned text (Whisper-medium word timestamps) and a Gemini 2.5 Pro LLM scores instruction following / response appropriateness from the transcript; an audio-LLM judge was also reported effective but more costly.
   - anchor `§3.4 Dual-channel Evaluation` @ arXiv:2509.26388v4 (html): "we first transcribe the dual-channel audio (user and model) to obtain time-aligned text. This transcription is then provided to an LLM judge"
1. Human agreement is moderate and measured only on the Advanced (temporal) tasks: Spearman 0.677 / Pearson 0.675 for the text judge (audio-LLM judge 0.643 / 0.625), over 4 models x 35 scores, 20 samples per task, 3 Prolific raters each. No confidence interval is given.
   - anchor `§5.2 Human Evaluation` @ arXiv:2509.26388v4 (html): "Across 4 models × 35 data scores, we observe a reasonably high correlation (Spearman’s ρ=0.677, Pearson’s r=0.675) for our dual-channel evaluation method."
2. The paper contains NO turn-taking judge results or human-agreement numbers: it only asserts the method "can effectively evaluate other behaviors such as turn-taking" with results deferred to a project website (a JavaScript shell that could not be read from disk). So FDB-v2 borrows the method, not any validated turn-taking agreement figure from this paper.
   - anchor `§3.4 last paragraph` @ arXiv:2509.26388v4 (html): "this method can effectively evaluate other behaviors such as turn-taking. Due to space limitations, these additional results are available on our project website."
3. Local-judge substitution is untested: the paper says Gemini 2.5 Pro was chosen after a preliminary study as "superior to other open-source and commercial models" but shows no comparison numbers. Because the judge input is plain transcript text, a local LLM can in principle be given the same prompt, but it must be validated against human ratings before use.
   - anchor `§4 Dual-channel Evaluation` @ arXiv:2509.26388v4 (html): "we selected Gemini 2.5 Pro as the LLM judge, which we found superior to other open-source and commercial models"
4. Benchmark scale: 1,475 spoken-instruction instances (700 Basic, 775 Advanced) synthesised with CosyVoice TTS; it tests explicit time/tempo/simultaneous-speech instruction following, not endpointing latency.
   - anchor `§3.3 end` @ arXiv:2509.26388v4 (html): "In the end we have a total of 1,475 test instances"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Narrowed the FDB-v2 linkage: the paper provides no turn-taking agreement numbers; its 0.677 is for temporal instruction tasks. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: audio-LLM judge was 'also effective but more costly', not rejected. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://ga642381.github.io/Game-Time - JS-only site; content not readable from disk. [wave4 declined]; arXiv:2506.05984 - Audio-LLM judge protocol; FDB-v2's judge is text-only so it does not change the local-judge conclusion. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

Game-Time settles the judge FDB-v2 follows: a text-only Gemini 2.5 Pro judge over Whisper-timestamped transcripts, human agreement r~0.68 (moderate, 4 systems, Advanced tasks only). It does NOT validate a turn-taking judge, and says nothing about any local judge. Do not carry "Game-Time shows LLM judges are reliable for turn-taking" - the turn-taking evidence is off-paper. A local judge is plausible mechanically (text input) but must clear a stated agreement bar against human ratings on our own sessions before it gates anything.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2031#record — TurnBench: A Multi-Domain Benchmark for Turn-Taking Dynamics in Spoken Dialogue

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25218
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty high, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2016#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. English only, studio-recorded, dyadic: 30 h / 154 dialogues / 106 voice actors across six conversation types, triple-annotated (Fleiss kappa 0.78). Conversation TYPE is the controlled variable; there are NO paired / same-words / context-controlled stimuli, so this does not fill the ECHO "same words, different actions" gap.
   - anchor `§VIII Conclusion` @ arXiv:2608.25218v2 (html): "All dialogues are English, studio-recorded, and dyadic."
1. Two tracks are scored with causal commit timestamps: End-of-Turn and Interruption. A mid-turn pause fired on counts as an EOT false positive; a backchannel (or non-content event) fired on counts as an INT false positive; non-floor-taking interruption attempts are excluded rather than negatives. Metrics are recall, FPR and signed latency (10/50/90th pct); the leaderboard ranks by test recall subject to FPR <= 0.15.
   - anchor `§V-C Submission and scoring` @ arXiv:2608.25218v2 (html): "Inside a negative span, firing counts as at most one false positive and not firing counts as one true negative."
2. Best open systems: VAP EOT 0.845 recall / 0.055 FPR / 368 ms and INT 0.945 / 0.107 / 994 ms; SmartTurn v3 (ONNX) EOT 0.752 / 0.047 / 1017 ms but INT recall only 0.107. Acoustic-only baselines have high false-positive rates (e.g. RMS VAD FPR .632 overall, 0.51-0.70 per conversation type). Interruption false positives are type-dependent and highest in casual (backchannel-dense) talk. The INT 0.107 FPR for VAP exceeds the stated 0.1 dev budget though the text calls it in-budget (test vs dev).
   - anchor `§I contributions` @ arXiv:2608.25218v2 (html): "The strongest system, VAP, reaches 0.845 end-of-turn recall and 0.945 interruption recall at median latencies of 368 ms and 994 ms."
3. No system matches humans: human listeners start a floor transfer a median 151 ms before the turn ends (281 ms including interruptions); the fast INT systems (SmartTurn 159 ms, ESPnet 210 ms, OpenAI) pay in FPR or recall; accurate ones wait about 1 s.
   - anchor `abstract` @ arXiv:2608.25218v2 (html): "no current system performs equivalently without incurring excessive false positives"
4. Cascade-compatible scoring: a submission is a JSON of per-speaker eot/interruption timestamps from ANY system, with lookahead folded into the timestamp; scorer is MIT and runs locally on the public dev split (38 dialogues, labelled, gated HF, non-commercial data licence); test split is audio-only (116 dialogues, leaderboard submission). Full-duplex models are scored on EOT only, by detecting model speech onset with pyannote VAD, so their latency includes generation time.
   - anchor `docs/SUBMISSION_FORMAT.md "Causality is a term of submission"` @ SesameAILabs/turnbench@38a6f874322430cb3ca71d8a52aa1e636e88bad8: "Lookahead is simply latency you pay, as in deployment."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected expansion framing (intake-2016#record): not paired/same-words; conversation-type-controlled. Confirmed cascade scoring is by-design. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: RMS VAD statement restated from the table (FPR .632 overall).

### Verdict

TurnBench is the first English, human-annotated, multi-register benchmark we have found that scores a cascade endpointer directly (timestamps in, recall/FPR/latency out) and penalises false cuts on mid-turn pauses and false barge-ins on backchannels - exactly CS-22 and CS-23. It is NOT a paired same-words benchmark (ECHO remains the closest) and it does not score full-duplex interruption. Carry the Table IV numbers as reference points for English endpointers (SmartTurn v3, VAP, Kyutai SVAD), not as our own results. Data is gated; the dev split is enough to score an arm.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2032#record — Full-Duplex Interaction in Spoken Dialogue Systems: A Comprehensive Study from the ICASSP 2026 HumDial Challenge

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21406
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2016#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Challenge tasks: Interruption (follow-up question, negation, repetition request, topic switch, silence/stop) and Rejection (user backchannel, pause handling, third-party speech, speech directed to others). The paper text says "eight scenarios" but Table 1 and the repo list nine sub-scenarios. Data: >100 h dual-channel, Chinese and English, actor-recorded from LLM scripts; test zip is on HF (not downloaded).
   - anchor `§3.1 Scenarios` @ arXiv:2604.21406v2 (html): "the released dataset includes two major categories: Interruption and Rejection, with a total of eight scenarios."
1. This is NOT an independent study: the authors are affiliated with ASLP@NPU (inferred to be the organisers and the Easy Turn group; not stated), the reference baseline is their own Easy-Turn + OSUM-EChat, and Table 2 is the organisers' leaderboard. Its in-text citation for Easy-Turn ([17]) actually points to the Badcat team paper (arXiv 2601.20230).
   - anchor `§5.1 Model Evaluation` @ arXiv:2604.21406v2 (html): "To ensure consistent evaluation across systems, we use a baseline that integrates Easy-Turn [17] with Osum-EChat [5]."
2. Results (organiser leaderboard): best final score Cookie asr 76.6 (Int 79.3, Rej 72.2, 1.26 s first-response delay); best interruption Badcat 89.7 but Rej only 57.8; Gemini-2.5 Int 79.8 but Rej 36.5. Reading the table (main's inference, not paper text - the paper itself wrongly names Freeze-Omni as rejection leader at 50.2), rejection of non-interruptive input is the weak axis for nearly all systems; per-scenario breakdown is not in the paper text, so interruption-vs-backchannel cannot be separated from these tables.
   - anchor `README.md leaderboard table` @ ASLP-lab/HumDial-FDBench@f51d8ebfeac5461c5df1b38ed99882e33a6861c1: "| Cookie asr | 79.3 | 72.2 | 1.260 | 79.9 | 76.6 | 1 |"
3. Architectures: most submissions are cascaded or semi-cascaded (VAD + streaming ASR + decision module + TTS); top-2 are cascade (cookie_asr) and semi-cascaded Qwen3-Omni decision-token (Badcat). The team literally named "Cascade" scored 37.7, so architecture alone does not predict rank. Specialised small classifiers (Whisper-tiny 4-class, TinyBERT) are reported as robust front filters. Which rows are open-source/open-weights is encoded only by table colours that did not survive extraction, so it is UNKNOWN from disk.
   - anchor `§6.1 System Architecture` @ arXiv:2604.21406v2 (html): "Most submissions adopt cascaded or semi-cascaded pipelines."
4. Scoring depends on a cloud-grade judge: responses are classified Respond/Resume/Uncertain/Unknown by DeepSeek-V3 on Parakeet-TDT (English) / Paraformer (Chinese) transcripts, following FDB v1.5; latency uses Silero-VAD. Same local-judge validation issue as FDB v1.5.
   - anchor `§4.1 Behavior evaluation` @ arXiv:2604.21406v2 (html): "DeepSeek-V3 [12] is employed to categorize the model responses using carefully designed prompts."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected "independent results": none - organiser paper with own-lab baseline. Flagged 8-vs-9 scenario discrepancy and mis-cited Easy-Turn reference. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: organiser/Easy-Turn-group identity and 'rejection is the weak axis' marked as inference.

### Verdict

HumDial is an organiser-run Chinese+English interruption/rejection benchmark whose top systems are cascades, which supports (but is not independent evidence for) the cascade-first plan. Its useful content is the scenario taxonomy (backchannel / pause / third-party / side-speech rejection) and the lesson that rejection, not interruption, is where systems fail. Do NOT cite the final scores as comparable across languages, and do not rely on it for backchannel-vs-interrupt separation (no per-scenario table in the paper). Open-weights status of the winners is unknown here.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2033#record — Easy Turn: Integrating Acoustic and Linguistic Modalities for Robust Turn-Taking in Full-Duplex Spoken Dialogue Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23938
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2016#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Architecture: Whisper-Medium audio encoder (2 conv + 24 transformer layers) + adaptor (3 conv + 4 transformer) + Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct; it emits the ASR transcript followed by a turn-state tag. Size is about 0.85-0.86 B parameters (inference from the table's "850" under an "MB" header; checkpoint byte size not in the saved files).
   - anchor `§3.1 Model Architecture` @ arXiv:2509.23938v1 (html): "we adopt the Whisper-Medium model as the audio encoder, which includes two 1D convolutional layers and 24 Transformer layers."
1. Four states: complete, incomplete, backchannel ("brief feedback"), wait (explicit request to pause/stop). It classifies an utterance segment; it is not a streaming endpointer and the paper gives no streaming or chunked-latency analysis.
   - anchor `§2.1 Turn States` @ arXiv:2509.23938v1 (html): "Backchannel In natural conversations, backchannel signals refer to brief listener responses"
2. Accuracy (authors' own 800-sample testset, half TTS-synthetic): Easy Turn 96.33 / 97.67 / 91 / 98 vs TEN Turn Detection+Paraformer 86.67 / 89.3 / - / 91 and Smart Turn V2 78.67 / 62 / - / -. Latency 263 ms (4090) vs 204 ms and 27 ms. No Parakeet, Silero or silence-threshold baseline, no confidence intervals, no false-cut rate, no independent reproduction found.
   - anchor `README.md Main Results table` @ ASLP-lab/Easy-Turn@fe175eaa8442c9a0834acf29f8640ee74c2aceca: "| **Easy Turn (Proposed)** | 850 | 263 | 2559 | **96.33** | **97.67** | **91** | **98** |"
3. Language: all inspected material is Mandarin. Training data are MagicData-RAMC, Aishell/WenetSpeech ASR alignment and Chinese-text synthetic sets; the test list inspected has lang <CN>; prompts are Chinese. HF tags list en+zh, but the paper reports no English result, so English accuracy is unknown.
   - anchor `§4.1 Experimental Setup` @ arXiv:2509.23938v1 (html): "alignment training on the ASR task with 23,000 hours of data, including the Aishell1"
4. Ablation: fusing acoustic and linguistic via ASR+turn-detection gives ACCavg 95.75 against 85.5 (acoustic-only) and 86.25 (text-only), and 87.88 without the transcript step.
   - anchor `§4.3 Ablation` @ arXiv:2509.23938v1 (html): "Finetuned Whisper + Linear refers to fine-tuning Whisper-Medium audio encoder"
5. Release: model (HF ASLP-lab/Easy-Turn, Apache-2.0), WeNet-based training and inference code, 1,145 h trainset, testset. No ONNX/GGUF/CPU path; inference scripts take a GPU id.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Language narrowed to Mandarin (paper and test list), "850 MB" read as ~0.86 B params. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: removed unsaved checkpoint byte count; swapped anchors/verdicts for claims 3 and 4 (language vs ablation). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/ten-framework/ten-turn-detection - 7B text-only labeller/baseline; not a CPU-cheap CS-22 arm. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

Easy Turn is a heavy (about 0.86 B, GPU-run, Mandarin-trained) utterance-level turn-state classifier whose comparison is on the authors' own half-synthetic Mandarin testset; it does not beat or even test the baselines that matter to us (Silero+silence, Parakeet-EOU). It is NOT a ready CS-22 arm: no English evidence, no streaming, no CPU path. Carry the four-state taxonomy (esp. backchannel / wait, which map to CS-23) and the data-construction recipe; do not carry the accuracy table as evidence about English turn-end quality.

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2034#record — Full-Duplex-Bench: A Benchmark Suite for Evaluating Full-Duplex Spoken Dialogue Models and Systems

- **URL**: https://github.com/DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2003#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Cascade contract (v1/v1.5): a system under test must produce, per stimulus folder, a time-synchronous output.wav of the same timeline as the supplied input.wav; scoring then needs NeMo Parakeet-TDT word-timestamp ASR (get_transcript/asr.py -> output.json {text, chunks:[{text,timestamp:[s,e]}]}), plus clean_input/clean_output (+ their .json) for v1.5 adaptation analyses. Nothing in the scorers needs the system to be a speech-to-speech model.
   - anchor `v1_v1.5/README.md:L133-L133 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "The goal of model inference is to let the model generate the time-synchronous `output.wav` given the audio stream of user speech (`input.wav`)."
1. Fully local (no keys): v1 pause_handling, smooth_turn_taking and backchannel scorers (pure JSON + Silero VAD + repo-shipped icc_gt_distribution.json), v1.5 get_timing.py (stop/response latency intervals from Silero VAD over input.wav and output.wav; parameters USER_MERGE_GAP 0.6 s, MODEL_MERGE_GAP 0.5 s, whose inline comments say 300 ms / 50 ms and disagree with the values), and general_before_after/significance_test (WPM, pitch, intensity, UTMOSv2 + paired t-tests; first run downloads Silero via torch.hub and UTMOSv2 weights, no key). Hard network/key coupling is therefore only first-run weight fetches.
   - anchor `v1_v1.5/evaluation/get_timing.py:L5-L6 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "Writes a **`latency_intervals.json`** file inside every folder that contains `input.wav` + `output.wav`:"
2. Cloud-judge coupled (OpenAI client built with no base_url, model names hardcoded): v1.5 `behavior` task (gpt-4o-2024-08-06, retries forever on any exception), v1 `user_interruption` rating (gpt-4-turbo, plus a client.models.list() probe at startup in evaluate.py). Because the SDK client is constructed with defaults, redirecting to a local OpenAI-compatible endpoint is a base-URL environment variable plus serving a model under the hardcoded name (inference from SDK default behaviour; not exercised here). A local judge breaks comparability with published numbers. asr.py hard-codes `.cuda()` on the NeMo model: it must be patched for CPU or run under a ROCm torch build (not verified).
   - anchor `v1_v1.5/evaluation/eval_behavior.py:L54-L54 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "MODEL_NAME = "gpt-4o-2024-08-06""
   - anchor `v1_v1.5/evaluation/eval_user_interruption.py:L32-L32 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "MODEL_NAME = "gpt-4-turbo""
   - anchor `v1_v1.5/get_transcript/asr.py:L22-L23 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "model_name="nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2" ).cuda()"
3. v3 split: the three evaluators (evaluate_tool_calls.py, evaluate_pass_rate.py, analyze_tool_latency.py) read only result_{provider}.json files plus benchmark_data_v2.json (in repo) and do not touch LiveKit (README). --provider is a free-form string, so a custom 'cascade_epyc' provider needs no code change. Required result fields: example_id, actual_tool_calls [{function,args,timestamp_start,timestamp_end} relative to stream start], transcript, asr_chunks (word timestamps), user_speech_end_rel, audio_agent_speech_start. Without --use-llm, argument matching falls back to exact match and response quality is skipped; analyze_tool_latency.py always constructs an OpenAI client from OPENAI_API_KEY (a dummy key suffices) and uses gpt-4o only for the task-completion latency (key-information sentence), so first-response and tool-call latency are judge-free.
   - anchor `v3/README.md:L99-L99 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "The evaluation scripts (Step 3 below) do **not** require LiveKit."
   - anchor `v3/evaluate_tool_calls.py:L69-L69 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "_openai_client = OpenAI()"
   - anchor `v3/analyze_tool_latency.py:L271-L271 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "client = OpenAI(api_key=os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"))"
4. v3 inference harness is the cloud-coupled part: run_tool_benchmark.py streams input.wav into a LiveKit room (LIVEKIT_URL/KEY/SECRET) where cascaded_agent.py (Silero VAD min_silence 0.55 s, openai.STT whisper-1, openai.LLM gpt-4o, openai.TTS tts-1, min_endpointing_delay 0.5 s) runs, logging tool calls to /tmp/agent_tool_calls.log and latency JSON to /tmp/agent_heartbeat.log. The cascade reference baseline is thus an all-OpenAI-API cascade; an EPYC cascade replaces the harness (local audio feeder + whisper :9000 / LLM / qwentts :9002 + our own tool-call logger) and writes the same result JSON. The 100 v3 audio files (48 kHz human recordings) and the v1/v1.5 audio are NOT in the repo (Google Drive links only); only benchmark_data_v2.json and the mock tool APIs (mock_apis.py, latency_injector.py) are. Repo also commits node_modules under v1_v1.5/model_inference/gpt-realtime. License CC BY-NC 4.0 (fact only).
   - anchor `v3/run_tool_benchmark.py:L455-L455 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "result["actual_tool_calls"] = actual_tool_calls"
   - anchor `v3/cascaded_agent.py:L326-L327 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "llm_model = openai.LLM( model="gpt-4o","
   - anchor `v3/livekit_inference.py:L17-L17 @ 3e799c4` @ DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench@3e799c45a045256f47d5f1c9cda90157e2d2ec9e: "LIVEKIT_URL – wss://your-project.livekit.cloud"
5. gpt-realtime examiner / gemini judge scope: the v2 folder (WebRTC Node.js orchestrator with an automated AI examiner) and the gpt-realtime/gemini model_inference scripts are cloud-only and were not read in depth; they are irrelevant to scoring a local cascade on v1/v1.5/v3.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Settled from code that scorers are local except named judge calls; found asr.py is CUDA-hardcoded and that get_timing.py inline comments contradict its constants. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1DtoxMVO9_Y_nDs2peZtx3pw-U2qYgpd3 - Dataset download, not a source to dive; belongs to execution. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

Treat Full-Duplex-Bench as an offline file-in/file-out scorer: a local cascade is scorable on v1/v1.5 (overlap, stop/response latency) and v3 (tool-call F1/pass-rate/latency) by producing output.wav + word-aligned JSON + a tool-call log, with only the GPT-4o behavior classifier, the gpt-4-turbo interruption rating and the v3 --use-llm judges needing an LLM endpoint. Do NOT carry: that the repo ships audio data (it does not; Google Drive), that LiveKit Cloud is needed for scoring (it is needed only for the reference inference harness), or that a local judge reproduces published numbers. Never carry a claim of having run anything: nothing was executed.

## intake-2035#record — NeMo Voice Agent (examples/voice_agent, NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech)

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech/tree/main/examples/voice_agent
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2017#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The examples/voice_agent tree is marked deprecated in its own README in favour of NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent (a repo that exists, pushed 2026-10-20 per the GitHub API; not read). The actual logic lives under nemo/agents/voice_agent (pipecat services), which the example imports; the sparse checkout covered both paths.
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/README.md:L4-L4 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "> `nemo/agents/voice_agent` and `examples/voice_agent` in this repository are deprecated."
1. Pipeline order (server.py): websocket transport input (Silero VAD via pipecat, can_create_user_frames=False so the transport never interrupts by itself) -> RTVI -> STT -> [diar] -> NeMoTurnTakingService -> user aggregator -> LLM -> TTS -> websocket output; PipelineParams(allow_interruptions=True). Turn-taking is therefore a single stateful processor sitting between ASR and LLM that alone decides when StartInterruptionFrame is emitted.
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/server/server.py:L320-L320 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "allow_interruptions=True,"
2. VAD defaults (default.yaml): Silero confidence 0.6, start_secs 0.1, stop_secs 1.2, min_volume 0.4. The 1.2 s silence is the fallback end-of-turn; the in-band <EOU> token ends the turn earlier when the ASR emits it. README says a dedicated turn-taking model is not released, so turn-taking is VAD + EOU-token heuristics.
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/server/server_configs/default.yaml:L15-L15 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "stop_secs: 1.2 # min amount of silence to trigger UserStopSpeaking"
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/README.md:L226-L226 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "As the new turn-taking prediction model is not yet released, we use the VAD-based turn-taking prediction for now."
3. Chunking: audio arrives in 16 ms websocket frames; stt.py batches buffer_size=5 frames = 80 ms per model call; streaming_asr uses att_context_size [70,1] and chunk_size_in_secs 0.08 (the config echoes frame_len 0.08). On <EOU> or <EOB> in the decoded text the service resets audio buffer, encoder caches and hypotheses (reset_state), so each utterance is decoded from a fresh state.
   - anchor `nemo/agents/voice_agent/pipecat/services/nemo/stt.py:L66-L66 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "buffer_size: Optional[int] = 5 # number of audio frames to buffer, 1 frame is 16ms"
   - anchor `nemo/agents/voice_agent/pipecat/services/nemo/streaming_asr.py:L299-L299 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "self.reset_state(stream_id=stream_id)"
4. Barge-in/backchannel logic (turn_taking.py): while VAD says the user is speaking, tokens are accumulated; on <EOU> the completed text is pushed and UserStopped emitted, unless the bot is speaking and the text matches the 78-line backchannel_phrases.yaml (then it is routed upstream as '(text)' and the bot is NOT interrupted); a trailing <EOB> while the bot speaks is also treated as backchannel. If no backchannel list is configured, VAD start immediately interrupts. Otherwise, interim text with at least max_buffer_size=2 completed words (code: len(completed_words) >= max_buffer_size) (the last word is assumed unfinished) triggers UserStartedSpeaking + StartInterruptionFrame before EOU. bot_stop_delay=0.5 s keeps the bot flagged as speaking after BotStopped to cover client-side playout lag.
   - anchor `nemo/agents/voice_agent/pipecat/services/nemo/turn_taking.py:L206-L206 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "has_eou = self._user_speaking_buffer.endswith(self.eou_string)"
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/server/server_configs/default.yaml:L35-L35 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "max_buffer_size: 2 # num of words more than this amount will interrupt the LLM immediately if not backchannel phrases"
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/server/server_configs/default.yaml:L36-L36 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "bot_stop_delay: 0.5 # a delay in seconds allowed between server and client audio output"
5. Hardware: README requires at least one GPU (21 GB VRAM for 9B LLMs); every default config sets device cuda; TTS Kokoro-82M default; environment.yaml pins onnx 1.19.0 / onnxruntime 1.23.0 but no ONNX path is used in the voice agent code (grep: only environment.yaml hits). Nothing in the turn-taking, backchannel or buffering logic is GPU-specific; only the NeMo STT/diar/TTS model calls are.
   - anchor `examples/voice_agent/README.md:L61-L61 @ 6f55840` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech@6f558408dbe1c2a7e6b730184e8d46e1474d6dca: "- A computer with at least one GPU. At least 21GB VRAM is recommended for using 9B LLMs, and 13GB VRAM for 4B LLMs."
6. Portable to our cascade: (a) the turn_taking.py state machine, reimplementable without pipecat in ~150 lines around whisper/Silero events; (b) backchannel phrase list and the 'ignore backchannel while bot speaking' rule for CS-23; (c) max_buffer_size word-count early interrupt; (d) bot_stop_delay compensation; (e) the reset-on-EOU decoding discipline if an EOU ASR arm is added (CS-22). Not portable without work: NeMo cache-aware streaming runtime on ROCm (unverified), diarization, pipecat transport.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Discovered the example is deprecated and logic is under nemo/agents/voice_agent; no benchmark in repo. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: interrupt threshold is >= 2 completed words, not > 2.

### Verdict

Take the control-flow design (EOU token OR VAD stop_secs ends turn; backchannel phrases suppress barge-in only while the bot is speaking; >=2 completed words interrupt early; 80 ms ASR chunks; full state reset after each EOU) as a reference spec for CS-22/CS-23. Do NOT carry: any latency or false-cut figure (the repo has none), the idea that the stack is GPU-independent end to end, or that the example path is maintained (deprecated, successor repo labs-Voice-Agent unread).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2036#record — Parakeet Realtime EOU 120M v1 ONNX

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2017#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Exported: two graphs, encoder-model.onnx and decoder_joint-model.onnx (the decoder and joint are fused into one graph), each in fp32, fp16 and int8 (six .onnx files), plus vocab.txt and config.json (per-file byte sizes from the HF tree API were not saved to disk; repo usedStorage 858,328,106 bytes).
1. Token-exactness claim is narrow: the card says fp32, fp16 and decoder-only int8 give exact token/text/raw-text parity and encoder-only int8 and full int8/int8 do NOT, measured on one unnamed 'smoke fixture'. Parity is stated against NeMo's output implicitly (the card never names the reference run). Recommended pairings are fp32/fp32, fp16/fp16 or fp32 encoder + int8 decoder, so the CPU-fast int8 encoder is explicitly not recommended.
   - anchor `README.md:L105-L105 @ 6d6be8e` @ HF ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx@6d6be8e9113b4aa8ae7b4d5dfb655795c084d0c6: "- encoder-only INT8: not exact"
   - anchor `README.md:L104-L104 @ 6d6be8e` @ HF ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx@6d6be8e9113b4aa8ae7b4d5dfb655795c084d0c6: "- decoder-only INT8: exact token/text/raw-text parity"
2. Frontend contract (config.json + card): 16 kHz mono, 128 mel bins, 10 ms frame shift, subsampling 8, centered valid-length mode, raw log-mel with NO per-feature normalisation (the card warns this differs from the nemo128 contract). The mel frontend is NOT in the ONNX package: it ships as a JavaScript frontend inside @asrjs/speech-recognition, so a Python/C++ consumer must reimplement the NeMo log-mel front end. config.json ids: vocab_size 1026, blank_id 1026, eou_id 1024, eob_id 1025, pred_hidden 640, pred_layers 1, max_symbols_per_step 10.
   - anchor `README.md:L89-L89 @ 6d6be8e` @ HF ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx@6d6be8e9113b4aa8ae7b4d5dfb655795c084d0c6: "This matters: this model does **not** use the normalized `nemo128` frontend contract reused by some other NeMo exports."
   - anchor `config.json:L9-L9 @ 6d6be8e` @ HF ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx@6d6be8e9113b4aa8ae7b4d5dfb655795c084d0c6: ""eou_id": 1024"
   - anchor `README.md:L79-L79 @ 6d6be8e` @ HF ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx@6d6be8e9113b4aa8ae7b4d5dfb655795c084d0c6: "For `@asrjs/speech-recognition`, this ONNX package is intended to run with the shared in-repo JavaScript NeMo frontend."
3. The card does NOT document the encoder's cache inputs/outputs (cache_last_channel, cache_last_time, lengths), the chunk size, or the pre-encode cache; those must be read from the ONNX graph signatures at execution time. The card contains no latency or WER figure; its only usage examples are JavaScript/WASM via asrjs. Independent third-party consumers confirm the model is runnable under onnxruntime: soniqo/Parakeet-EOU-120M-ONNX-INT8 (3 graphs, 320 ms chunks of 64 mel frames, preCacheSize 9, outputFrames 4, int8 encoder only) and altunenes/parakeet-rs (Rust, 160 ms chunks of 2560 samples). These are restatement-grade for correctness: neither is validated against NeMo in what was read.
   - anchor `soniqo_README.md:L62-L62 @ d09ec62` @ HF soniqo/Parakeet-EOU-120M-ONNX-INT8@d09ec62858af0f33506de73b1821599c6da3e0f3: "| Streaming | 320 ms chunks (64 mel frames), left context 70, conv cache 8 |"
   - anchor `soniqo_README.md:L83-L83 @ d09ec62` @ HF soniqo/Parakeet-EOU-120M-ONNX-INT8@d09ec62858af0f33506de73b1821599c6da3e0f3: "| Encoder RTF | ~67× | Faster than real time; per-chunk compute well under the 320 ms window |"
   - anchor `pr_master.md:L57-L57 @ master` @ github altunenes/parakeet-rs@master (README, sha not recorded): "const CHUNK_SIZE: usize = 2560; // 160ms at 16kHz"
4. CS-22 cheapest-route judgement: this package is NOT the cheapest turnkey route as shipped (no documented cache contract, no Python reference, frontend missing). It is, however, the best-parity artifact set (fp32/fp16 exact on the card's fixture), and the NeMo reference (intake-2035#record) supplies the state-reset-on-EOU and 80 ms chunk discipline. The lowest-risk path is: inspect the ONNX I/O signatures (shape metadata only), implement the 128-bin NeMo log-mel in numpy, drive fp32 encoder + int8 decoder_joint with onnxruntime CPU in 80-160 ms chunks, and check token parity against a NeMo CPU run on the same clips before measuring EOU latency and false-cut rate on CS-4. CPU latency is unknown: the only figure found (soniqo: encoder RTF ~67x, macOS arm64, int8) is vendor-style, a different platform, and not for these files.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. W2 summary 'decoder-only INT8 token-exact, encoder INT8 not exact' confirmed; added that decoder and joint are one graph, the frontend is absent from the package, and the cache contract is undocumented. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: per-file sizes removed (not in saved files). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://huggingface.co/soniqo/Parakeet-EOU-120M-ONNX-INT8 - Card makes an unreliable 25-language claim for an English-only base; parakeet-rs chosen instead. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

The ysdede package is a plain encoder + decoder_joint ONNX conversion with a narrow exactness claim (fp32, fp16, decoder-int8 only) and no latency evidence; budget real work for the mel frontend and the cache-state wiring. Do NOT carry: 'int8 is exact' (only the decoder), any CPU speed number for this package, or soniqo's multilingual/RTF claims. License fact only: NVIDIA Open Model License.

## intake-2037#record — CUDA: mul_mat is not batch-invariant for IQ quants at n=5 and n=8 on SM120

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/28111
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty high, relevance high; categories: speculative_decoding, hardware_optimization, quantization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Issue #28111 (open, no labels, 4 comments, opened 2026-08-31 by cuhawk, updated 2026-10-17) reports that on SM120 ggml_mul_mat with IQ3_S and IQ4_XS weights is not batch-invariant at n=5 and n=8: a batched mul_mat is bitwise different from n serial single-column calls, while n=1..4 agree (3 geometries, tolerance 0, 12/18 pass). The reporter did not fix it.
   - anchor `issue.json /body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28111 via GitHub API (updated_at 2026-10-17T20:29:54Z): "All three geometries fail at exactly `n=5` and `n=8`"
1. The MMVQ/MMQ crossover is NOT the cause: GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_MAX=4 leaves 12/18, =1 gives 3/18 (batched side moves to MMQ while the serial reference stays on MMVQ). The reporter infers a per-ncols specialisation inside MMVQ changing accumulation order.
   - anchor `issue.json /body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28111 via GitHub API (updated_at 2026-10-17T20:29:54Z): "That suggests the divergence at `n=5`/`n=8` is within MMVQ itself"
2. Independent reproductions: RTX 5090 (SM120, master 207bdab950, n=5..8 fail) and RTX 5060 Ti. Both report the remedy: forcing the number of warps constant across n restores invariance. Cost is disputed: 5090 commenter says forcing 2 or 4 warps costs performance; 5060 Ti commenter says forcing the generic table to nwarps=2 costs nothing in a limited test. Nobody has a merged fix; a canonical-reduction-tree implementation was promised but not posted by the data cutoff.
   - anchor `comments.json /2/body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28111 via GitHub API: "Forcing the number of warps at 2 or 4 irrespective of `n` fixes the issue (restores invariance) but it affects the performance of this kernel."
   - anchor `comments.json /3/body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28111 via GitHub API: "The solution of just forcing generic's (blackwell) nwarp count to 2 does work without a penalty"
3. Code at upstream master 9df0b73c7: calc_nwarps GENERIC table returns 4 for ncols_dst 1-4 and 2 for 5-8 (mmvq.cu L452-L467), which matches the observed n<=4 pass / n=5..8 fail split exactly (nwarps sets the number of tmp_shared partial sums added before warp_reduce_sum, mmvq.cu L816-L819). The failing path is MMVQ (mul_mat_vec_q), not MMQ.
   - anchor `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/mmvq.cu:L452-L467 @ 9df0b73` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp@9df0b73c707900b7277a46b5ae52ff7e7e567d13: "case 8: return 2;"
4. HIP/gfx90a (INFERENCE, not tested by any source): the same mul_mat_vec_q kernel and calc_nwarps run in the HIP build; gfx90a maps to table MMVQ_PARAMETERS_GCN (GCN||CDNA, mmvq.cu L113-L114), where nwarps is 2 for n=1, 2-3 and 4 for most types but 1 for n>=5 (Q3_K: 1 at n=2,3; Q6_K: 1 at n=4). So the reduction tree changes at n=5 for IQ3_S/IQ4_XS on gfx90a too, and at n=2 for Q3_K, n=4 for Q6_K. ggml_cuda_should_use_mmvq for CDNA2 sends IQ3_S/IQ4_XS to MMVQ for ne11<=8 (default branch), so the affected range is n=5..8 there. The upstream test would settle it on our card.
   - anchor `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/mmvq.cu:L468-L482 @ 9df0b73` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp@9df0b73c707900b7277a46b5ae52ff7e7e567d13: "} else if (table_id == MMVQ_PARAMETERS_GCN) {"
   - anchor `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/mmvq.cu:L113-L114 @ 9df0b73` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp@9df0b73c707900b7277a46b5ae52ff7e7e567d13: "if (GGML_CUDA_CC_IS_GCN(cc) || GGML_CUDA_CC_IS_CDNA(cc)) {"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: issue is NVIDIA-only evidence; added code-grounded mechanism (calc_nwarps GENERIC 4->2 at ncols 5) and the gfx90a GCN/CDNA table inference; nwarps-remedy cost is disputed, not settled.

### Verdict

MUL_MAT batch variance is a SECOND, independent cause of greedy spec-dec divergence beside flash attention: MMVQ picks nwarps by ncols_dst, which changes the reduction tree, so a batched verify (n>=5) is not bitwise equal to n=1 decode. Reproduced on SM120 only; the HIP/gfx90a applicability is an inference from the GCN/CDNA table (nwarps 2->1 at n>=5) and has not been run by anyone. Do NOT carry "fix = force nwarps" as a validated remedy: the cost claims conflict and there is no merged patch. The batch-invariance test shape (batched vs serial, memcmp, tol 0) is the reusable artifact.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`

## intake-2038#record — cuda: stabilize quantized FA for speculative batches

- **URL**: https://github.com/Apoze/llama.cpp/commit/6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: speculative_decoding, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The commit (one commit on a personal fork branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance; message title only "cuda: stabilize quantized FA for speculative batches") touches ggml-cuda fattn-common.cuh, fattn-vec.cuh and fattn.cu only (+16/-5), no test and no benchmark.
   - anchor `commit.json /files` @ Apoze/llama.cpp@6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa (branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance, parent 8767ef1ca8d644080a14e78378704989df16764c): "cuda: stabilize quantized FA for speculative batches"
1. Dispatch change (fattn.cu): on the NVIDIA cc>=Ada branch with quantized K/V, the VEC kernel is chosen for Q->ne[1] <= 4 instead of <= 2.
   - anchor `fattn.cu hunk @@ -466 (diff)` @ Apoze/llama.cpp@6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa (branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance, parent 8767ef1ca8d644080a14e78378704989df16764c): "+ if (Q->ne[1] <= 4) {"
2. Vec launcher change (fattn-vec.cuh): on NVIDIA cc>=Ada with a quantized K or V type, always instantiate cols_per_block = 1 (as the ne[1]==1 case does), so every query column runs with the single-column tile arithmetic.
   - anchor `fattn-vec.cuh hunk @@ -548 (diff)` @ Apoze/llama.cpp@6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa (branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance, parent 8767ef1ca8d644080a14e78378704989df16764c): "+ if (Q->ne[1] == 1 || fixed_quantized_vec_partition) {"
3. Partition change (fattn-common.cuh launch_fattn): for NVIDIA cc>=Ada, Q->ne[1]<=4, quantized K/V and no f16 conversion, parallel_blocks is NOT clamped to ntiles_KV and the efficiency search loop is capped at the initial parallel_blocks (parallel_blocks_max), so the KV split/combine order no longer depends on ntiles_KV.
   - anchor `fattn-common.cuh hunk @@ -1149 (diff)` @ Apoze/llama.cpp@6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa (branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance, parent 8767ef1ca8d644080a14e78378704989df16764c): "+ const int parallel_blocks_max = fixed_quantized_vec_partition ? parallel_blocks : ntiles_KV;"
4. Scope and cost: gated to NVIDIA Ada+ (GGML_CUDA_CC_IS_NVIDIA && cc >= ADA_LOVELACE), unconditional (no env switch), quantized KV only; performance cost is NOT stated anywhere in the commit. No upstream PR from this author was found (GitHub search author:Apoze in ggml-org/llama.cpp returned 0 items); the closest upstream attempt, #28108 (different design, Blackwell-only), was closed unmerged on 2026-09-21. Nothing here applies to gfx90a as written (NVIDIA-gated).
   - anchor `fattn-common.cuh hunk @@ -1149 (diff)` @ Apoze/llama.cpp@6_263_445_513_416ef93fd5d05391299f33c64702fa (branch fix/cuda-speculative-batch-invariance, parent 8767ef1ca8d644080a14e78378704989df16764c): "+ const bool fixed_quantized_vec_partition = GGML_CUDA_CC_IS_NVIDIA(cc) &&"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: confirmed exact scope; established it differs from #28108 (cols_per_block=1 plus fixed parallel_blocks, Ada+, quantized KV) and has no stated cost, test or upstream PR.

### Verdict

This is the minimal "make the FA reduction order independent of batch width" recipe for quantized-KV vec attention (per-column single-width tile + KV split fixed regardless of KV length), unmerged, untested in the commit, cost unreported, NVIDIA-gated. Take the IDEA (pin both tile width and parallel_blocks) as a design pattern for an FA batch-invariance variant; do NOT cite it as a validated or upstream fix, and do NOT assume a cost.

## intake-2039#record — CUDA: batch-invariant flash-attn for narrow widths on SM120

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/28108
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: speculative_decoding, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PR #28108 (closed, merged=false, 0 reviews, 0 review comments, 2 comments: a template/AI-detected bot note and a maintainer AI-usage-policy note) adds tests/test-backend-ops.cpp struct test_flash_attn_ext_batch_invariance plus a Blackwell-gated dispatch fix.
   - anchor `pr.json /state, comments.json /1/body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "It is strictly prohibited to use AI to write your posts for you"
1. Test design: one graph builds a BATCHED ggml_flash_attn_ext over n_tokens queries and a SERIAL chain of n_tokens single-query flash_attn_ext ops over the same K/V views, concatenates [batched | serial] into one output, and err() returns 0.0 if memcmp of the two halves is equal else 1.0 with max_err() = 0.0, so any bit difference fails. Fixed shapes: head_size 256, 24 q heads, 4 kv heads, Q8_0 K/V stored as 2*n_kv views of length n_kv, F16 kq mask, GGML_PREC_F32, run_whole_graph() true. Sweep: n_kv in {2048, 7168, 16384} x n_tokens in {1,2,3,4} = 12 cases (KV length is swept because it changes the parallel_blocks choice).
   - anchor `pr.diff tests/test-backend-ops.cpp test_flash_attn_ext_batch_invariance` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "+ return memcmp(backend, backend + count/2, count/2 * sizeof(float)) == 0 ? 0.0 : 1.0;"
   - anchor `pr.diff tests/test-backend-ops.cpp test list` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "+ for (int64_t n_kv : {2048, 7168, 16384}) {"
2. The test is backend-agnostic (plain ggml graph, no CUDA code), so it is directly reusable as a template for an AK batch-invariance gate on HIP/Vulkan/CPU; it must be extended with other KV types (F16, Q4/IQ) and widths up to the real verify width. Its compare is batched-vs-serial on the SAME backend, not vs CPU, so it needs no reference build.
   - anchor `pr.diff test build_graph` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "ggml_flash_attn_ext_set_prec(result, GGML_PREC_F32);"
3. Fix: on cc >= BLACKWELL, Q->ne[1] in 3..4 and ne[3]==1, route to BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC and instantiate cols_per_block=4 (the tile widths 1 and 2 already reduce to), gated by env LLAMA_SM120_FA_INVARIANT (0 disables). Cost +15.5-16.5% FA time at widths 3-4, none elsewhere - measured on one RTX 5080 Laptop at hs256, 24/4 heads, kv 7168, q8_0 KV.
   - anchor `pr.diff fattn.cu hunk` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "+ if (cc >= GGML_CUDA_CC_BLACKWELL && can_use_vector_kernel && Q->ne[1] <= 4 && Q->ne[3] == 1 &&"
4. Why closed unmerged: most likely (inference: no comment states the reason; the policy note shares the close timestamp) the maintainer policy note (2026-09-21, JohannesGaessler) quoting the AI-usage policy that prohibits AI-written PR descriptions, after the bot flagged template non-compliance and AI-detected content on 2026-08-31. The closure comment contains no technical objection; nothing on design merits was reviewed. The author kept a MUL_MAT test out of this PR deliberately because it would fail CI on master (see #28111).
   - anchor `comments.json /0/body` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28108 via GitHub API (base 2d8d612e4c68d3801e556a1b4a028f55ec33ecbb, head 1e2f6954442e6369eda1c4c5c30561109e5f3d8a; closed unmerged 2026-09-21T14:38:30Z): "AI-generated content"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: closure reason pinned to the AI-usage policy (no technical review occurred); test design extracted from the diff; the test is backend-agnostic. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: added measurement conditions to the cost figure; closure reason marked as inference.

### Verdict

Take the TEST DESIGN (batched-vs-serial in one graph, memcmp, tolerance 0, KV-length sweep, F32 prec) as the template for an AK batch-invariance op gate. Do NOT cite the +16% cost or the byte-identical result as general: single card, single prompt, Blackwell-only. It was closed for an AI-writing policy reason, not rejected on technical merit, so closure is no evidence against the approach.
