## intake-2040#record — Semble: Fast and Accurate Code Search for Agents

- **URL**: https://github.com/MinishLab/semble
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: search_retrieval, tool_implementation, rag_alternatives, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2019#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design (README + src/semble/search.py @8ee6463): tree-sitter code-aware chunks; two retrievers, static Model2Vec embeddings (minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2, no transformer at query time) and BM25; each retriever over-fetches top_k*5 candidates, both converted to RRF scores 1/(60+rank), combined as alpha*semantic + (1-alpha)*BM25 with alpha 0.5 for natural-language queries and 0.3 for symbol-like queries; then boosts (definition x3.0, identifier stems x1.0, file coherence 0.2) and path penalties (tests, compat/legacy, examples, .d.ts).
   - anchor `README.md "How it works"` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "The two score lists are fused with Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF)."
   - anchor `src/semble/ranking/boosting.py:L81-L82 @ 8ee6463` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "_ALPHA_NL = 0.5 # balanced semantic + BM25"
1. Numbers (self-reported, benchmarks/README.md): NDCG@10 0.855 over ~1,251 queries / 63 repos / 19 languages vs CodeRankEmbed 0.839, ColGREP 0.693, BM25 0.673 and zvec-grep 0.670 (zvec-grep is the same potion-code-16M-v2 + BM25 recipe without semble ranking). No confidence intervals or seeds are reported. Labels are file-level (annotations/*.json hold "relevant"/"secondary" file paths) generated by Claude Sonnet 4.6 and checked by the same model as judge.
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md "Main results"` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "| **semble** | **0.855** | **240 ms** | **0.69 ms** |"
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md "Dataset" Notes` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "queries and ground-truth relevance labels are generated by Claude Sonnet 4.6. The same model is used as LLM-as-judge to verify label quality."
2. Ablation shows the code-aware ranking, not the dense model, carries most of the gain: BM25 raw 0.686 -> 0.838 with ranking; adding potion-code-16M-v2 on top gives 0.855 (+0.017, no CI). Dense raw alone (0.652) is below BM25 raw (0.686).
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md "Ablations"` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "| BM25 | 0.686 | 0.838 |"
3. The "agent-facing" evaluation is a SIMULATION, not an agent run: expected tokens until first relevant hit (ripgrep per-keyword + read matched files in full, 32k cap) vs semble top-50 chunks: 45,587 vs 353 tokens ("99% fewer"); recall at 2k tokens 0.963 vs 0.037. The ripgrep baseline is a fixed keyword heuristic written by the authors (same weakness as sgrep intake-1981#record notes for its literal-OR baseline). No task-success (SWE-bench-style) evaluation exists in the repo; sgrep has one (vendor, 50 tasks).
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md "Token efficiency / Methodology"` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "`ripgrep+read` splits the query into keywords (dropping stopwords and short words), runs `rg --fixed-strings --ignore-case` for each keyword, then reads matched files in full"
4. Operational: MIT; Python (uv tool install), CPU only, on-disk index cache with mtime incremental reindex, MCP server (search, find_related), library API SembleIndex.from_path/search, SEMBLE_MODEL_NAME lets a local Model2Vec path replace the HF download. Not run; repo only cloned.
   - anchor `README.md "Using a custom model"` @ MinishLab/semble@8ee6463efbb1ff755c339e965c7298b7afd7db5b: "you can set your `SEMBLE_MODEL_NAME` environment variable to a local path or Hugging Face repository"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry; flagged label provenance (same LLM generates and judges), absence of CIs, simulated rather than live agent eval, and ablation showing BM25+ranking 0.838 vs hybrid 0.855. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/zvec-ai/zvec-grep - Semble's own table already contrasts it; no new decision. [wave4 declined].

### Verdict

Semble is a better-SPECIFIED public retrieval baseline than sgrep on retrieval quality (pinned repos, annotations in-repo, 11 baselines, ablation, one-command reproduction) but a weaker one on agent outcome (simulated tokens only, no task-success run). Both use the same potion-code-16M-v2 + BM25 core. Its own ablation says the code-aware reranker, not the dense half, gives most of the lift, and the benchmark is code-only with LLM-written file-level labels, so none of it transfers as evidence about REPL spill files / context bundles. Use it as an additional arm and a harness pattern, not as a rationale for the REPL-EMB-2.1 margin.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md`

## intake-2041#record — EloScaling — code release for "When Agents Slow Down" (repo agent-tts/Agent-TTS-Code)

- **URL**: https://github.com/agent-tts/Agent-TTS-Code
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2010#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The repo is the code release for the Elo-per-token paper (identity by identical title only; no arXiv ID in the repo). It ships the Harbor watchdog patches, benchmark adapters, and the analysis pipeline, but NOT the raw trials or aggregates, so nothing is reproducible offline from this repo alone.
   - anchor `README.md:L1-L4 @ f10c0c5` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "Code for the paper **"When Agents Slow Down: Understanding LLM Agents' Test-Time Strategies via Elo-per-token Analysis"**."
1. Estimator: players are (agent, token-checkpoint) pairs; games are formed only within a task from per-trial prefix-max scores (all trial pairs, ties = half a point); counts are pooled over tasks; 0.5 phantom games per ordered player pair; Bradley-Terry fitted by Hunter-style MM, strengths renormalised to geometric mean 1 each iteration, Elo = 400*log10(pi), one player anchored at 1000.
   - anchor `analysis/export_joint_elo_ci.py:L4-L9 @ f10c0c5 (docstring); fit at analysis/elo_checkpoint_multitask.py:L57-L112` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "task-local all-pairs games over fixed trial panels, prefix-max with 0 before a trial's first submission and carry-forward after its last, ties half a point, 0.5 phantom games per ordered player pair, Bradley-Terry MM fit, 1.5x checkpoint grid 100K..100M"
2. Checkpoint grid and CIs: 1.5x geometric grid from 100K to 100M tokens (cache-inclusive axis; uncached variants use their own ranges, e.g. 50K..2M); CIs are 500-replicate percentile bootstraps that resample whole sessions within each (task, agent) cell and refit with the same anchor.
   - anchor `analysis/export_joint_elo_ci.py:L10-L14 @ f10c0c5` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "500-replicate session bootstrap: complete sessions are resampled with replacement within every (task, agent) cell and the tournament is refitted with the same anchor."
3. Self-Elo versus joint-Elo: self-Elo fits each agent alone and removes same-trial pairs (a trace must not play its own earlier prefix); joint-Elo keeps all pairs. Trials that ended before a checkpoint carry their final value forward (censored flag, alive_frac reported); trials with no submission yet score 0.
   - anchor `analysis/export_selfelo_ci.py:L5-L11 @ f10c0c5` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "games are task-local between the agent's own checkpoint players, cross-trial pairs only (same-trial pairs excluded, as in the shipped data), prior 0.5 phantom games, lowest checkpoint anchored at 1000."
   - anchor `analysis/aggregate.py:L28-L45 @ f10c0c5 (COLUMNS); analysis/elo_checkpoint.py:L68-L98 (censoring)` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "If a trial exists but made no submission before the checkpoint, its prefix-best score is 0."
4. Log schema needed for reuse: one row per scored evaluation with trial_id, tokens_so_far (any monotone cumulative budget) and score; task and agent identity from the path. The pipeline ignores the other 13 columns of aggregate.py. The token axis is only an ordering/threshold key, so evaluations, wall-clock or controller tokens work unchanged.
5. Not in the repo: raw data, a license for the project own code (one-line fact), and any code for the b_inf inflection point or K=round(B/b_inf) rule; those would have to be written from the paper (about 20 lines on top of the fitted self-Elo curve).
   - anchor `README.md "Data" section @ f10c0c5` @ Agent-TTS-Code@f10c0c5adf0388500b75983762178e3976a202e5: "Raw trials (per-trial `agent/submissions.jsonl`, agent logs, trajectories), the aggregated `data/elo/combined/` layer, the AtCoder human dataset and the FlashInfer archive will be released separately."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: confirmed repo is the Elo-per-token code release (title match only); resolved the App. B estimator details left open in intake-2023#record; found the inflection rule is not in the code. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {4: 3}).

### Verdict

This repo settles the estimator: it is a ~100-line numpy/pure-python BT-MM with task-local pairs, a 1.5x checkpoint grid and session bootstrap, and it consumes just (trial_id, tokens_so_far, score). Reimplement from these functions; do not expect data or the inflection rule here. Do not carry any headline Elo number from this entry; the repo has none that can be regenerated.

## intake-2042#record — When Independent Sampling Outperforms Agentic Reasoning

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08478
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, cost_aware_routing, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2010#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design is compute-matched in both dollars (with prompt caching on for agents) and model calls: k-shot independent single-call attempts, one SWE-agent run with budget c, and three independent SWE-agent runs with c/3 each, on 216 Codeforces problems (c_max $2, k_max 50) with o3, Claude Sonnet 4 and Gemini 2.5 Pro.
   - anchor `§2 Methods, "Evaluation objective and budgets"` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "Unless otherwise noted, we set c max = 2.0 (USD per problem) and k max = 50 (model calls per problem)"
1. Result as stated: k-shot solves strictly more problems than both agent variants at every fixed budget, by cost and by query count; the gap narrows only marginally as budget grows. The text gives no numbers; everything is read from curves.
   - anchor `§3 Results and Interpretation, para 1` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "for any fixed budget threshold, k -shot solves strictly more problems than both a single SWE-agent run and the budget-partitioned SWE-agent ( 1 / 3 × 3 ) variant."
   - anchor `Fig. 1 caption` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "k -shot inference outperforms budget-partitioned agents and one full SWE-agent by both metrics."
2. Its own explanation is task structure: competitive-programming problems are self-contained, so agents burn budget on loops and debugging (Table 1 shares are over unsuccessful agent runs, judged by GPT-4.1), while independent calls surface rare correct paths early.
   - anchor `§3 Results and Interpretation, para 3` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "Instead, agents are less effective per model invocation , despite benefiting from iterative refinement and tool access."
3. Theory: for success-or-fail solvers with independent retries, the cost-optimal solver minimises -ln(1-p)/c; a stateful search should be truncated at a finite q* and then repeated independently. Evidence for a finite q* is one Div-3 problem (plus one Div-1 problem in an appendix), estimated with Clopper-Pearson intervals.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction, scaling-trends paragraph` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "the cost-optimal cost limit per attempt for an agent is finite in our experiment."
4. Corroboration status for "long agentic sessions lose to independent sampling or restarts per token": PARTIAL and different in kind. The comparison is a one-call model versus an agent, not a restarted-agent policy versus a continued session. 1/3x3 versus single agent is never ordered in the text. Single agent framework (SWE-agent) and a private evaluator are acknowledged limits.
   - anchor `§7 Conclusion` @ arXiv:2605.08478v1 (html): "A limitation of this study is that it evaluates only a single agent framework, SWE-agent."
5. Implication for AK restarts: supports measuring per-restart success probability and cost rather than assuming longer is better, and supports the K-restarts idea only through the log-failure-per-cost metric, which needs a binary success definition (for example a keep above a threshold). AK scores are continuous and state carries over, so the theorem does not apply as is.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: narrowed the intake-2023#record follow-up: corroboration is PARTIAL (k-shot vs agent, not restart policy), single framework, private evaluator, figures only. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {3: 1, 4: 3, 5: 4}). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): W4-A surfaced (princeton-pli/competitive-programming-agents, agentpatterns.ai restatement, zhaiyi000/tlm, SLA repo) - All dive-recommended decline (restatement, duplicate of intake-2024#record, or code without new decision value). [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Treat as a weak, partly independent signal that cost-matched independent attempts beat a single SWE-agent on self-contained algorithmic tasks. Do NOT cite it as evidence that restarting long agent sessions at an inflection point wins per token; it never tests that, and it contains no numeric result tables. For AK, take only the metric -ln(1-p)/c and the idea of choosing a finite per-attempt budget, and only after defining a binary per-restart success.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2043#record — Enabling Tensor Language Model to Assist in Generating High-Performance Tensor Programs for Deep Learning

- **URL**: https://usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/zhai
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: hardware_optimization, autonomous_research, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2026#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity: the "TLM (Zhai et al., 2024)" cited by Pruner is this OSDI 2024 paper (author, year and name match); the only primary URLs observed this run are the USENIX presentation page and PDF. No arXiv version was observed.
   - anchor `p.289 title/abstract` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/zhai"
1. A pretrained LM does NOT generate kernel source or full schedules directly. It samples one decision token group at a time (tile sizes, unroll, parallelism) inside a fixed Ansor or MetaSchedule decision space and the compiler builds the program; sentences are capped at 1024 tokens and a 100M-parameter GPT-2-small is used.
   - anchor `p.289 §1 Introduction` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "instead of aiming for direct end-to-end generation source code, we utilize tensor compilers for constructing tensor programs"
   - anchor `p.295 §5.1 Model architecture` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "TLM adopts the architecture of GPT-2 Small, which encompasses approximately 100 million parameters."
2. It shortens rather than removes the measurement loop: with 1 measurement per subgraph (1xg) it averages 0.83 / 0.80 of the 20K-measurement baseline, with 10xg it matches it (about 1.0 average), and with 20K measurements it is 1.08x / 1.04x faster on V100. The headline 61x / 95x compilation speedups are measurement-count ratios (Appendix A), not wall time, and exclude the offline cost.
   - anchor `p.299 §6.4.1 Compilation time; Appendix A p.304` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "can deliver the same performance level while accelerating compilation by 95×."
3. Offline cost is the catch: about 300K labelled measurements (tens of hours) plus about 10 h pre-training on 4 V100s; the authors say TLM trades compile time for pre-compile time and is uneconomic for one or a few models, and the training and target distributions must be aligned. It is not limited to seen subgraphs: 12 of 138 workloads were held out for test.
   - anchor `p.300 §8 Limitation` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "TLM’s design philosophy is trading compile time for pre-compile time."
4. Hardware is NVIDIA V100 and a 4-core Intel laptop CPU (CUDA 11.6, TVM 0.12); nothing on AMD or ROCm. The idea (an LM trained on measured history proposing the next knob decision inside a bounded space) is portable; the TVM-specific tensor language is not.
   - anchor `p.298 §6.1 Experimental settings` @ USENIX OSDI 2024 proceedings PDF (osdi24-zhai.pdf sha256 ca202420617e02d28cc0c1d4c5e9ff56bd9b8a7fef60ed2a47989b00d527349b): "we utilize a server outfitted with a 48-core Intel(R) Xeon(R) Gold 6226 CPU, 376GB memory, and four 32GB NVIDIA Tesla V100 GPUs."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: located primary source (OSDI 2024), established generation is per-decision not source, savings are measurement counts excluding offline cost, hardware V100/laptop; narrowed Pruner "seen in pretraining" characterisation. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {3: 1, 4: 3, 5: 4}).

### Verdict

TLM is a 100M-parameter decision sampler trained offline on hundreds of thousands of measured programs, not a code generator, and its 61x/95x figures count measurements, not hours. Carry forward: LM-over-bounded-decision-space as a pattern and the measure-then-SFT loop; do NOT carry the speedups to AK, whose measured-history corpus is orders of magnitude too small and whose targets are CPU/ROCm kernels.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`

## intake-2044#record — Eval bug: HIP/ROCm on Windows gfx1201 (R9700), batched target scoring changes logits/top-1; Vulkan control is stable

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/28768
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: speculative_decoding, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Issue #28768 (open, labels bug-unconfirmed and stale, no maintainer reply, no 'first bad commit', updated_at 2026-10-19) reports that on Windows HIP (gfx1201, R9700, 'ROCm 10', b10909) Gemma 4 31B Q4_K_XL scoring the same prefix as 1-token vs 3-token batches flips greedy top-1 at two of 27 positions, at near-ties (margin 0.22 logits); the report names NO kernel, op or code path.
   - anchor `issue.json /body (HIP flip, margin)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "The first greedy top-1 reversal occurs at completion index 22, predicting absolute position 51."
1. The reporter's Vulkan control (byte-identical rows, no flips) was shown to be weak evidence for 'HIP-specific': commenter DanoPTT built HIP and Vulkan from the same commit (b10327, Qwen3.8-27B - not the reporter's Gemma/b10909 setup) and found BOTH differ in all 512 rows between widths (Vulkan 3 flips, HIP 1-2), and the reporter conceded his wording was too broad; Vulkan is not a batch-invariant control.
   - anchor `issue.json /body (Vulkan control)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "selected full float32 logits rows are byte-identical across single-token, three-token batched and repeated single-token scoring"
   - anchor `comments.json /0/body (DanoPTT section 2, Vulkan not stable)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "Both backends differ in **all 512 rows** between widths."
2. The one localisation in the thread is not upstream: DanoPTT's apparent 15x 'regression window' came from his own fork's fused gated-delta-net gate/beta kernel enabled only at ne[1]==1, so batch width 1 and width>=2 took different kernels; he withdrew the window claim, and disabling fusion restored upstream-level behaviour (14 flips / 512, max_dP 0.052), which is itself not zero and has no root cause named.
   - anchor `comments.json /2/body (DanoPTT correction, gated-delta-net fusion)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "The fused kernel therefore runs only at batch width 1."
3. Measurement method from the thread that transfers: score the same prefix at widths W in {1,2,3,4}, compare full f32 rows; report centred (row-mean-subtracted) max delta and max_dP rather than raw max_abs; classify near-ties from the REFERENCE arm's pre-softmax top1-top2 gap (<1.0 and <0.25) so the denominator is fixed; a clean result on a confident prompt proves nothing; and fixed-width perplexity cannot see a path that differs only at width 1.
   - anchor `comments.json /2/body (PPL blindness)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "Perplexity is measured at one fixed batch width, so a bug that only fires at width 1 is invisible to it."
4. A second, independent reproduction on Linux with ROCm 7.2.4 (2x gfx1201, Qwen3.8-27B-Q8_0 dense, MTP speculative decoding, greedy) shows 3/8 to 5/8 prompts diverging from non-speculative output, deterministic across -np 1/2, flipping where the baseline top-2 probabilities were 0.4982 vs 0.4875; again no kernel is localised. All AMD reports are gfx1201 (RDNA4); no CDNA/gfx90a data and no ROCm-version isolation exist in the thread (DanoPTT says ROCm 7.14 vs 10.0.0 runtimes gave byte-identical output).
   - anchor `comments.json /5/body (clauscampbell Linux ROCm 7.2.4 MTP)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp issue #28768 api snapshot updated_at 2026-10-19T01:27:39Z: "Same symptom here: changing only the target-scoring shape flips greedy top-1."
5. Transfer to gfx90a is INFERENCE only: ggml-cuda MMVQ/FA/GDN code is shared between CUDA and HIP, so width-dependent kernel selection plausibly also exists on MI210, but nothing in this thread tests it; the sibling intake-2037#record nwarps table prediction remains untested.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: the Stage-1 expectation that this issue shows an AMD HIP kernel cause for batched target scoring is NOT met: no kernel/path is named, the Vulkan control is contested by a same-commit comparison, the only localisation (fused GDN at ne[1]==1) was in a commenter's fork and not upstream. It is also not the only AMD HIP report: a Linux ROCm 7.2.4 MTP replication sits in the same thread (still gfx1201). ROCm version: reporter 'ROCm 10' (Windows; the same-GPU commenter lists ROCm runtime 10.0.0, HIP SDK 7.2.0), replication ROCm 7.2.4. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {2: 1, 3: 2, 4: 3, 5: 4}). Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim 1 setup corrected (b10327/Qwen3.8-27B); #28211 details moved to notes (not in this thread). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): W4-B surfaced (#28211, LMR-P4-UPSTREAM-REPRO.zip) - #28211 is a different bug (host-buffer defect); zip has no URL. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Treat this as an open, unlocalised, near-tie-regime report: width-dependent logits exist on gfx1201 HIP and also on Vulkan, with a Linux ROCm 7.2.4 MTP replication, but no kernel is identified, no maintainer has engaged and nothing is measured on gfx90a. Take the MEASUREMENT RECIPE (centred delta, max_dP, reference-arm near-tie density, multi-width, same-commit backend control) and the lesson that a width-1-only fusion (decode-only GDN fusion) silently breaks invariance while fixed-width PPL stays clean. Do NOT carry 'Vulkan is stable' or 'HIP regression window 10400->10513' (withdrawn), and do NOT claim an MI210 defect from it.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`

## intake-2045#record — vulkan: make MMVQ path selection independent of batch size

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/28489
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: speculative_decoding, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PR #28489 (open, not draft, by Ankk98 who has 3 open PRs, AI-use disclosed 'YES', created 2026-09-06, no commits or comments since, updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z) removes the Vulkan early-out `if (n > 1) { return true; }` from ggml_vk_should_use_mmvq, so the MMVQ-vs-F32-DMMV decision uses the same vendor/type/k checks for every batch size.
   - anchor `pr.json /body Overview` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 api snapshot updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z head Ankk98:multi-token-precision-fix: "ggml_vk_should_use_mmvq returned true for any n > 1, so N=1 decode ran F32 DMMV while N>1 speculative verify staged Q8_1 + integer-dot MMVQ."
   - anchor `pr.diff ggml-vulkan.cpp hunk @@ -9561` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 pr.diff: "- // MMVQ is generally good for batches - if (n > 1) { - return true; - }"
1. Claimed mechanism: N=1 decode ran the F32 mul_mat_vec (DMMV) path while N>1 speculative verification quantised activations to Q8_1 and used integer-dot MMVQ; the two differ by up to 0.2 and flip greedy picks at near-ties. The new MMVQ_BATCH test compares column 0 of an N=1 and an N=2 mul_mat at ULP-0 over Q2_0/Q4_0/Q4_1/Q4_K/Q5_0/Q5_1/Q6_K/Q8_0 x F32 (m=512,k=2048 and m=256,k=1024): 8 of 16 cases fail before and all pass after (Vulkan) and on CPU. No IQ-type or MUL_MAT_ID batch case is covered.
   - anchor `pr.json /body Overview (test)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 api snapshot updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z head Ankk98:multi-token-precision-fix: "Adds MMVQ_BATCH (Q2_0/Q4_0/Q4_1/Q4_K/Q5_0/Q5_1/Q6_K/Q8_0 x F32, N=1 vs N=2 col0 ULP-0): 8/16 fail pre-fix, 16/16 pass post-fix on Vulkan, 16/16 on CPU."
2. Measured perf cost is thin and does not test the contested regime: only tg128 at N=1 (43.76 vs 43.67 tok/s, 'default vs FORCE_MMVQ', device and model unnamed), pp128 'identical' and an inconclusive spec-decode end-to-end; no N>1 verify-width throughput, no AMD-specific figure and no real-model flip-rate evidence is given.
   - anchor `pr.json /body Overview (validation)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 api snapshot updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z head Ankk98:multi-token-precision-fix: "Decode timing tg128 43.76 vs 43.67 tok/s default vs FORCE_MMVQ; spec-decode e2e shows no conclusive delta."
3. Only maintainer response: 0cc4m (author_association CONTRIBUTOR) wrote the PR 'needs actual model evidence, not synthetic tests, and even then it is likely not the right solution. It will cost a lot of performance.'; the author replied he has model evidence 'soon' and has posted none; zero reviews or review comments. Status as fetched: open, un-merged, stalled, with a standing performance objection.
   - anchor `comments.json /1/body (0cc4m, CONTRIBUTOR)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 api snapshot updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z head Ankk98:multi-token-precision-fix: "This needs actual model evidence, not synthetic tests, and even then it is likely not the right solution. It will cost a lot of performance."
   - anchor `comments.json /2/body (Ankk98)` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28489 api snapshot updated_at 2026-09-06T13:34:40Z head Ankk98:multi-token-precision-fix: "I do have actual model evidence. I will share it soon."
4. For us the PR is a design pattern, not an adoptable patch: it states a principle (path selection must not depend on N; serial N==1 is the specification) and a cheap ULP-0 N=1 vs N=2 test, but it makes verify-width kernels slower by design and is blocked by the maintainer for that reason; Vulkan is not our serving path, though the MI210 can run it (inference).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: confirmed it is an open PR; the 'measured perf cost' is only tg128 at N=1 (so effectively not a cost measurement of the changed behaviour), and the PR comparison label 'default vs FORCE_MMVQ' is ambiguous; maintainers objection recorded; no response since 2026-09-06. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {1: 0, 4: 3, 5: 1}).

### Verdict

This is an unmerged, stalled, maintainer-contested PR with a one-line functional change and a ULP-0 test; its perf cost for the case that matters (N>1 verification) was never measured, only N=1 tg128. Take the principle and the test design; do NOT cite it as an accepted upstream fix or as proof that Vulkan MMVQ costs little at N>1.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`

## intake-2046#record — tests: add gated delta net batch invariance test

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/28109
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: speculative_decoding, ssm_hybrid, hardware_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2021#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PR #28109 (DRAFT, open, by cuhawk, created 2026-08-31, one bot comment flagging template/AI-text/PR-limit, no reviews, no human comment, updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z) adds one test-only change (+92/-0 in tests/test-backend-ops.cpp): test_gated_delta_net_batch_invariance, registered for n_tokens in {1,2,3,4,5,8}.
   - anchor `pr.diff tests/test-backend-ops.cpp hunk @@ -10290` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "+ for (int64_t n_tokens : {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8}) { + test_cases.emplace_back(new test_gated_delta_net_batch_invariance(48, 128, n_tokens));"
1. Design: one batched ggml_gated_delta_net(q,k,v,g,beta,state) over n_tokens versus a serial chain of n single-token calls (views per token, state threaded step to step via a view into the previous result), outputs and final state concatenated; err() memcmp's the batched half against the serial half and max_err is 0, so any bit difference fails; run_whole_graph is true so it does not use the CPU reference.
   - anchor `pr.json /body What it does` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "Builds one batched `ggml_gated_delta_net` call and a serial chain that threads the state from step to step, then compares them with `memcmp` and `max_err() = 0`."
   - anchor `pr.diff test_gated_delta_net_batch_invariance::err` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "+ return memcmp(backend, backend + count/2, count/2 * sizeof(float)) == 0 ? 0.0 : 1.0;"
2. Shapes and data: head_count 48, head_size 128, n_seqs 1, F32 only, q/k l2-normalised, scalar gate (g and beta have ne0=1, i.e. per-head not per-key gating), g in [-20,-1e-4], beta in [0,1], v in [-0.3,5.0], state random uniform. Tolerance: exact bits.
   - anchor `pr.diff initialize_tensors` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "+ init_tensor_uniform(t, -20.0f, -1e-4f);"
3. Result and scope: 6/6 pass on CUDA0 (RTX 5080 Laptop) only; the author states this is regression coverage, not a bug report, and that no other backend (HIP, CPU, Vulkan, Metal) was run; nothing is claimed about GDN being batch-invariant elsewhere.
   - anchor `pr.json /body Testing` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "`test-backend-ops test -o GATED_DELTA_NET_BATCH_INVARIANCE -b CUDA0` on an RTX 5080 Laptop: **6/6 passed**."
   - anchor `pr.json /body Testing` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28109 api snapshot updated_at 2026-08-31T18:24:26Z head cuhawk:gated-delta-net-batch-invariance: "This is regression coverage, not a bug report — the op is invariant on CUDA today. I have not run it on other backends."
4. As a template for our Qwen3.x recurrent layers it is a direct, cheap op-level starting point (runs on any backend with -b, including HIP gfx90a, and n=5/8 cover verify widths we use) but is NOT sufficient: it tests the isolated op with the scalar-gate F32 case, not the model graph; it cannot see graph-level fusions conditioned on batch width (the fused gate/beta GDN kernel at ne[1]==1 in issue #28768 sits outside the op), the chunked-vs-autoregressive GDN scheduling used for prefill or the conv1d/ssm_conv state update, nor quantised surrounding matmuls. Whether the champion's DFlash2 verify graph calls this op or another path for n>1 is NOT established by this source (inference; must be checked in our tree).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: confirmed test design; the only result is CUDA 6/6 and the author explicitly disclaims other backends; the test's scope is the isolated op with scalar gate. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {0: 1, 1: 0, 2: 1, 4: 3, 5: 2}).

### Verdict

A small, draft, unreviewed, CUDA-only-verified op-level test whose design (batched vs serial state-threaded chain, memcmp, widths 1-8, outputs plus final state) is a good, directly portable template for an AK gate on gfx90a and CPU. Its pass on CUDA does not establish model-level invariance for our champion, and it was never run on HIP or CPU. Do NOT cite it as evidence that GDN is batch-invariant on AMD.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-all-devices-all-dimensions.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/dflash2-block-drafter-experimental-build.md`

## intake-2047#record — Smart Turn v3.2

- **URL**: https://github.com/pipecat-ai/smart-turn
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2031#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Smart Turn v3.x is an AUDIO-native turn-completion classifier (16 kHz mono PCM in, no transcript): Whisper-Tiny ENCODER + linear head, ~8M params, int8 ONNX 8 MB (CPU) or fp32 ONNX 32 MB (GPU); output is a single sigmoid P(turn complete).
   - anchor `repo/README.md Features "Audio native"` @ pipecat-ai/smart-turn@4786657e242dfe77dd138699ac564ee074a2a543: "The model works directly with PCM audio samples, rather than text transcriptions, allowing it to take into account subtle prosody cues in the user's speech"
1. Input contract: up to 8 s of the CURRENT turn audio, left-zero-padded to 8 s (80 mel x 800 frames Whisper features); it is meant to be run only after a lightweight VAD (Silero) sees silence, and re-run on the whole turn if the user resumes. It is not a streaming/anticipating model and not meant for short clips.
   - anchor `repo/README.md "Notes on input format"` @ pipecat-ai/smart-turn@4786657e242dfe77dd138699ac564ee074a2a543: "Up to 8 seconds of audio is supported, and we recommend providing the full audio of the user's current turn."
2. CPU path is plain onnxruntime (CPUExecutionProvider, ORT_SEQUENTIAL, inter_op_num_threads=1, graph opt ALL) via inference.py + transformers WhisperFeatureExtractor; vendor-stated CPU latency 12.6 ms on c7a.2xlarge up to ~95 ms on t3.medium (blog, v3.0); no EPYC number exists and none was measured here.
   - anchor `repo/inference.py build_session` @ pipecat-ai/smart-turn@4786657e242dfe77dd138699ac564ee074a2a543: "so.execution_mode = ort.ExecutionMode.ORT_SEQUENTIAL so.inter_op_num_threads = 1 so.graph_optimization_level = ort.GraphOptimizationLevel.ORT_ENABLE_ALL"
   - anchor `Daily blog "Performance" (v3.0 launch post)` @ https://www.daily.co/blog/announcing-smart-turn-v3-with-cpu-inference-in-just-12ms/ (curl 2026-10-20, sha256 b1701bb0...): "CPU (AWS c7a.2xlarge) 450.6 ms 12.6 ms"
3. Vendor accuracy (v3.2, 31,527 clip-level samples, 23 languages) is 92.63% int8 / 93.71% fp32 with FPR 4.73% / 3.51%; English 94.26%. This is clip-level complete-vs-incomplete classification on its own synthetic+human test set, NOT a streaming endpointing score; weakest languages are Vietnamese, Marathi, Bengali.
   - anchor `HF benchmarks/smart-turn-v3.2-cpu.md "Overall Performance"` @ HF pipecat-ai/smart-turn-v3@f766f81d63 (benchmarks/smart-turn-v3.2-cpu.md): "| Overall | 31,527 | 92.63 | 0.909 | 0.947 | 0.927 | 4.73 | 2.64 |"
4. TurnBench correction: the .752 / .047 / 1017 ms row was produced by TurnBench's own wrapper (baselines/smart_turn_v3/predict.py) around smart-turn v3.1 weights (not v3.2): Silero gate at 0.5, 1000 ms of silence before the FIRST model call, 2 s settle window re-scored every 8 chunks, and predict.py's fallback default threshold 0.05; the published row uses the operating point swept on dev, theta_eot = 0.0082 / theta_int = 0.0155 (baselines/smart_turn_v3/README.md:38). The 1017 ms median latency is therefore dominated by the wrapper's hard 1 s silence gate, not by model speed; do not read it as Smart Turn's intrinsic latency, and the recall is conditional on that wrapper and threshold.
   - anchor `turnbench baselines/smart_turn_v3/predict.py L~85 and model selection` @ SesameAILabs/turnbench@38a6f874322430cb3ca71d8a52aa1e636e88bad8: "_STOP_CHUNKS = math.ceil(1000.0 / _CHUNK_MS)"
   - anchor `turnbench baselines/smart_turn_v3/predict.py model selection` @ SesameAILabs/turnbench@38a6f874322430cb3ca71d8a52aa1e636e88bad8: "_model_file = "smart-turn-v3.1-gpu.onnx" if _cuda else "smart-turn-v3.1.onnx""
   - anchor `turnbench predict.py CHECKPOINT_DEFAULTS` @ SesameAILabs/turnbench@38a6f874322430cb3ca71d8a52aa1e636e88bad8: ""pretrained": (0.05, 0.95),"
   - anchor `dive-w3-turnbench repo baselines/smart_turn_v3/README.md:L38` @ TurnBench repo clone in /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w3-turnbench (see intake-2031#record source_revision): "θ_eot = 0.0082, θ_int = 0.0155 (`turnbench.sweep`)."
5. Scoring it on TurnBench for CS-22: reuse baselines/smart_turn_v3/predict.py (Silero + ONNX; it emits probs at 12.5 Hz and sweeps thresholds via turnbench.sweep) and swap the weights for v3.2-cpu; to compare fairly with a Silero+silence arm, shorten _STOP_CHUNKS (the 1 s gate) and report both settings.
6. Training/eval data are open: pipecat-ai/smart-turn-data-v3.2-train and -test on HF (README); the card says the sets mix synthetic (chirp3, orpheus, rime) and human/contributed (liva, midcentury, mundo, human_*) audio per the benchmark report dataset names; sizes and recipe NOT read (dataset cards not fetched).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Reattributes the TurnBench 1017 ms median to the wrapper's 1000 ms silence gate and v3.1 weights, not v3.2/model speed. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {6: 4}). Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: TurnBench threshold is the swept 0.0082 (0.05 is only the fallback default); added README anchor; removed superlative in W4-SMARTTURN-01. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://huggingface.co/datasets/pipecat-ai/smart-turn-data-v3.2-test - Vendor test-set card; clip-level accuracy meaning only. [wave4 declined_surfaced]; https://www.daily.co/blog/announcing-smart-turn-v3-with-cpu-inference-in-just-12ms/ - Already read in the dive (v3.0 CPU latency table). [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Smart Turn v3.x is a cheap (8 MB int8, ~10-100 ms CPU per call by vendor) audio-only "is this turn complete" scorer that must sit behind a VAD silence gate; it is a plausible CS-22 arm in the onnxruntime CPU loop. DO NOT carry TurnBench's 1017 ms as its latency (that is the 1 s silence gate of the TurnBench wrapper, on v3.1 weights) and DO NOT carry the vendor 92-94% as endpointing accuracy (clip-level, vendor test set, 23 languages). No EPYC latency has been measured.
