## intake-2048#record — Endpoint Anticipation for Low-Latency Spoken Dialogue

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13450
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty high, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2031#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Method: instead of detecting the endpoint after silence, a speech-only causal model forecasts it. For each horizon h in {320,640,...,2560} ms it outputs P(end-of-turn within the next h ms) per 80 ms frame from user AND system audio (two streaming encoders over frozen Mimi codes); the first frame above a threshold triggers the pipeline (Eq. 2-3).
   - anchor `§3.2 Endpoint Anticipation (EPA)` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "We formulate Endpoint Anticipation (EPA) as a set of independent binary classification tasks."
1. It is a speculation trigger, not a turn-taking decision: on trigger the system forks state, has the LLM generate a short look-ahead (~10 tokens) from the PARTIAL TRANSCRIPT, pre-synthesises TTS into a cache, and either releases the cache when the normal endpointer confirms within h or discards it. The endpointer (Unmute semantic VAD) still decides when the user is done.
   - anchor `§4.5.2 Speculative Execution Strategy` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "Trigger & Fork: Upon anticipating an endpoint with horizon h, the system “forks” the conversation state. It triggers the LLM to generate a short look-ahead buffer (e.g., 10 tokens) based on the current partial transcript."
2. Latency result: Unmute average latency 1195 -> 690 ms (505 ms) at h=960 ms with Gemma 3 4B on vLLM, local LLM/TTS, Full-Duplex-Bench v1 turn-taking protocol; the residual 690 ms is attributed to low-anticipation turns, semantic-VAD delay and WebSocket hops. The abstract's "28.4% increase in speculative computation" is the ERC metric (discarded fraction of the theoretical maximum anticipations), not a measured 28.4% rise in total FLOPs.
   - anchor `§5.4 Table 2` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "Unmute Baseline 1195 – Unmute + EPA-M 690 28.4"
   - anchor `§5.4` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "The 28.4% ERC indicates that the volume of discarded speculative computation is restricted to roughly 28% of the theoretical maximum for these turns."
3. False-trigger trade-off is large: at the ERC~33% operating point for h=640/1280 about 66%/53% of turns contain at least one premature trigger (PAR); at the stricter ERC~15% point the median realized anticipation falls to 480 ms with only 22% horizon-entry accuracy. Harm is wasted compute, not user-visible cuts, because speculative audio is cached, but the paper never evaluates response quality when a speculative prefix is committed with a later transcript.
   - anchor `§5.1` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "at a stricter ERC constraint of ≈15%, EPA-M maintains an MRA of 480 ms with a 22.1% Horizon Entry Accuracy (HEA)"
4. Beats a adapted VAP baseline in MRA/HEA at matched ERC (Table 1); works markedly better on task-oriented SpokenWOZ than conversational Switchboard (Sec 5.2); English only, 8 kHz telephone training data; the comparison is against VAP, not against SmartTurn/Parakeet-EOU or silence thresholds.
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "making endpoint anticipation currently most viable for structured applications"
5. Availability: code at github.com/bloodraven66/EndpointAnticipation (model + Unmute integration, README claims Interspeech 2026) and weights at HF viks66/endpoint-anticipation (best_val_acc.pt, best_val_acc_epa-M.pt, config.yaml; sha b9937c63); weights not downloaded here.
   - anchor `repo README Model Weights` @ bloodraven66/EndpointAnticipation@531e1d7e70751e980b82088a3a358a2e75fa8a12: "Pre-trained model checkpoints are available on HuggingFace: > **[viks66/endpoint-anticipation](https://huggingface.co/viks66/endpoint-anticipation)**"
6. Applicability to our cascade: the mechanism (fork LLM+TTS on a forecast, commit on confirmed endpoint) maps onto whisper.cpp -> LLM -> qwentts.cpp only if STT can deliver a partial transcript during speech (the paper uses a streaming ASR; whisper.cpp is chunked) and the LLM server can cheaply discard/prefix-cache speculative generations (llama.cpp prompt cache); the forecaster itself needs Mimi (24 kHz codec) plus a 25M transformer, small enough for CPU. This applicability reading is the dive's inference, not a paper claim.
   - anchor `§4.4 Training Setup` @ arXiv:2606.13450v1 (html): "The model is a 25M parameter streaming Transformer [29]. It comprises a 6-layer encoder with 4 attention heads and a feed-forward dimension of 1024."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Clarified that "28.4% increase in speculative computation" is the ERC ratio, and that speculation (not endpointing) is what is accelerated. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {6: 6}). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://huggingface.co/viks66/endpoint-anticipation - Model card/config only; weights not to be downloaded. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Endpoint anticipation is a latency-hiding speculation technique, not a better endpointer. Its 505 ms win is measured on an Unmute stack with a 4B local LLM on vLLM and a 1195 ms baseline, English, one benchmark, with ~28% of theoretical anticipations discarded; nothing here measures answer quality after a speculative commit or compares against SmartTurn/Parakeet-EOU. For us it is a second-phase idea (needs partial transcripts from STT and cheap LLM-prefix reuse); do NOT carry 505 ms as a gain for the whisper.cpp cascade.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2049#record — parakeet-rs

- **URL**: https://github.com/altunenes/parakeet-rs
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2036#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. parakeet-rs implements the NVIDIA parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 streaming loop end to end on onnxruntime (Rust, ort crate; ONNX files encoder.onnx + decoder_joint.onnx + tokenizer.json, plus scripts/export_realtime_eou_120m.py to make them): the loop is small (parakeet_eou.rs 364 lines + model_eou.rs 230) and directly translatable to Python/C++ onnxruntime.
1. Frontend constants: 16 kHz, n_fft 512, win 400, hop 160, 128 mels (HTK scale, area-normalised triangles, fmax 8000), pre-emphasis 0.97, natural-log with additive guard 5.96e-8, zero-padded centered STFT with NeMo-style floor(len/hop) frames; NO per-feature normalisation or dither in the Rust code (whether NeMo's EOU config requires it was not verified).
   - anchor `src/parakeet_eou.rs:L11-L17 @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "const N_FFT: usize = 512; const WIN_LENGTH: usize = 400; const HOP_LENGTH: usize = 160; const N_MELS: usize = 128; const PREEMPH: f32 = 0.97;"
2. Chunking: 160 ms chunks (2560 samples); a 4 s rolling audio buffer is re-featurised EVERY chunk (inefficient: whole-buffer STFT each 160 ms) and only the last 25 frames (9 pre-encode cache + 16 new) go to the encoder; first output is withheld until 1 s of audio is buffered.
   - anchor `src/parakeet_eou.rs:L150-L153 @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "pre_encode_cache=9 frames, new_frames=~16, total=~25 frames to encoder"
   - anchor `src/parakeet_eou.rs:L169-L172 @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "// Extract features from FULL buffer (provides context for feature extraction) let buffer_slice: Vec<f32> = self.audio_buffer.iter().copied().collect(); let full_features = self.extract_mel_features(&buffer_slice)?;"
3. Encoder cache I/O (ONNX names): in audio_signal [1,128,T], length, cache_last_channel [17,1,70,512], cache_last_time [17,1,512,8], cache_last_channel_len [1]; out outputs [1,512,T], new_cache_last_channel, new_cache_last_time, new_cache_last_channel_len. Decoder/joint: encoder_outputs [1,512,1], targets [1,1], target_length, input_states_1/2 [1,1,640] -> logits (1027 wide: tokenizer ids 0..1025, blank 1026).
   - anchor `src/model_eou.rs:L28-L30 @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "cache_last_channel: Array4::zeros((17, 1, 70, 512)), cache_last_time: Array4::zeros((17, 1, 512, 8)), cache_last_channel_len: Array1::from_vec(vec![0i64]),"
4. EOU decoding: greedy argmax per encoder frame, up to 5 symbols/frame; stop on blank (id = vocab_size) or id 0; id of "<EOU>" from the tokenizer (fallback 1024); on EOU with reset_on_eou it zeroes only the decoder LSTM state and last_token=blank and KEEPS encoder cache and audio buffer (hard reset commented out as "DON'T reset"). An optional blank-logit penalty exists to recover borderline words.
   - anchor `src/parakeet_eou.rs:L259-L266 @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "// Soft reset: Only reset decoder states // at this state, we need to keep encoder cache and audio buffer flowing for continuous context"
5. No validation against NeMo and no latency figures for the EOU path were found: the only unit tests in parakeet_eou.rs cover the blank penalty, audio.rs has a sine-bin STFT test, and the author's own example comment says reset-on-EOU underperforms in real-world tests. Treat the Rust port as a reference to port plus diff against NeMo, not a validated oracle.
   - anchor `examples/streaming.rs header comment @ 6ad1501` @ altunenes/parakeet-rs@6ad1501898842fda76f86f7bcad6b361daf5df9e: "let reset_on_eou: bool = false; I must admit that this is not work very well on my real world tests :/"
6. It also carries Nemotron streaming (600M), multitalker, Sortformer diarisation, TDT and a Burn backend, which are out of scope for the turn-end arm.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. No NeMo parity/latency evidence exists; author flags reset_on_eou as weak. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: W4-PARAKEET-RS-02 rewritten: with reset off the EOU is not surfaced. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://huggingface.co/nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 - Already ingested and dived as intake-2017#record. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Use parakeet-rs as a readable reference for the Parakeet-realtime-EOU onnxruntime loop (mel constants, 25-frame slice, cache tensors and shapes, greedy EOU decode). Copy the structure, not the efficiency (whole-buffer STFT per chunk) and not the reset-on-EOU behavior the author says underperforms. No latency and no NeMo-parity numbers exist in the repo; we must produce both (feature dump diff vs NeMo preprocessor, CPU ms/chunk on EPYC).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2050#record — NeMo Voice Agent

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2035#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. It does not supersede examples/voice_agent with a new turn-taking model: the repo is the same NeMo Voice Agent moved ("graduates") from NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech into its own repo on 2026-08-06, with a Pipecat 1.6.0 pipeline, newer default LLM (Nemotron-3.5-Lightning-30B-A3B NVFP4) and an added evaluation harness.
   - anchor `README.md "Latest Updates" 2026-08-06 @ 2b08bdb` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "NeMo Voice Agent graduates from [NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech](https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech/tree/main/examples/voice_agent) into its own repo."
1. Turn-taking is Silero VAD + the ASR model's own <EOU>/<EOB> tokens from nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 (the only entry in ASR_EOU_MODELS), arbitrated by NeMoTurnTakingService; VAD stop (default 1.2 s) is the fallback and a "[EOU missing]" log line flags it. The alternative type speech_timeout is plain VAD silence + a 0.6 s timer (additive).
   - anchor `docs/about/core-concepts/speech-pipeline/turn-taking.md "How Turn Detection Works"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "Only ASR models listed in `ASR_EOU_MODELS` in `stt.py` produce these tokens. The shipped default `nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1` does."
2. Backchannel handling is a lookup, not a model: a 78-phrase English-only exact-match list (normalised, non-letters deleted) suppresses interruption only while the bot is speaking; any longer utterance interrupts after max_buffer_size=2 completed words (early barge-in), with a 0.5 s bot_stop_delay tail. This is directly relevant to CS-23 and trivially portable.
   - anchor `turn-taking.md "Backchannel Suppression"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "A phrase is suppressed **only while the bot is speaking**."
   - anchor `turn-taking.md "Backchannel Suppression"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "Only English is supported — `clean_text` raises `ValueError` for any other `Language`."
3. Evaluation tooling: a two-bot harness where a simulated-user voice agent talks to the agent under test over WebSocket/RTVI; any external agent implementing the Pipecat WS+RTVI contract can be evaluated. It measures task success on 328 scenarios and agent response latency (user-stop to agent-start, mean/P50/P95); it does not score endpointing recall/false-cut or barge-in.
   - anchor `docs/evaluate/index.md "What the Bridge Does"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "Every measurement pairs the moment the user stopped speaking with the moment the agent started speaking. The bridge reports mean, P50, P95, min, and max per scenario and across the run."
4. Hardware assumptions: Linux + NVIDIA GPU (CUDA 13 wheels), NVFP4-class LLM (~24 GB+), ASR/TTS/diarisation on CUDA, browser mic client. No ROCm path is documented (installation.md does list a cpu torch-backend wheel, so CPU is not wholly unsupported); the NeMo ASR service is not portable to our stack as-is.
   - anchor `docs/get-started/prerequisites.md "Hardware Requirements"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "An NVIDIA GPU and driver compatible with the CUDA wheel selected in `pyproject.toml`. The default is CUDA 13.0."
5. Portable pieces: the turn_taking.py policy (backchannel list and matching, max_buffer_size early interruption, bot_stop_delay, VAD defaults), the response-latency bridge methodology, and the scenario sets (EVA/tau2, MIT-sourced) for end-to-end tests; the EOU model itself is the same one parakeet-rs runs on onnxruntime.
   - anchor `turn-taking.md "Turn-Taking Settings"` @ NVIDIA-NeMo/labs-Voice-Agent@2b08bdbee70052a5bf36f898e8bf510db35c3e8e: "Number of completed words that can accumulate mid-utterance before the bot is interrupted and an interim transcript is pushed downstream."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected expectation: no new turn-taking model; successor = same pipeline relocated. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: CPU support overstated; installation.md lists a cpu torch backend. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech/tree/main/examples/voice_agent - Deprecated predecessor; already dived as intake-2035#record. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

labs-Voice-Agent is a relocation plus an eval harness, not a new turn-taking model; its turn-end signal is the same Parakeet-realtime-EOU tokens plus Silero. Take the backchannel/early-interrupt policy and the response-latency bridge idea for CS-23; do not expect published latency or endpointing accuracy numbers, and do not plan to run its NeMo/CUDA services.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2051#record — Unit-Based Agent for Semi-Cascaded Full-Duplex Dialogue Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20230
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, multimodal, inference_serving)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2032#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Architecture: one multimodal LLM (Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B, prompted, train-free) is the only decision-maker. It receives the raw audio plus a state-specific prompt and emits a single word, continue or switch. In the listen state, incomplete utterance = continue, complete = switch (go speak); in the speak state, backchannel = continue, genuine interruption = switch (stop TTS, return to listen). Silero VAD, optional CAM++ speaker verification, an asynchronous Paraformer ASR (transcripts cached as context for the NEXT decision) and IndexTTS 1.5 are the auxiliary modules; the Switch/Continue tokens come from a prompt, not from a trained head.
   - anchor `§2 Method (listen state)` @ arXiv:2601.20230v2 (html): "In the listen state, the system determines whether the user’s utterance is semantically complete, with incomplete utterances outputting a continue decision and complete ones outputting a switch decision that moves the system to the speak state, where the system generates the assistant’s spoken re..."
1. The paper's own numbers (Table 1): dev first-response delay 1.528 s vs baseline 2.753 s, dev interruption 89.7 vs 80.2, dev rejection 50.0 vs 45.6; test interruption 89.7, test rejection 57.8, total delay 1.632 s (baseline dev total delay 2.436 s). The 89.7 interruption and 57.8 rejection figures quoted from the HumDial dive are the paper's TEST row and match the organisers' Table 3; the dev interruption figure is also 89.7. The paper reports no per-scenario breakdown (backchannel, pause, third-party) and no ablation, so the reason rejection lags interruption is not explained by the paper.
   - anchor `§3 Table 1 (test row)` @ arXiv:2601.20230v2 (html): "ours(Test) - 89.7 57.8 1.632s"
2. Hardware/serving as described: two NVIDIA A100 GPUs, Qwen3-Omni and IndexTTS 1.5 both vLLM-based. The repo README launches Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B with --dtype bfloat16 --max-model-len 65536 -tp 4 (four-way tensor parallel), which is inconsistent with the paper's two A100s. The organisers evaluated on RTX A6000 Docker (overview §4B).
   - anchor `§3 Experiments` @ arXiv:2601.20230v2 (html): "Experiments are conducted on two NVIDIA A100 GPUs using the dataset provided by the challenge organizers"
3. Code is available (Apache-2.0, commit f4ff2b1 of 2026-04-08), but the repo HEAD no longer matches the paper: src/module.py calls DashScope cloud APIs (qwen3-asr-flash for ASR, qwen3-omni-flash-2025-12-01 for the omni model, qwen3-tts-flash for TTS), not the local Paraformer/Qwen3-Omni-vLLM/IndexTTS the paper describes. The prompts live in src/config.yaml and demo/config.yaml. The pipeline is a hand-written timer state machine: 0.64 s VAD end-hold before the judge call, plus a 2.5 s (src) / 1.5 s (demo) after-continue timeout; the shipped repo also has an extra SHIFT prompt (a topic-shift detector; shift_s repeats the prior answer on speaker mismatch) that the paper does not describe.
   - anchor `src/module.py:L72 and L126 @ f4ff2b1` @ yu-haoyuan/fd-badcat@f4ff2b1f4b24f83366bf93d3017a773709da4226: "使用 qwen3-asr-flash 进行语音识别"
4. Adoptable for CS-22/CS-23: (a) collapse turn-end and barge-in into one two-token decision space (continue/switch) keyed on the current state, instead of separate classifiers; (b) a 0.64 s post-VAD hold before invoking the judge; (c) the backchannel-vs-interruption prompt (continue for acknowledgements such as hao de, zhidao le, en en; switch for contradiction/question/stop) as a labelled test oracle; (d) async ASR used only as context for the next turn, off the critical path. NOT adoptable as-is: the decision model is a 30B-A3B omni model that must hear the audio, which is not in our cascade (whisper.cpp STT then text LLM); an audio-in decision would need a text-only analogue (judge on the partial transcript) and is then closer to EasyTurn-style text turn classification.
   - anchor `src/config.yaml judge prompt L24-L34 @ f4ff2b1` @ yu-haoyuan/fd-badcat@f4ff2b1f4b24f83366bf93d3017a773709da4226: "如果你认为用户已经说完，请只输出字符串'switch'。不要输出其他内容。"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: confirmed 89.7/57.8 as the paper's test row; found repo HEAD diverges from the paper (cloud DashScope ASR/omni/TTS, extra SHIFT prompt, 4-way TP in README vs two A100 in paper); no ablation or scenario split exists. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: SHIFT prompt is a topic-shift detector, not a repeat-request detector. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): arXiv:2604.21406 - Already dived as intake-2032#record. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Badcat is a 4-page challenge system description: a prompted Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B emits one word (continue/switch) per state, wrapped in a timer state machine. Take away the two-token state-conditioned decision design and the prompts as test oracles. Do NOT carry: the 89.7 interruption as evidence of good rejection (rejection is only 57.8, rank-2 overall, and the paper has no ablation or per-scenario split), nor the assumption that the released code reproduces the paper (HEAD calls DashScope cloud APIs). Not a recipe our text cascade can run without an audio-capable judge.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2052#record — The ICASSP 2026 HumDial Challenge: Benchmarking Human-like Spoken Dialogue Systems in the LLM Era

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05564
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, multimodal, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2032#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. HumDial (ICASSP 2026) is a two-track challenge: Track I Emotional Intelligence (3 tasks) and Track II Full-Duplex Interaction. It drew over 100 registered teams and 15 valid submissions. Data are scripted (Gemini 2.5-pro for Track I, DeepSeek for Track II) and performed by professional actors rather than synthetic overlap mixing. Track II test set is 5,000 utterances (Interruption 600 each x 5 scenarios, Rejection 600/600/600/200).
   - anchor `Abstract / §5` @ arXiv:2601.05564v2 (html): "The HumDial Challenge attracted over 100 registered teams, yielding 15 valid submissions."
1. Track II taxonomy. Interruption scenarios: follow-up questions (FQ), negation/dissatisfaction (ND), repetition requests (RR), topic switching (TS), silence/termination (ST). Rejection scenarios: user real-time backchannels (URB), pause handling (PH), third-party speech (TPS), speech directed at others (SDO). The overview gives only these names and a one-line gloss ('non-instructional speech'); it gives no formal per-class definitions, and the training Rejection split has 0 SDO utterances (1211/1211/1210/0).
   - anchor `§4 Track II` @ arXiv:2601.05564v2 (html): "2) Rejection, testing robustness against non-instructional speech such as user real-time backchannels (URB), pause handling (PH), third-party speech (TPS), and speech directed at others (SDO)."
2. Track II results (Table 3): Cookie_asr rank 1 (Int 79.3, Rej 72.2, delay 1.260 s, delay score 79.9, final 76.6); Badcat rank 2 (89.7 / 57.8 / 1.632 s / 72.6 / 73.5); SenseDialog rank 3 (76.4 / 60.9 / 1.237 s / 80.5 / 71.0); organiser Baseline rank 6 at 56.4 (75.9 / 35.2 / 2.531 s); organiser Cascade rank 9 at 37.7 (28.1 / 30.9 / 1.739 s). Final = 0.4 Int + 0.4 Rej + 0.2 delay score (delay score relative to a 60-point baseline). Rej is below Int in every listed row except the organiser Cascade (28.1 vs 30.9), and the paper calls rejection the primary hurdle.
   - anchor `Table 3 (Badcat row)` @ arXiv:2601.05564v2 (html): "Badcat 89.7 57.8 1.632 72.6 73.5 2"
3. Track I (Table 2): TeleAI rank 1 (final 4.27), NJU-TencentHY 2 (4.24), BJTU_Unisound 3 (4.21), SenseDialog 4 (4.06); Task 3 empathy generation is the weak point while Tasks 1-2 are near ceiling (4.97-5.00). Automated judge is Qwen3-Omni-30B plus 20 human raters for Task 3.
4. The overview contains NO team system descriptions and does not say which entries are open or cascaded; it announces a follow-up publication comparing commercial/open models. System architecture for Badcat comes from its own paper (2601.20230); a search snippet from the later study (2604.21406) labels Cookie_asr as cascaded, which was not verified from the primary source in this dive. Evaluation ran in standardized Docker on NVIDIA RTX A6000 GPUs, so 'delay' numbers are GPU-served.
   - anchor `§4B` @ arXiv:2601.05564v2 (html): "all systems are evaluated within standardized Docker environments powered by NVIDIA RTX A6000 GPUs"
   - anchor `§5` @ arXiv:2601.05564v2 (html): "We plan to utilize HumDial datasets to benchmark leading commercial and open-source models, with a comprehensive comparative analysis against these submissions presented in a follow-up publication."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: the hoped-for team descriptions, open/cascade labels and formal rejection definitions are NOT in the overview (names only); per-track numbers confirmed. Table 3 also gives delay scores and ranks of 9 teams, and shows every team has Rej below Int. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {3: 4}). Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: Cascade row is the exception to Rej<Int.

### Verdict

This is a 4-page challenge summary: it settles the rank table and the scenario NAMES, nothing about how winners are built. Do NOT cite it for which systems are open or cascaded, nor for formal rejection-class definitions (it gives none; SDO has 0 training utterances). The organiser 'Cascade' baseline scored 37.7 with Int 28.1, but its composition is not described here, so it must not be read as evidence about a whisper+LLM+TTS cascade like ours. Delay figures are GPU (RTX A6000) numbers and judge/score pipeline details sit in the follow-up study.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2053#record — potion-code-16M-v2 Model Card

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: search_retrieval, rag_alternatives, training_distillation, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2040#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. potion-code-16M-v2 is a Model2Vec static embedding model (no transformer forward pass): ~16.2M F16 parameters (safetensors total 16,244,992; file 32.49 MB), 256-dimensional, ~63.5k vocabulary, normalised outputs, float16 storage. Pipeline per the card: mine 43k code-specific tokens from CornStack and add them to the CodeRankEmbed tokenizer (~63.5k total), distil from the teacher with Model2Vec (256-d via PCA), Tokenlearn on 1.2M CornStack (query, document) pairs, then contrastive fine-tuning (MultipleNegativesRankingLoss) on 1.2M pairs.
   - anchor `Model Details table / How it works` @ HF minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2@e9d2a44ca6a05ac6685f3b23709ea57eb7352d5b: "code-specific tokens are mined from CornStack and added to the base CodeRankEmbed tokenizer (43k extra tokens → ~63.5k total)"
1. Teacher is nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed (137M params); training data is CornStack (six languages: Python, Java, JavaScript, Go, PHP, Ruby) plus the minishlab tokenlearn-cornstack query/doc datasets. It is the model inside Semble (MinishLab code-search library for agents) and the successor to potion-code-16M.
   - anchor `Model Details table` @ HF minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2@e9d2a44ca6a05ac6685f3b23709ea57eb7352d5b: "| Teacher model | nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed |"
2. Standalone CoIR NDCG@10 (card table, mteb>=2.10): potion-code-16M-v2 AVG 39.08 vs CodeRankEmbed 59.14, BM25 42.31, potion-code-16M 37.05, potion-retrieval-32M 32.10, potion-base-32M 31.42; v2 + BM25 RRF(k=60) hybrid 43.36. Per task v2: CosQA 24.36 (teacher 35.92), CodeFeedback-ST 53.22 (78.11), CodeFeedback-MT 38.02 (42.61), StackOverflow 59.57 (80.53), COIRCodeSearchNet 46.37 (94.70), AppsRetrieval 5.19 (23.46). So the static model recovers about 66% of the teacher's CoIR average and is BELOW plain BM25 on average (it trails the teacher on every listed task). Numbers are self-reported by the model authors (no independent replication found).
   - anchor `Results table (AVG row)` @ HF minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2@e9d2a44ca6a05ac6685f3b23709ea57eb7352d5b: "| **potion-code-16M-v2** | **16M** | **39.08** |"
3. The model card makes NO speed claim and names no hardware: it says only that static embeddings are computed 'orders of magnitude faster' than transformers on GPU and CPU. The speed figures come from the Semble benchmarks README: semble (potion-code-16M-v2 + BM25 + ranking) index 240 ms and query p50 0.69 ms vs CodeRankEmbed 116 s and 16 ms ('CPU only', one repo per language, cold index, p50 of 5 runs) and the 'about 480x indexing, 23x query' claim. These are whole-pipeline numbers including tree-sitter chunking and BM25, they name no CPU model, core count or thread settings, and the benchmark compares against CodeRankEmbed (137M), not against BGE-large or EmbeddingGemma 2.
   - anchor `Overview` @ HF minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2@e9d2a44ca6a05ac6685f3b23709ea57eb7352d5b: "It uses static embeddings, allowing text and code embeddings to be computed orders of magnitude faster than transformer-based models on both GPU and CPU."
   - anchor `benchmarks/README.md Speed (semble@main, sha256 c3cfcdad...)` @ MinishLab/semble benchmarks/README.md (main, retrieved 2026-10-20): "All tools run CPU-only. semble uses `minishlab/potion-code-16M-v2`; CodeRankEmbed uses `nomic-ai/CodeRankEmbed` (137M params)."
4. Semble's end-to-end retrieval quality (NDCG@10 0.855 hybrid with ranking, on 63 repos) is far higher than the standalone CoIR average: its own ablation lists potion-code-16M-v2 raw 0.652 rising to 0.824 with the ranking layer. The ranker (identifier-stem boost, noise penalties, RRF with BM25) does much of the work, so potion's standalone embedding quality should not be inferred from the Semble headline. For REPL-EMB-2.2, CPU encoder speed on EPYC is therefore unmeasured by the source; it must be measured on our 9655 (one thread bind, no GPU) with the arm as a plain lookup-and-mean-pool, whose expected cost is tiny but unproven here.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: card contains no speed claim or hardware; speed numbers live only in the Semble repo; standalone CoIR average (39.08) is below BM25, so Semble's 0.855 is mostly its ranking layer plus hybrid, not the embedding alone. Params 16.24M F16, file 32.49 MB. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: claim_anchors re-indexed per second reader (positions->claims {4: 3}). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/MinishLab/model2vec - Already ingested and dived as intake-2019#record. [wave4 declined_surfaced]; https://github.com/CoIR-team/coir - Benchmark harness; potion's CoIR numbers already read from its card. [wave4 declined_surfaced].

### Verdict

Take away: a 32 MB, 256-d static code embedder that is cheap to run on any CPU and a viable cheap arm for REPL-EMB-2.2 retrieval A/B, but its standalone quality is weak (CoIR 39.08, below BM25 42.31; CodeRankEmbed 59.14) and it was trained on code only (six languages), so do not assume it transfers to our REPL corpora (handoffs/prose). Do NOT carry any CPU speed number as established for EPYC: the card has none, and the Semble figures are whole-pipeline, hardware-unnamed, and versus CodeRankEmbed only. Measure it ourselves.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md`

## intake-2054#record — Unmute

- **URL**: https://github.com/kyutai-labs/unmute
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2048#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Unmute is a three-service cascade: a browser/frontend websocket to a Python backend that streams user audio to a Kyutai STT server over websocket, calls any OpenAI-compatible LLM server with streamed tokens (rechunked to whole words), and streams the words into a Kyutai TTS server whose audio is forwarded back; the protocol to the browser is an OpenAI-Realtime-API-style event stream.
   - anchor `README.md L27 @ e348e56` @ kyutai-labs/unmute@e348e567c7020d40020412024f7aa03c38a3bc3e: "Once the speech-to-text detects that the user has stopped speaking and it's time to generate a response, the backend connects to an **LLM** server to retrieve the response."
1. The 'semantic VAD' is not a separate model: the Kyutai STT 1B (en/fr, ~0.5 s delay, 16-layer 2048-d transformer plus 4 extra heads in the moshi-server config) emits a per-frame (80 ms) 'Step' message whose prs[2] is a pause probability; the backend smooths it with a 10 ms-half-life EMA (essentially unsmoothed at 80 ms frames), declares end-of-turn when it exceeds 0.6 while the user is speaking, and the head's horizon/training are not documented in this repo (the DSM paper 2509.08753 text does not describe it either).
   - anchor `unmute/unmute_handler.py L372-390 @ e348e56` @ kyutai-labs/unmute@e348e567c7020d40020412024f7aa03c38a3bc3e: "if stt.pause_prediction.value > 0.6:"
2. Barge-in has two triggers: (a) any non-empty STT word arriving while the bot is speaking, or (b) the same pause score dropping below 0.4 (i.e. user speech detected) after the first 3 s of audio; interrupt_bot() appends an interruption marker to the assistant message, clears the audio queue and swaps the output queue, and removes the TTS and LLM tasks.
   - anchor `unmute/unmute_handler.py L351-358 @ e348e56` @ kyutai-labs/unmute@e348e567c7020d40020412024f7aa03c38a3bc3e: "elif ( self.chatbot.conversation_state() == "bot_speaking" and stt.pause_prediction.value < 0.4 and self.audio_received_sec() > UNINTERRUPTIBLE_BY_VAD_TIME_SEC ):"
3. Hardware assumptions are CUDA-only: README requires an NVIDIA CUDA GPU with >=16 GB VRAM, x86_64, Docker NVIDIA toolkit (or CUDA 12.1 for dockerless Rust STT/TTS); STT 2.5 GB, TTS 5.3 GB, LLM 6.1 GB VRAM; the vendor's production setup puts STT, TTS and the vLLM server on separate GPUs.
   - anchor `README.md L36 @ e348e56` @ kyutai-labs/unmute@e348e567c7020d40020412024f7aa03c38a3bc3e: "Hardware: a GPU with CUDA support and at least 16 GB VRAM."
4. Portable to our whisper.cpp -> LLM -> qwentts.cpp cascade are the control-plane patterns (end/interrupt hysteresis 0.6/0.4, 3 s post-start barge-in guard, word-triggered interrupt, cancel TTS+LLM then swap the queue, word-level rechunking); the semantic pause head itself and the flush trick are NOT portable because they exist only inside Kyutai's Rust STT server (whisper.cpp is a non-streaming encoder-decoder with no pause head).
   - anchor `kyutai.org/stt "Low latency" (flush trick); sha256 f20b96145b4557514fb54300e0efda4d260dde4216bf9403eff3bf8653e9e596` @ https://kyutai.org/stt retrieved 2026-10-20 (html sha256 f20b9614...): "The semantic VAD is available in the Rust server but not yet in the other implementations ."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Semantic VAD is an STT-model output head (prs[2]) thresholded at 0.6/0.4 in the backend, not a standalone VAD; TurnBench's 1007 ms median EOT latency for this head qualifies the 'low latency' framing. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: W5-UNMUTE-01 guard semantics and threshold calibration caveat corrected. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13450 - Already dived as intake-2048#record. [wave6 declined].

### Verdict

Unmute is a reference CASCADE control plane, useful as a source of concrete constants and the cancel chain for CS-22/CS-23. Its end-of-turn quality comes entirely from a Kyutai STT model head (CUDA, Rust moshi-server) that we cannot reuse with whisper.cpp; third-party TurnBench puts that head at EOT .773/.059/1007 ms vs VAP .845/.055/368. Do NOT carry the vendor 'semantic VAD' framing or the 125 ms flush figure as measured results for our stack; do NOT claim the pause head's horizon (undocumented in read sources).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2055#record — Real-time and Continuous Turn-taking Prediction Using Voice Activity Projection

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04868
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2048#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. VAP maps stereo (one channel per speaker) audio at 50 frames/s to a distribution over 256 joint future voice-activity states in a 2 s window (4 bins per speaker); a pretrained CPC encoder feeds per-channel self-attention and a cross-channel transformer, and p_now / p_future are derived by summing bin probabilities and a softmax across speakers.
   - anchor `§2 Voice activity projection` @ arXiv:2401.04868v1 (html): "The VAP model predicts voice activities for two speakers within a two-second time window by predicting the joint activity of both speakers over four binary bins."
1. CPU real-time claim: on an Intel Xeon Gold 6128 (3.40 GHz) the model needs 14.61 ms per 20 ms frame (RTF 0.73) when the transformer sees a 1 s context, versus 273.84 ms (RTF 13.69) with 20 s context; so real time holds only with truncation to roughly <=1 s, and the margin is thin (0.73) on that CPU. No thread count, batch, or variance is given.
   - anchor `§3 Performance` @ arXiv:2401.04868v1 (html): "The inference was performed on an Intel Xeon Gold 6128 CPU with a clock speed of 3.40 GHz."
2. Accuracy is reported only as balanced accuracy of hold-vs-shift prediction during mutual silences >0.25 s on a Japanese travel-agency dialogue test set: about 75-76% for 0.5-10 s contexts, 62.8% at 0.1 s; English (Switchboard) and Mandarin (HKUST) models are said to give 'similar results' with no numbers in this paper.
   - anchor `§3 Performance / Table 1` @ arXiv:2401.04868v1 (html): "The evaluation metric used is balanced accuracy which calculates the accuracies for both positive and negative examples and then takes the average of them."
3. Code is public (ErikEkstedt/VoiceActivityProjection, MIT) with a pretrained state-dict in example/ and a run.py inference script; training uses a private vap_dataset repo that the README says is 'not strictly required' (a user-written DataModule can replace it).
   - anchor `§2 footnote 1` @ arXiv:2401.04868v1 (html): "The source codes for the VAP model are publicly available 1 1"
4. TurnBench's top-recall VAP row (EOT .845 recall / .055 FPR / 368 ms) is NOT this paper's demonstrated real-time configuration: TurnBench's baseline defaults to a fine-tuned 'oto' checkpoint (viks66/VAP_checkpoints; what 'oto' denotes is not stated), thresholds swept on TurnBench dev (theta_eot 0.9161, theta_int 0.8591), and runs overlapping 25 s windows with 20 s context, the setting this paper measures at RTF 13.69 on CPU; the 368 ms is a signed t_pred - t_gold decision latency, not compute time; TurnBench does not state which checkpoint produced its table row.
   - anchor `baselines/vap/README.md L22 + L28-30 @ 38a6f87 (sesameailabs/turnbench)` @ SesameAILabs/turnbench@38a6f874322430cb3ca71d8a52aa1e636e88bad8: "bash baselines/vap/run.sh # default: dev + test (oto checkpoint)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Corrected the implied link to TurnBench: its VAP baseline is a fine-tuned 'oto' checkpoint with dev-swept thresholds and 20 s context windows, not the 1 s real-time configuration demonstrated here. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: removed unsourced '16 kHz' and 'oto=otoSpeech'; training-from-repo statement corrected; latency definition made exact.

### Verdict

VAP is a 2-channel continuous turn-taking predictor with a public MIT repo and a small transformer head over CPC features; the paper's CPU real-time evidence is one old Xeon, one language (Japanese), balanced accuracy 75-76%, RTF 0.73 only with <=1 s attention context. Do NOT cite TurnBench's .845/.055/368 ms as what the public VAP checkpoint does: that row used a TurnBench/otoSpeech fine-tuned checkpoint (per the TurnBench repo README default; the paper text does not name the checkpoint) with thresholds tuned on its dev split. For us the structural fit is strong: a cascade has two clean channels (user mic and our own TTS output).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2056#record — ICASSP 2026: Human-like Spoken Dialogue Systems Challenge

- **URL**: https://aslp-lab.github.io/HumDial-Challenge/
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2052#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Track 2 (Full-Duplex Interaction) defines nine sub-tasks in two scenarios. Interruption (the system must stop and answer): Follow-up Questions, Negation/Dissatisfaction, Repetition Requests, Topic Switching, Silence/Termination. Rejection (the system must NOT stop/answer): User Real-time Backchannels, Pause Handling, Third-party Speech, Speech Directed at Others. The abbreviations URB, PH, TPS, SDO, FQ, ND, RR, TS, ST do not appear on the site or in the Hum-Dial repo; mapping them to these names is by initials only and comes from the overview paper, not from this source.
   - anchor `track2/description "Challenge Tasks" (xtrack2_description.html sha256 12f2dacf...)` @ aslp-lab.github.io/HumDial-Challenge retrieved 2026-10-20; github.com/ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "Pause Handling : The user may pause mid-sentence due to thinking or hesitation, leading to incomplete semantics. The model should wait until the user’s intent is fully expressed before responding."
1. Scoring follows Full-Duplex-Bench v1.5: RESPOND rate plus stop latency and response latency for interruptions; RESUME/rejection rate plus early-interrupt rate for rejections; first-response delay for all nine; Total = 0.4 Interruption + 0.4 Rejection + 0.2 Delay-score, where Delay-score = 100 - 40*log(L/Lmin)/log(Lbase/Lmin) and the baseline is pinned at 60; latency was measured by the organizers on RTX A6000 machines.
   - anchor `track2/results "2. Final Total Score Calculation Formula" (xtrack2_results.html sha256 fae9517c...)` @ aslp-lab.github.io/HumDial-Challenge retrieved 2026-10-20; github.com/ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "\text{Total_Score} = \text{Interruption_Total_Score} \times 0.4 + \text{Rejection_Total_Score} \times 0.4 + \text{Total_Delay_Score} \times 0.2"
2. Results (Dec 2025): eight teams plus an organizer Baseline row (56.4, built on Easy Turn + OSUM-EChat, needs at least two RTX 4090-class GPUs) and an unexplained 'Cascade' row (37.7: interruption 28.1, rejection 30.9, delay 1.739 s) that scored lowest; rejection scores were low for nearly everyone (best 72.2, most below 62), so rejection (backchannel/pause/third-party/other-directed) is the hard half. The site publishes no team system descriptions, so which top systems are cascades or open cannot be settled from it.
   - anchor `track2/results "3. Results"` @ aslp-lab.github.io/HumDial-Challenge retrieved 2026-10-20; github.com/ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "A total of eight teams submitted their results and system descriptions for this challenge."
3. Data access is restricted: train (>107 h real recordings, CN+EN, >100 speakers), dev (9 sub-tasks x 200 samples) and test (5,000 test + 4,100 clean wavs) train/dev were distributed by email to registered institutional addresses (home page rule), while the Track 2 test-set page says only 'officially released'; the only public dataset is HumDial-EIBench on HF, which belongs to Track 1 (emotion), not Track 2. Scoring scripts and the baseline are public in ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial.
   - anchor `track2/description "Dataset"; /test_set` @ aslp-lab.github.io/HumDial-Challenge retrieved 2026-10-20; github.com/ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "The data will be sent via the registered email."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: new entry. Site is readable; abbreviations and team descriptions are not on it (they must come from the overview paper intake-2052#record or team papers). Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: gating applies to train/dev; test set described as released. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench - Already dived as intake-2034#record. [wave6 declined]; HumDial Track 2 train/dev data - Gated to institutional e-mail; a data request, not a source. [wave6 declined].

### Verdict

The site is a static, fully readable Hugo page (not a JS shell). It gives the task taxonomy, scoring formula and results table, but NOT team system descriptions, NOT the URB/PH/TPS/SDO/FQ/ND/RR/TS/ST abbreviations, and NOT public Track 2 data. Take: the nine-class taxonomy as a labelling scheme for our CS-4/CS-22 clips (especially the four rejection classes) and the scoring structure. Do NOT carry a claim about which top teams are cascades or open (not on this source), and do NOT treat the unexplained 'Cascade' row as evidence about cascades in general.

## intake-2057#record — delayed-streams-modeling (Kyutai STT / TTS inference code)

- **URL**: https://github.com/kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2054#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Semantic VAD = 4 extra pause-prediction heads on kyutai/stt-1b-en_fr (not on the 2.6B English model). The STT emits a per-step "Step" message whose prs field holds P(no voice activity) at horizons 0.5, 1, 2, 3 s (indices 0-3).
   - anchor `stt-rs/src/main.rs:L193-L195 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "In kyutai/stt-1b-en_fr-candle, these horizons are 0.5s, 1s, 2s, and 3s."
   - anchor `configs/config-stt-en_fr-hf.toml:L44-L46 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "[modules.asr.model.extra_heads] num_heads = 4 dim = 6"
1. Index 2 (2.0 s horizon) is the reference end-of-turn signal in every shipped consumer: Rust standalone (prs[2][0] > 0.5), websocket demo script (index 2, > 0.5), PyTorch script (vad_heads[2], > 0.5); Unmute smooths prs[2] and uses 0.6 / 0.4 hysteresis. Index 2 is a default chosen by Kyutai, not a documented optimum; no per-head accuracy is published in the repo.
   - anchor `scripts/stt_from_mic_rust_server.py:L22-L24 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "Lower indices predict pauses more aggressively. In Unmute, we use 2.0 seconds = index 2."
2. The PyTorch implementation exposes the heads via step_with_extra_heads (--vad), so the semantic VAD is not Rust-only (the README lists --vad only under the Rust standalone; a kyutai.org statement was not saved to disk). The Rust standalone has an explicit --cpu flag; there is no ROCm/HIP backend in the repo (CUDA, cuDNN, Metal features; MLX for Apple).
   - anchor `scripts/stt_from_file_pytorch.py:L185 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "text_tokens, vad_heads = lm_gen.step_with_extra_heads(audio_tokens)"
   - anchor `stt-rs/src/main.rs:L22 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "/// Run the model on cpu."
3. Training of the heads is undocumented in this repo; the model card gives only the data mix (Fisher 2000 h + proprietary). No training code for STT is released.
   - anchor `HF kyutai/stt-1b-en_fr README.md "Training Data" L74 (sha256 095cb8ad...)` @ HF kyutai/stt-1b-en_fr@1c34c6b4f7e9299bb61985f145052ff131005dde: "We finetune on the Fisher dataset of 2000 hours of English audio, plus proprietary data (1000 hours in English, 600 hours in French)."
   - anchor `FAQ.md:L50-L54 @ 4c4f65e` @ kyutai-labs/delayed-streams-modeling@4c4f65e147df056adf3346290d64c7b9649b18c9: "Some finetuning code can be found in the [kyutai-labs/moshi-finetune repo]"
4. As a CS-22 arm on EPYC CPU the STT would be a second ~1B-parameter streaming model (Mimi + 16-layer 2048-d LM) running alongside or instead of whisper.cpp; CPU speed is not stated anywhere in the repo (README only quotes H100/L40S batch throughput), so feasibility is unmeasured, not shown.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: README/kyutai.org "Rust only" is wrong for the pause heads (PyTorch script uses step_with_extra_heads); Rust standalone runs on CPU via --cpu; horizons 0.5/1/2/3 s confirmed from main.rs comment; head training undocumented. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: README does not literally say Rust-only; kyutai.org statement not on disk. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi (rust/moshi-server and moshi crate) - Declined at the operator's 6-wave cap (steering seq 9). Dive note: wave cap reached (final wave); head training recipe is a nice-to-have, the arm is measured black-box anyway [wave6 surfaced (cap)].

### Verdict

Head index 2 = 2.0 s no-voice-activity horizon is the end-of-turn signal; the signal is available in PyTorch and Rust-candle (CPU), not CUDA-only. Do NOT carry "Rust-only" from the README, and do NOT carry any claim about how the heads are trained or what accuracy each head has: neither is documented here. CPU real-time capability on EPYC is unmeasured.

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`

## intake-2058#record — Voice Activity Projection: Self-supervised Learning of Turn-taking Events

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.09812
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2055#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. VAP objective: predict the joint future voice activity of both speakers over a 2 s window discretised into 4 bins per speaker of 200/400/600/800 ms (8 bits = 256 states), modelled as one categorical distribution so bin dependencies are kept (vs. 60 or 40 independent 50 ms bins in earlier work).
   - anchor `§2 Voice Activity Projection` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "Specifically, we use bin durations of 200, 400, 600 and 800ms"
   - anchor `§2` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "we limit the number of bins to 4 for each speaker, resulting in 8 total bits, or 256 different possible states"
1. Training data: Switchboard only (English telephone, 2205 train / 135 test dialogs, mixed to mono, 10 s chunks with 2 s overlap). Fisher and Candor appear only in the later stereo repo README, not in this paper.
   - anchor `§4 Model & Training` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "We train on the Switchboard dataset"
2. Event metrics (weighted F1, Switchboard test, Discrete model): shift/hold .899 (shift-class F1 .510 vs majority-class .843), short/long .786, shift-prediction .733, backchannel-prediction .723. Discrete is significantly better than Independent only on the two prediction tasks (p<0.025); on S/H and S/L the gain over Independent is +0.002/0.000 and not significant. These numbers are NOT end-of-turn latency/false-cut numbers.
   - anchor `§5 Table 1` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "Discrete (ours) .899 (.510) .786 .733 * .723 *"
   - anchor `§3` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "we take a zero-shot approach to these tasks"
3. Model: CPC-feature encoder + 4-layer, 256-d, 4-head causal transformer; no parameter count given. The 2022 model consumes 16 kHz mono plus VA features (not stereo audio alone), so a cascade must supply VAD frames from both channels.
   - anchor `§4 Model & Training` @ arXiv:2205.09812v1 (html, sha256 d5ae076d...): "using a hidden size of 256, 4 layers, 4 heads and 0.1 dropout"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: Fisher is not training data of this paper (Switchboard only); no parameter count in the paper; Discrete advantage is significant only on prediction tasks. 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): arXiv:2403.06487 Multilingual Turn-taking Prediction Using Voice Activity Projection - Declined at the operator's 6-wave cap (steering seq 9). Dive note: wave cap reached; English-only use is acceptable for a first CS-22 arm [wave6 surfaced (cap)]; arXiv:2209.05161 How Much Does Prosody Help Turn-taking? (cited in VAP project README) - Declined at the operator's 6-wave cap (steering seq 9). Dive note: wave cap reached; background only [wave6 surfaced (cap)].

### Verdict

Use this paper for the objective definition and event-level F1 on Switchboard only. Do NOT quote the F1 values as EOT-latency or false-cut performance, do not assume Fisher/Candor training, and do not assume a parameter count (not stated). Deployed checkpoints (public example, oto fine-tunes) differ from this 2022 model.

### Claims (as verified)

0. viks66/VAP_checkpoints holds three fine-tuned VAP state files (oto, swbd, swbd_oto), each 54.76 MB, with no model card (README is a licence stub, cc-by-4.0). It is a delivery bucket for TurnBench, not a documented release.
   - anchor `api/models/viks66/VAP_checkpoints?blobs=true siblings[oto.ckpt,swbd.ckpt,swbd_oto.ckpt].size` @ HF viks66/VAP_checkpoints@b9aa0ba1718221153e04ef343c7f3b8cf84bfbc3: "oto.ckpt 54761165 / swbd.ckpt 54761165 / swbd_oto.ckpt 54761165 (bytes)"
1. TurnBench's default VAP baseline loads the "oto" checkpoint (dev+test, thresholds swept at FPR<=0.1: eot 0.9161, int 0.8591) ; the leaderboard row (0.845 EOT recall / 368 ms) does not name its checkpoint, so attributing it to the fine-tuned oto checkpoint rather than the public pretrained example is inference from the default.

2. "oto" most plausibly denotes fine-tuning on the otoSpeech 104 h English full-duplex turn-taking set (TurnBench training split); this naming link is inferred from the TurnBench paper and the dataset card, not stated by the checkpoint repo. Training recipe, epochs, learning rate: undocumented (the VAP training repo is not public per the VAP README).

These are English fine-tunes whose provenance is only inferable via TurnBench. Treat the TurnBench VAP row as "oto-fine-tuned", not as the expected performance of the public pretrained checkpoint; do not assume the fine-tune recipe, or that swbd/swbd_oto mean what their names suggest.

## intake-2060#record — Hum-Dial (HumDial Challenge: Emotional Intelligence and Full-Duplex Interaction)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2056#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Track 2 (full-duplex) scoring is two-stage: (a) ASR with NeMo parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2 produces word-level timestamps for the user and model channels (clean and noisy pairs); (b) interruption RESPOND and rejection RESUME labels are produced by an LLM judge (DeepSeek deepseek-chat via the cloud API, prompt in evaluation/instruction/behavior.txt) while rejection rates and first-response delay come from deterministic timestamp rules (first model word after the user's segment end).
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/interruption/eval.py:L11 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "DEEPSEEK_BASE_URL = "https://api.deepseek.com""
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/interruption/eval.py:L12 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "MODEL_NAME = "deepseek-chat""
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/get_transcript/infer_en.py:L38 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "model_name="nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2""
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/rejection/Pause_Handling/compute_rejection_rate.py:L38 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "if diff is not None and diff > 0:"
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/interruption/compute_first_response_delay.py:L19 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "diff = first_chunk_start - first_origin_xmax"
   - anchor `Full-Duplex_Interaction/evaluation/instruction/behavior.txt:L61 @ dbbd8ad` @ ASLP-lab/Hum-Dial@dbbd8adf86bbb459aad6d07f6c8cc1572e267803: "C_RESPOND The model meaningfully addresses the overlap’s content"
1. Scenario scores: interruption = mean RESPOND over 5 sub-scenarios plus stop latency and response latency; rejection = RESUME (Third-party-after, Speech-directed-at-others, Backchannels) or timing rejection rate (Pause handling, Third-party-before), mixed 50/50 for Third-party. Final = 0.4 RESPOND + 0.4 RESUME + 0.2 latency score, latency score log-scaled against the best team and the baseline (field-relative).
2. Offline scoring of our cascade is feasible for the rule-based half (needs per-turn word timestamps and the dataset's *_sentence.json segment files) and for the ASR front end (NeMo can run on CPU, though Parakeet is not our stack); the LLM-judge half has a cloud default but a swappable base_url constant, and swapping the judge makes our numbers non-comparable to leaderboard numbers.
3. Train and dev data are not in the repo (dev data / test set are distributed outside it; site says data sent via the registered email), so the scripts cannot be run end-to-end from the repo alone.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2: judge identified (deepseek-chat via cloud API, swappable constant), ASR front end identified (parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2), timing metrics verified as pure rules; repo does not carry the data. Main correction 2026-10-20 after per-claim second reader: behavior.txt anchor re-indexed to claim 0; claims 1-3 unanchored (scoring weights are only in total_score.png). 2026-10-20 Stage-2 close-out, dive-surfaced sources declined (named so they are not lost or re-derived): https://github.com/DanielLin94144/Full-Duplex-Bench - Declined at the operator's 6-wave cap (steering seq 9). Dive note: wave cap reached; HumDial scripts already carry the needed pieces [wave6 surfaced (cap)].

### Verdict

The timing metrics are rule-based and reusable offline; the content labels depend on an LLM judge that defaults to the DeepSeek cloud API. Do NOT report a cascade score as a HumDial score unless the same judge model and the same data are used. Scripts alone cannot reproduce the challenge: the data is distributed separately.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
